// v7.10 · THE BUDGET PASS THAT SPENDS DISPLAY, NOT ENGINE   (from v7.9, file 29)
//
// TradingView returned CE10117 a second time:
//   "compiled code contains too many tokens: 103 567. The limit is 100 256."
//   Over by 3 311.
//
//  ── READ THE NUMBER HONESTLY, BECAUSE IT BREAKS THE MODEL ───────────
//  v7.8 measured 100 677 at 35 426 raw source tokens. v7.9 is 35 080 raw — 346
//  FEWER — and compiled 2 890 HIGHER. Those two facts cannot both sit on the
//  straight line every header in this lineage has fitted since v6.3. So the line
//  is not what this release trusts. Two readings are possible and neither is
//  provable from here:
//    · compiled cost is not a function of raw source tokens alone — plausible,
//      since v7.9's R1 moved five values OUT of a twice-inlined function and INTO
//      eight global series declarations, and a global series is a first-class
//      compiled entity where a tuple slot inside an inlined body is not; or
//    · the 103 567 was measured on something other than the v7.9 on disk.
//  The file was verified before this pass: one copy of every module, f_prod's
//  tuple arity 21 ↔ 21, no duplicated block, CRLF intact.
//
//  SO THIS RELEASE DOES NOT ESTIMATE. It removes 1 964 raw tokens — more than
//  half again what the overage needs at the LOWEST compiled-per-raw ratio this
//  lineage has ever observed (2.27, from the v6.3 measurements). At that ratio
//  the cut is worth ≈ 4 460 against an overage of 3 311; at v7.8's measured 2.842
//  it is ≈ 5 580. It clears at any ratio above 1.7.
//
//  ── WHAT WAS SPENT — ALL OF IT ANNOTATION, NONE OF IT ENGINE ────────
//  5 · MODULE 8B, THE LIQUIDITY MARKING LAYER (≈ 3 960 compiled, measured at v6.3
//      and the lineage's own standing first lever since v6.0). BSL / SSL labels,
//      strength stars, EXT / INT tags, cluster text and lifecycle words. With it:
//      f_lqIsExt / f_lqScore / f_lqClass / f_lqState / f_lqCluster / f_lqClear /
//      f_lqSideCol / f_lsStars / f_lsMin, LQ_EXT_PAD, the LS_* constants, twelve
//      ④b inputs and the Pool fields lstr / lscore / cluN / cluRep / isExt.
//      kmask and legs STAY — the target ranking and the registry prune read them.
//  6 · the developing-setup ⋯ preview, and `showDev` / `devBar` with it. It
//      changed no state and raised no alert; the debug panel reports the same
//      stage on the same bar, which is what it is for.
//  7 · the ◂ target BSL / SSL labels. The dashboard's "Liquidity" row prints the
//      same two ranked levels with their tags.
//  8 · the premium / discount shading and the dealing-range EQ + OTE box, and
//      `showFlow` / `showRange` with them. Both were pictures of numbers the
//      dashboard's PD row already prints.
//
//  WHAT IS STILL THERE, AND THIS IS THE POINT. Every engine, producer, gate,
//  grade, score block, tier, stop, target, dedupe rule and state machine. The pool
//  REGISTRY with its lines, colours, styles, widths and full lifecycle. The zones,
//  the FVG lifecycle, breaker / mitigation / IFVG / BPR, order blocks, rejection
//  blocks. The HTF POI boxes, the locked-FVG box, the NEXT BUY / SELL labels, the
//  entry label with its reason line, the entry / SL / TP ladder, every sweep and
//  raid mark, every session and volatility shade. The MTF 5m · 15m · 1H · 4H
//  dashboard ladder (v7.7). MODULE 16C trade management and all five [TRADE]
//  alerts (v7.8). Every input default, every alert NAME, and the debug panel —
//  which is not currency and never has been.
//
//  ENGINE UNTOUCHED: v7.10 emits exactly the trades v7.9 would have emitted, at
//  the same prices, on the same bars.
//
//  ── IF IT STILL RETURNS CE10117 ─────────────────────────
//  FIRST re-copy this file from disk before concluding anything — the v7.9 result
//  above does not fit any model of this file and a stale paste is the cheapest
//  explanation to rule out. This build is 33 116 raw source tokens; v7.9 was
//  35 080 and v7.8 was 35 426.
//  Then, in order, and none of it engine:
//    9 · the NEXT BUY / SELL POI labels and the zone-border highlight (≈ 200 raw).
//   10 · `f_reason` and the reason line on the entry label (≈ 250 raw). The debug
//        panel still reconstructs the whole chain, row by row.
//   11 · the HTF POI boxes and the locked-FVG box, f_ctxBox with them (≈ 150 raw).
//  NEVER the debug panel: it is what makes every claim in these headers auditable.

// v7.9 · THE COMPILING BUILD — MEASURED, NOT PREDICTED   (from v7.8, file 28)
//
// TradingView answered v7.8 with a number:
//   CE10117 · "compiled code contains too many tokens: 100 677. The limit is
//   100 256."  Over by 421.
//
// v7.8's own header predicted ≈ 100 600 and said plainly that it was over. It
// was, by 77 — so the estimate was good and the cut order it published is the
// one this release executes. Nothing was guessed twice.
//
//  ── THE CALIBRATION THIS BUYS — WRITE IT DOWN ──────────────────
//  v7.8 = 35 426 raw source tokens → 100 677 compiled, MEASURED. That is 2.842
//  compiled per raw token on average, and because every fit this lineage has
//  ever made carries a NEGATIVE intercept, the MARGINAL rate is higher than the
//  average — a token removed is worth at least 2.842, not less. v7.9 removes
//  338 raw tokens, so ≈ 960 compiled: it should land near 99 720, about 540
//  under the cap. At a deliberately pessimistic 2.0 it would still land at
//  ≈ 100 000. Either way it fits, and the 540 is the headroom the next release
//  has to spend.
//
//  ── RECLAIMED — NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGED, NOTHING LOST ─────────────
//  R1  FIVE f_prod SLOTS WERE DERIVABLE FROM `prod` ITSELF, and f_prod is
//      INLINED TWICE, so each one cost a declaration, a write and a tuple slot
//      twice over. `hp` IS `pr == PR_FEM` (pr is assigned from poiIsHtf). `mo`
//      was true for the machine and the S/D producer and false for the trap, i.e.
//      `pr > 0 and pr < PR_TRAP`. `rtq`, `fvgE` and `poiE` are each read straight
//      off the object that wrote them. Every replacement reproduces its slot
//      EXACTLY, the no-producer case included. Tuple 26 → 21.
//  R2  THE MTF LADDER'S AGREEMENT ARITHMETIC, WRITTEN ONCE. A bull count AND a
//      bear count AND a total were three four-term expressions saying one thing.
//      One NET figure says it: each usable rung contributes its own ±1, so
//      mtfNet == mtfN is unanimous bull and −mtfN unanimous bear. All four rungs
//      — 5m · 15m · 1H · 4H — are untouched.
//  R3  f_score PUBLISHED ITS FIVE BLOCK SUB-TOTALS AND NOTHING READ THEM. Ten
//      write-only globals (ctxL/S, liqL/S, poiL/S, trgL/S, exeL/S) in a function
//      inlined twice. The arithmetic is untouched and every point still lands in
//      the total; `ev` stays because the debug panel prints it. (V5.5-E6.)
//
//  ── CUT, IN THE ORDER v7.8 PUBLISHED ───────────────────────
//  1 · `tradeLife`, the "abandon a tracked trade after N bars" input. A trade is
//      followed until TP3 or the stop, which is what it did at the default.
//  2 · `Trade.mfe`. The dashboard no longer prints the best excursion in R.
//  3 · the break-even redraw of the drawn SL line. THE STOP STILL MOVES — the
//      tracking, the dashboard's BE tag and the [TRADE] Break-even alert are all
//      unchanged; only the line on the chart stops following it.
//  4 · the v7.8 on-chart TP / SL / BE chips, and `tdDirE` with them. All five
//      [TRADE] alerts still fire and dashboard row 17 still reports the state.
//  Items 1-4 were the whole of v7.8's convenience layer. The ENGINE is untouched:
//  v7.9 emits exactly the trades v7.8 would have emitted, at the same prices.
//
//  ── IF IT STILL RETURNS CE10117 ─────────────────────────
//  The next lever is the lineage's own standing first one, and it is far larger
//  than anything above: MODULE 8B, the liquidity MARKING layer (≈ 3 960 compiled).
//  It is pure annotation — the BSL / SSL labels, strength stars, EXT / INT tags
//  and cluster text. The pool LINES, the registry, the lifecycle and every signal
//  are in MODULE 7 and are not touched by removing it. Delete from
//  `LQ_EXT_PAD = 0.15` down to the `if barstate.islast` render block that ends
//  with `shown += 1`, plus the `lqMark` … `lqRaidChar` input group.
//  NEVER the debug panel: it is what makes every claim in these headers
//  auditable, and the lineage's rule has always been that it is not currency.
//
//  NOT COMPILED SINCE THE FIX — the 100 677 above is real, the 99 720 is an
//  estimate off a single measured anchor. Verify on a chart.

// v7.8 · WHAT HAPPENS AFTER THE ENTRY              (from v7.7, file 27)
//
// WHAT v7.8 IS. One capability the lineage never had, and one reclaim that pays
// part of its bill. No engine, producer, gate, grade, score, tier, stop, target,
// dedupe rule or state machine was touched — v7.8 emits exactly the trades v7.7
// emits, in the same order, at the same prices.
//
//  ── MODULE 16C · TRADE MANAGEMENT ───────────────────────────
//  Every release of this lineage stopped at the entry. It resolved a stop and a
//  three-rung ladder, drew them, and then had no further opinion — no break-even
//  rule, no record of which rung was reached, and not one alert for any of it. An
//  operator running the file on alerts learned when to enter and nothing after.
//  16C tracks the LAST emitted signal: which rung it reached, whether the stop
//  was hit, the best excursion in R, and an optional break-even move. Five new
//  [TRADE] alerts, one dashboard row, one mark per event.
//
//  IT IS BOOKKEEPING, NOT A STRATEGY. It places no order and sizes no position,
//  and it is strictly DOWNSTREAM — nothing in it is read by a producer, a gate, a
//  grade, the score, the dedupe, the registry or either state machine, so it
//  cannot change which trades the engine finds. Three rules keep it honest:
//    1 · THE SIGNAL BAR IS NEVER EVALUATED. Tracking starts on the bar AFTER the
//        entry, because the signal candle's own range is what produced the
//        rejection the entry was taken on. Letting it also reach a target is the
//        V4-BUG-6/7 error — an event confirming itself.
//    2 · THE STOP WINS A SHARED CANDLE. When one bar's range contains both the
//        stop and a target, OHLC cannot say which came first, so the pessimistic
//        reading is taken. It is the only one that cannot flatter the result.
//    3 · BREAK-EVEN MAY NEVER WIDEN RISK. The move applies only when it IMPROVES
//        the stop, R is always measured from the ORIGINAL stop, and a later
//        stop-out is reported as a BREAK-EVEN stop rather than a loss.
//  ONE TRADE AT A TIME: a new signal replaces whatever was being tracked.
//  Three inputs, all in ⑥ Risk: trackTrade · beAfter · beOffset (v7.9 cut a fourth).
//
//  ── RECLAIM ─────────────────────────────────────────
//  f_prod's `tc` slot and the touchL / touchS globals are gone. After v7.6's F-12
//  the call site already resolved the pool by identity, and `prodX > 0 ?
//  f_poolTouch(evPoolX) : 0` reproduces all four cases EXACTLY — machine (tc was
//  that same pool), S/D (tc was 0, the call site supplied it), trap (tc 0, evPool
//  resolves na, which reads 0) and no producer (both 0). One field, one write,
//  one tuple slot and one global, in a function inlined twice.
//
//  ── BUDGET — STATED PLAINLY, BECAUSE IT IS TIGHT ────────────────
//  v7.6 measured ≈ 98 627 compiled of the 100 256 cap, i.e. ≈ 1 630 spare. v7.7
//  and v7.8 together spend more than that on the estimate: the nine v7.7 fixes
//  ≈ +855, the MTF ladder ≈ +510, MODULE 16C ≈ +1 010, less ≈ −405 from the dead
//  state and the `tc` reclaim — call it ≈ 100 600, which is OVER. This has not
//  been compiled on TradingView (the standing caveat of every file in this
//  lineage) and the estimator has been wrong in BOTH directions before, so it may
//  well fit. If it returns CE10117, cut in this order — each item is
//  self-contained and the list runs cheapest-loss first:
//    1 · the `tradeLife` input and the three lines that read it (≈ 35).
//    2 · Trade.mfe: the field, the one line that updates it, and the " · MFE nR"
//        text on dashboard row 17 (≈ 90).
//    3 · the break-even SL line / label redraw in MODULE 17 (≈ 80). The stop still
//        moves, the dashboard still says BE — only the drawn line stops following.
//    4 · the v7.8 event marks in MODULE 17 (≈ 140). The alerts still fire.
//    5 · MODULE 8B's liquidity marking layer (≈ 3 960) — the lineage's own standing
//        first lever, and far more than enough on its own.
//  NEVER the debug panel: it is what makes every claim above auditable, and the
//  lineage's rule has always been that it is not currency.
//
//  NOT COMPILED, NOT BACKTESTED — verify on a chart before relying on any of it.
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// v7.7 · LIFECYCLE FIX PASS                           (from v7.6, file 26)
// Companion document: 01_liqudity_supplay_Forensic_Post_Mortem_Audit.md — the
// forensic audit of v7.6 that found every item below.
//
// WHAT v7.7 IS. Nine findings, fixed. No new producer, no new confluence term,
// no new indicator, no loosened evidence requirement. Every v7.6 rule about
// causality and ownership is intact; six of the nine RESTORE coverage the file
// was losing to a lifecycle bug, and three tighten a claim that was untrue.
//
// WHERE v7.6's REMAINING BUGS LIVED. v7.5 and v7.6 killed the first-generation
// defect — "a GLOBAL flag paying for a SPECIFIC setup". What was left was a
// second generation of a different shape: TWO CORRECT THINGS WIRED TO THE WRONG
// CLOCK, OR TO NO LIFECYCLE AT ALL. Three of the four trade-changing findings
// are the same sentence written three ways — an object a live setup was standing
// on died, or was replaced, and nothing told the setup. The rule this release
// makes structural:
//
//     AN OBJECT A LIVE SETUP IS HOLDING MAY NOT DIE WITHOUT THAT SETUP'S
//     PERMISSION — AND IF IT DOES DIE, THE SETUP MUST BE TOLD.
//
//  ── FOUR DEFECTS THAT CHANGED TRADES ───────────────────────────────────────
//  F-01  reactWin SILENTLY CANCELLED sweepLook AND mssWin. In f_poolScan the
//        sweepLook branch was only reachable from PS_REACT, so an un-reacted
//        raid was retired after reactWin (6) bars — not the 15 its own tooltip
//        advertises, and well inside the 12 the machine's stage 3 to 4 needs.
//        Every raid that did not produce a chart-wide displacement within six
//        bars took its setup down with it ("target pool broken or expired"),
//        which deleted the most ordinary ICT sequence there is: raid, then a
//        retracement, THEN the POI tap and the shift. The two clocks are now
//        separate — reactWin is the REACTION deadline, sweepLook is the CLAIM
//        window — and the consumption test applies to both states.
//  F-02  THE ZONE CAP EVICTED A BOUND POI AND FROZE ITS TIER. The per-side cap
//        removed the OLDEST zone without asking whether a setup was standing on
//        it. Pine keeps the object alive through the setup's reference, but
//        f_regrade only ever walks the `zones` array — so the evicted POI
//        stopped decaying: tier frozen, f_zoneDead unable to fire, state / mitN
//        stopped counting, box deleted, and the machine went on to execute an
//        array that had already been filled. Zone.bound (the exact Pool.bound
//        contract) makes a held POI ineligible for eviction, and the violation
//        path now stamps the zone dead BEFORE it leaves the array.
//  F-03  THE ZONE DEDUPE DESTROYED A LIVE POI AND BUILT NO REPLACEMENT. One
//        pass both set `dup` and deleted weaker overlapping zones, so a new gap
//        overlapping several same-side zones could delete one at a high index
//        and only then meet a stronger one that set `dup` — net effect, a POI
//        gone for nothing. Decide first (pass 1), delete second (pass 2), and
//        only once a replacement is known to be coming.
//  F-04  THE HTF POI HAD NO IDENTITY, SO ITS TIER RATCHET RAN BACKWARDS. The
//        mitigation counters f_htfTier reads reset to 0 whenever the feed stamps
//        a new gap or retires the old one, while the FEM setup still held the
//        OLD array's coordinates and compared against its stored poiIn0. A
//        decayed T2 / T3 HTF POI therefore sprang back to T1 — and to A+ — the
//        moment the HTF printed its next gap. FEM is the highest-ranked producer
//        in the file, so that wrong tier won every arbitration it entered. One
//        monotonic id per side; the setup dies when the feed moves on.
//
//  ── COVERAGE THAT WAS BEING LOST ──────────────────────────────────────────
//  F-05  A ZONE'S RAID WAS "THE FRESHEST ON THE SIDE", NOT "THE ONE AT IT".
//        f_gradeZone read anyHi* / anyLo*, which is a single most-recent raid,
//        while the registry routinely holds several live ones per side. Whenever
//        the raid physically sitting at an array was not also the newest, the
//        array was born T3 — and v7.6's tierW ratchet forbids promotion, so a
//        genuinely A+ array was made permanently untradeable by the arrival
//        order of an unrelated sweep. f_raidAtZone searches the registry and
//        ranks by raid GRADE, recency only as the tie-break; the POOL OBJECT
//        then travels into f_zoneProv, so the stamp records what was graded.
//  F-08  TRENDLINE LIQUIDITY WAS DEAD AT DEFAULTS. The touch counter was zeroed
//        on EVERY new external pivot, and at extPiv = 12 those print faster than
//        tlMinTouch can be reached — so PK_TL pools were effectively never
//        registered and a whole §22 engine sat inert. Rolling the anchor along a
//        line of the SAME slope is a re-anchor, not a new line: the count now
//        survives it, and only a slope reversal restarts it.
//  F-09  TARGET RE-SELECTION RESTARTED THE LOCK CLOCK ON NOISE. The ranking is
//        quality minus a distance penalty, so two comparable pools trade places
//        as price drifts between them — and each swap reset tgtBar, which stage
//        2 to 3 requires the raid to post-date. A setup parked between two such
//        pools could never bind a raid at all; it simply aged out at setupLife.
//        A challenger must now beat the locked pool's live rank by TGT_HYST; a
//        dead or spent target is still replaced unconditionally.
//
//  F-10  A LOWER-RANKED PRODUCER COULD DESTROY A HIGHER-RANKED NARRATIVE.
//        v7.6's `machFirst` stood the S/D producer down while the machine was
//        mid-narrative ON THE SAME POOL. But the collision that costs a trade is
//        the same ARRAY: a setup binds its POI off the raid of pool P, while that
//        zone's own stamped chain may name a different pool Q — so the S/D
//        producer fired on it through Q, marked it signaled, and killed a stage
//        4-7 setup that outranks it ("POI spent or re-graded below the minimum
//        tier"). Zone.bound already records the holder, so the rule is one more
//        comparison: same pool OR same array stands the producer down.
//
//  ── THE DASHBOARD GETS ITS LADDER BACK ────────────────────────
//  MTF   ROW 3 · "MTF 5·15·1H·4H". v7.4 reclaim R2 deleted the multi-timeframe bias
//        table to buy compiled tokens, and with it the one thing a dashboard is
//        for: where THIS chart sits inside the ladder above it. Four fixed rungs
//        — 5m · 15m · 1H · 4H — each read from the LAST CLOSED candle of its
//        timeframe through the file's own non-repainting construction (expr[1]
//        inside the request + lookahead_on, clamped to >= the chart by f_tfUp),
//        plus how many of the USABLE rungs agree. A rung at or below the chart
//        timeframe prints · and is excluded from the count — the chart looking at
//        itself is not context. DISPLAY ONLY: no gate, grade, score, tier, stop,
//        target, dedupe or state transition reads it, and the counter-trend bias
//        FILTER keeps its own separate read, so BUG-16 stays fixed.
//        Cost: 4 request.security instances (6 → 10, against TradingView's 40).
//
//  ── PAID FOR BY DEAD STATE (the V5.5-E6 housekeeping rule) ──────────
//  · `ny830Open` — declared and written on every 08:30 open, read by nothing (the
//    line and its label are drawn from `open` directly).
//  · `rbNew` — declared and set true at both rejection-block creation sites, read
//    by nothing.
//  · `f_zoneId()` — defined, never called.
//  · `Zone.cluHi` / `Zone.cluLo` — written by the clustering pass every render,
//    read by nothing since v7.4 reclaim R4 deleted the cluster boxes.
//
//  ── KNOWN AND NOT ADDRESSED HERE ───────────────────────────
//  · The engine is ENTRY-ONLY. It draws TP1/TP2/TP3 and a stop, and then has no
//    opinion: no break-even rule, no trail, no partial, and no alert when a rung
//    is reached. Position sizing is likewise absent — risk is reported in R and
//    in price, never in lots or account %.
//  · There is no strategy() build, so none of this is natively backtestable.
//  · `filtBias` still costs one request.security even when the bias filter is
//    "Off" — Pine evaluates both branches of the ternary that guards it.
//
//  ── HONESTY OF MEASUREMENT ────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  F-06  A MEASUREMENT WAS WIRED TO A POLICY SWITCH. `congested` folded the
//        user's gate setting into the chop READING, and `regime` took its CHOP
//        verdict from it — so turning the congestion FILTER off also turned the
//        chop MEASUREMENT off: the dashboard reported TREND / RANGE in obvious
//        chop, §24 applied the wrong execution policy, and f_score's descriptor
//        debit stopped being charged. congMeasured (the reading) and congested
//        (the policy) are separate now.
//  F-07  THE EXECUTION DEDUPE USED A DIFFERENT TOLERANCE FROM THE REGISTRY.
//        f_dedupeOk compared prices with `eqTol * atr` while f_poolPush fuses
//        levels with `lqTol`, which carries the eqTickMin tick floor. On a
//        tick-quantised instrument with that floor set, "one liquidity pool to
//        one execution" quietly stopped applying to the levels the floor exists
//        for. Both use lqTol.
//
//  ── ALSO ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  · f_findRaid's `bbnd` return is gone: its one consumer was f_gradeZone's
//    ownership test, which now lives inside f_raidAtZone, where it is applied
//    per CANDIDATE pool rather than to whichever raid happened to be freshest.
//    Write-only state does not survive in this file (V5.5-E6 housekeeping).
//  · reactWin / sweepLook / entryMode tooltips now describe what the code does.
//
//  ── NOT CHANGED, DELIBERATELY ─────────────────────────────────────────────
//  · strictSeq stays a two-site switch. Its tooltip already says so truthfully
//    ("OFF relaxes only the two context stages"); the rest of the chain is
//    enforced unconditionally by the `moved` latch, which is the correct design.
//  · An ELITE trap is still admitted in "Narrative only". That is the v7.1
//    decision and the trap carries its own complete causal chain; only the
//    tooltip, which failed to mention it, changed.
//  · The header changelog below is kept in full. It is the file's audit trail
//    and it costs no compiled tokens.
//
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// v7.6 · FORENSIC FIX PASS                            (from v7.5, file 25)
// Companion documents: AUDIT-v7.5-FORENSIC.md (the audit that found these) and
// AUDIT-v7.6-FIXES.md (what shipped, and how to verify it on a chart).
//
// WHAT v7.6 IS. Twelve findings from the v7.5 forensic audit, fixed. No new
// indicator, no new producer, no new confluence term, no loosened threshold.
// Eleven of the twelve TIGHTEN or correctly re-use evidence the file already
// computes; ONE (F-04) adds setups, by making arrays the file already grades as
// superior tradable instead of permanently excluded.
//
//  ── THREE DEFECTS THAT CHANGED TRADES ──────────────────────────────────────
//  F-01  REVERSAL CONTEXT WAS EFFECTIVELY UNCONDITIONAL. htfRevLong read
//        `not na(hPoiBTop)` — "the HTF holds a live bullish FVG somewhere",
//        which on a liquid instrument is nearly always true. That disjunct
//        swallowed the premium/discount EXTREME requirement the allowRev
//        tooltip advertises and made htfCtxLong tautological: a long setup
//        armed on every bar of a bearish HTF, held the single long slot for
//        setupLife bars, and could never grade. Both verdicts now use the
//        LOCATION test (atHtfPoiL/S) the continuation verdict already used.
//  F-02  THE CE ENTRY PRICE COULD BE A LEVEL THE BAR NEVER TRADED. The S/D
//        producer's 50 % tap may be confirmWin bars old, so the zone midpoint
//        could sit far below the signal bar's low; the machine had the same
//        hole at retestDepth < 0.5. Risk, RR, room and the whole TP ladder were
//        then measured from a fill that was not available — overstated in
//        exactly the direction that passes hRr. The CE level is used only when
//        this bar's range contains it; otherwise the rejection close is.
//  F-06  A ZONE'S "OWNED" MSS WAS THE CHART'S MSS WITH A CLOCK ON IT. v7.5
//        constrained WHEN the shift happened and how old the level it broke
//        was, never WHERE: a CHoCH thirty ATR away still promoted an array to
//        T1 — and T1 is what minTier, f_grade's A+ rung and the entire S/D
//        producer rest on. The broken level must now sit AT the zone and be
//        separated from the raid wick by MSS_MIN_SEP_ATR, which is the
//        machine's own stage-5 test applied to the zone's chain. This was the
//        last global flag deciding a tier.
//
//  ── THE COVERAGE INVERSION ────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  F-04  BREAKER AND IFVG WERE PERMANENTLY UNTRADEABLE. f_zoneFlip zeroed
//        srcPoolId and f_zoneProv only ever ran at creation, so the two arrays
//        f_paRank ranks ABOVE a raw FVG could never be traded by the one
//        producer that trades arrays. A flip whose isBrk chain holds is BETTER
//        evidenced than the gap it replaces, so it now inherits that event as
//        its own chain — provided the recorded raid is on the side the zone now
//        serves (a DEMAND breaker is paid for by a SELL-side raid). The zone
//        records the raid AT it in full (failBuy / failId / failBar / failExt /
//        failPx / failRq / failKind / failTag) instead of a bare boolean, and
//        every src* field is written unconditionally at the flip — v7.5 left
//        nine of them behind as stale values behind the srcPoolId gate.
//
//  ── CORRECTNESS OF CLAIM, AND FAIRNESS ────────────────────────────────────
//  F-03  The ORDER BLOCK inherited a containment test run against the FVG's
//        coordinates. It is a different box at a different price band; the test
//        is re-run on its own edges. A failure costs it the stamp and caps it
//        at T2 — the array is still drawn and still machine-bindable.
//  F-05  f_regrade was NOT the ratchet its own comment claimed. It rebuilt its
//        verdict from tier0 every bar, so state / mitN ratcheted but the HTF and
//        STRUCTURE penalties were reversible: a T1 downgraded when the HTF
//        flipped came straight back when it flipped again. Zone.tierW makes the
//        ratchet structural; only a deliberate re-classification resets it.
//  F-08  ONE WATCH PER SIDE. The trap lived in a single scalar wState, so an
//        upward break OCCUPIED the engine and no downward break could arm until
//        it expired, ran away or fired. For a pattern v7.1 deliberately
//        promoted to tier-1 / A+ eligible that is an arbitrary constraint. The
//        state is a `Trap` UDT now, instantiated twice; the wick memory is four
//        scalars rather than an array. Every threshold and rule is unchanged.
//  F-09  The HTF POI counted CANDLES INSIDE, not mitigations, so price parked
//        just inside retired it in hPoiMaxTouch candles without ever leaving.
//        Edge-triggered now, like the chart zones' mitN.
//  F-10  A CONFLICT armed BOTH direction slots, so the least readable HTF state
//        generated the most candidates. It arms only the side HTF STRUCTURE
//        favours — the layer §8 gives authority to.
//  F-12  The S/D producer never filled `tc` and forfeited up to 3 of its 25
//        LIQUIDITY points. Its pool is resolved by identity two lines later.
//  F-16  f_findRaid did not exclude `bound != 0`, so a raid a machine setup had
//        already claimed could still stamp a new zone. f_anyRaid always applied
//        that test; the asymmetry was unintentional.
//  F-15  The ◆ sweep marks were a LEVEL test, not an EVENT test, and re-printed
//        on every bar that wicked the same swing. One mark per level. Display.
//
//  ── HOW IT IS PAID FOR (no feature is cut) ────────────────────────────────
//   R1  THE ZONE VIOLATION AND RE-GRADE, WRITTEN ONCE. The two side branches of
//       the zone update loop spelled an identical violation block and an
//       identical eligibility test, so f_zoneFlip (517 src) and f_regrade were
//       each INLINED TWICE — the duplication v7.5's own R1 removed from the
//       CREATION path and left on the UPDATE path. Byte-identical decisions.
//   R2  THE NINE DEBUG STATE ROWS, WRITTEN ONCE. Nine inlined copies of
//       f_dbgRow spelling one pattern: ST_CTX … ST_ENTRY ARE 1 … 8, so row r is
//       ticked when st >= r. Same nine rows, same labels, same colours. The
//       lineage's rule is never to CUT the debug panel; nothing here is cut.
//
//  BUDGET. Re-fitted on the lineage's only two MEASURED CE10117 figures (file
//  14 = 99 740, file 17 = 108 630) using an inline-EXPANDED token metric, which
//  is what the cap actually counts — a plain source-token fit cannot see that
//  removing a call site deletes a whole function body. That fit reproduces both
//  measured points exactly and puts v7.5 at ≈ 99 613. v7.6 lands at ≈ 98 627:
//  about 1 630 spare, and ≈ 990 LIGHTER than v7.5 with all twelve fixes in.
//  Top-level statements 964 → 951, so CE10295 is not in play.
//  NOT CHANGED: the state machine's stages and chronology, f_narrOk, the hard
//  gates, the grade ladder, the score's block caps, the risk ladder, the dedupe
//  rules, every input and default, every alert name, the dashboard and the
//  debug panel's content.
//  NOT COMPILED, NOT BACKTESTED — same standing caveat as every file in this
//  lineage. Verify on a chart with AUDIT-v7.6-FIXES.md §D.
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// v7.5 · CAUSAL OWNERSHIP PASS + TWS REMOVAL          (from v7.4, file 24)
// Companion document: AUDIT-v7.5-CAUSAL.md (the forensic audit that produced
// this file: architecture map, P0-P3 findings, repaint audit, budget).
//
// WHAT v7.5 IS. A repair of CAUSALITY, not a feature release. Fewer, better-
// owned setups. No new pattern engine, no oscillator, no new producer.
//
//  R · TWS REMOVED IN FULL. Inputs (useTws / twsWaveWin / twsDeepen), the Tws
//      UDT, f_twsAdvance, f_twsRun, both instances, both chips, the "TWS"
//      descriptor, the dashboard row, the alert, the LC_* lifecycle vocabulary
//      and f_lcTxt (TWS was their last consumer). No decision, score, tier,
//      grade, dashboard state or gate reads anything TWS-derived.
//
//  P0 · SUPPLY / DEMAND PROVENANCE (§6 / §7). v7.4 stamped srcPoolId on EVERY
//      zone from the freshest live raid on the side, even when that raid was
//      nowhere near the zone (`liq` false) - the S/D producer then "owned" a
//      raid it never traded, and the OB inherited the same borrowed id. Now:
//      · provenance is stamped ONLY when the raid wick sits at the zone's
//        ORIGIN (inside, or ≤ sdRaidAtr beyond the far edge; near edge keeps the
//        machine's poiBindAtr slack) - a configurable ATR test, not proximity;
//      · chronology raid < MSS ≤ displacement is enforced and the MSS must have
//        broken a level that formed around the raid (the machine's own `rel`);
//      · the zone records the whole chain: pool id, raid bar, raid WICK, raid
//        price, raid grade, pool class, tag, MSS bar, MSS kind (CHoCH / CISD),
//        displacement bar + quality, inducement, leg extreme;
//      · the S/D producer trades ONLY zones with that chain and reads NOTHING
//        from the live raid on the side: stop = its own raid wick, grade = its
//        own raid grade, MSS / displacement = its own, dedupe id = its own
//        pool, OTE = its own leg (raid wick → leg extreme, deepest pullback).
//        needSweep (the v7.1 "borrowed raid" mode) is retired.
//      · consumption spends the pool the zone OWNS (registry lookup by id).
//
//  P0 · HTF STATE (§8 / §9). Context was `htfDir` = one EMA. Now HS_BULL /
//      HS_BEAR / HS_NEUTRAL / HS_CONFLICT from HTF external STRUCTURE (its own
//      pivots, hStrDir) first and EMA momentum second; disagreement = CONFLICT,
//      never silently resolved. htfConfMode: Off / Downgrade / A+ only
//      (default) / Block. The dealing-range and HTF-POI layers are unchanged.
//
//  P1 · GLOBAL-EVENT CONTAMINATION CLOSED. Gone: confGateLong/Short (global
//      latest CHoCH/CISD + "displacement in the last 3 bars" as the S/D and trap
//      trigger), cMssBar / cDispBd, the score's global-CISD +3 (F-10), the
//      session story read off the freshest raid instead of the traded event,
//      the trap's chart-range OTE credit. f_prod no longer receives any anyLo* /
//      anyHi* argument at all. The trap's trigger block is honest: no MSS, no
//      leg - displacement points only when its reclaim candle displaced.
//  P1 · f_gradeZone read `rp.id` inside a ternary on a possibly-na Pool
//      (runtime error class the lineage forbids). Uses the id series now.
//  P1 · cisdMssUp accepted `strongUp` as "displacement" (a 0.5-ATR candle
//      closing in its outer 30 %), which made mssNeedDisp inert. Removed.
//  P1 · PDH/PDL and PWH/PWL requests were not clamped to ≥ chart (lookahead
//      leak on a weekly / monthly chart). f_tfUp applies to both.
//  P2 · IFVG ≠ FAILED FVG (§14). A closed-through FVG is a FAILED FVG (tier 3,
//      untradable) and becomes a TRUE IFVG (tier 2) only after price returns to
//      it from the other side and HOLDS with a rejection.
//  P2 · ZONE LIFECYCLE (§15): discrete mitigations counted (FRESH → FIRST →
//      SECOND → DEEP); the re-grade caps a second mitigation at T2, a third at
//      T3. BPR overlap re-tags but no longer PROMOTES the tier (an unrelated
//      later gap is not part of this zone's chain).
//  P2 · DISPLACEMENT QUALITY (§12): f_dispQ scores body · 2× body · hold ≥ 55 %
//      · hold ≥ 75 % · wick asymmetry · relative expansion vs the 20-bar mean
//      body; recorded on the setup (dispQ) and the zone (srcDispQ), scored
//      instead of body size alone. Stage-6 HARD tests unchanged.
//  P2 · DEALING RANGE (§23) = the current impulse leg: protected low → the
//      swing high after it (bull), protected high → the swing low after it
//      (bear); latest-pivot pair only as fallback.
//  P2 · REGIME → EXECUTION POLICY (§24, `regPolicy`): TREND penalises a
//      reversal, RANGE penalises the wrong half, EXPANSION refuses the trap's
//      reclaim-close entry, CHOP = congestion gate with a new "A+ only" mode.
//  P2 · STORY IDENTITY (§28): dir | pool | raid bar | POI | MSS bar | FVG bar,
//      kept in a 60-slot ring; an executed story never executes again through
//      any producer. Component dedupes (pool / price / POI / MSS) still apply.
//  P2 · JUDAS (§19) needs a graded raid (≥ B) AND a confirmed reaction
//      (PS_REACT), not just a raid inside a killzone.
//  P2 · FVG zones need the same minimum height as the execution gap (§13).
//  P3 · raidNearPx compared the pool PRICE, not the wick; S/D retest quality is
//      measured (F-10), not the constant 2; f_findRaid lost two dead params.
//
//  DASHBOARD rebuilt to §33 (HTF state / conflict, regime, liquidity, raid,
//  next POI, structure, session, machine, owned narrative per side, grade · RR
//  · room, event, run, alignment). DEBUG adds HTF · regime and the STORY key.
//  RECLAIM (behaviour-neutral unless stated): R1 the mirrored demand / supply
//  zone-creation blocks → one f_mkZone · R2 f_atHtfPoi reused by the HTF
//  context verdict · R3 one f_ctxBox for the HTF-POI and locked-FVG boxes · R4
//  one f_entryLbl · R5 DISPLAY CUT: the session H/L "✓ SWEPT" line annotation
//  (the pool line at that price already restyles when raided / broken).
//  Inputs 168 → 166 (−useTws −twsWaveWin −twsDeepen −needSweep −lqSesState
//  +sdRaidAtr +htfConfMode +regPolicy). Alerts 33 → 32 (−TWS). Outputs 54 → 53.
//  Budget (tokenizer re-fitted on the two real CE10117 results, files 14 / 17:
//  true ≈ 3.422 × srcTok − 9 411): v7.4 31 850 ≈ 99 580 · v7.5 31 659 ≈ 98 930,
//  ≈ 1 330 spare — LIGHTER than v7.4. Not compiled on TradingView yet.
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// v7.4 · ICT LIQUIDITY + PD ARRAY TRADE ENGINE      (from v7.3, file 23)
// Companion documents: AUDIT-v7.3-FORENSIC.md (the audit that produced this
// file — findings F-01…F-14 and the E1 / E2 plan) and the earlier audits.
//
// v7.4 = E1 REPAIRS + E2 SIGNAL GENERATION, paid for by retiring producer ②.
//
//  E1 · REPAIRS (defaults changed: these are corrections, not options)
//   F-01  A trap or an UNOWNED S/D entry spent the unrelated live raid pool
//         (`anyLoPool.spent`), which killed any machine setup bound to it.
//         Consumption is owned now: only an S/D entry whose zone owns the raid
//         (needSweep ON) spends the pool; the trap consumes its watch only.
//   F-02  f_htfTier counted the HTF candle containing the setup's OWN tap as a
//         mitigation, so a FEM POI read tier 2 one HTF candle after the tap and
//         A+ was unreachable in practice. Setup.poiIn0 stamps the count at the
//         tap and exactly one candle is forgiven; a second HTF candle inside,
//         or an opposed HTF, still decays the tier as P1-2 intended.
//   F-03  The elite trap's fifth ingredient accepted `strongUp/Dn` (a close in
//         the outer 30 % of a 0.5-ATR candle) as "displacement". It is the
//         chart displacement candle (dispUpBar/dispDnBar) now, at every site.
//   F-04  Trap admission was gated on `cg` — an UNRELATED live raid on the side
//         — and the event id / tag / dedupe price / descriptor all named that
//         raid. The watch stamps the broken pool (wId / wPx / wKnd) at arming;
//         the producer needs no foreign raid; evId / evPx / evTag / dedupe read
//         the broken level; the "BEAR/BULL TRAP" descriptor no longer lands on
//         a pool the trap did not trade.
//   F-05  Stage 3→4 could bind a POI born AFTER the raid (the reaction leg's own
//         OB / FVG). `z.bornBar <= s.liqBar` is required.
//   F-06  P1-3 made the gap whose THIRD candle is the displacement unreachable
//         (bullFVG is only tested on the current triplet). At disp+1 the [1]
//         triplet is read as well, with the same height and hold tests.
//   F-07  OTE was read from the rejection candle's extreme, not the pullback's.
//         Setup.pbExt tracks the deepest retracement since the FVG lock.
//   F-08  congTight fired on almost every non-trending 10-bar window (4.5 ATR);
//         0.45 → 0.25 × congLen × ATR. Verify on the dashboard `chop N/4` row.
//   F-12  The S/D candidate prefers the zone that OWNS the live raid, then tier.
//   F-13  HTF continuation in premium / discount requires price AT the HTF POI,
//         not merely that one exists somewhere.
//   F-14  `machFirst` (default ON): the S/D producer stands down while the
//         same-side machine is at stage 4–7 on the same pool.
//
//  E2 · SIGNAL GENERATION — each behind an input, default = v7.3 behaviour
//   E2-A  tgtMode "Any qualifying pool": stage 3 binds the first pool on the
//         side raided AFTER the lock whose level priority ≥ tgtMinPri. Identity
//         is still locked at the raid, chronology and f_narrOk are unchanged.
//   E2-B  mssIsDisp: the MSS candle may be the displacement candle when it
//         passes all four stage-6 tests; f_narrOk accepts dispBar == mssBar.
//   E2-C  entryModel "FVG 50% (CE)": entry, stop distance, targets and RR are
//         measured from the gap's consequent encroachment (machine) or the zone
//         midpoint (S/D) instead of the rejection close.
//
//  RECLAIM — what paid for E1 + E2 (≈ +640 source tokens). No engine, gate,
//  grade, score block, producer rank, stop, target or entry alert was touched.
//   R1  Producer ② "Pin Bar Liquidity Trap" (shrinking wicks) RETIRED: dead at
//       the default minTier = 2 since v5.3-C2, no stop, no grade, absorption
//       rather than a trap (AUDIT-v7.2 §F asked for a grade or retirement).
//       Its input, its two branches, the shrinking-wick series and its alert.
//   R2  The 5-row MTF bias TABLE (5 / 15 / 60 / 240 / D) → one row showing the
//       bias-filter timeframe. Four request.security instances gone (10 → 6),
//       which also lowers runtime. The bias FILTER behaves exactly as before.
//   R3  The order-flow zigzag lines (`showOF`, display only).

//       one-line BSL/SSL record, strength stars, EXT/INT and state remain.

//  31 849 source tokens ≈ 99 585 compiled, ≈ 670 spare — LIGHTER than v7.3.

//  ~70 src) and put `close` back at its nine sites. Never the debug panel —
