// ── v7.3 (file 23) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// v7.3 IS THE P1 RESIDUAL PASS. No module added, none removed, no input added,
// none removed, no alert name changed, no producer rank, grade rung or score
// block altered. Four residual ownership defects from the v7.2 forensic audit:
//
//   P1-1  S/D PRODUCER OWNERSHIP GAP. v7.2's BUG-02 fix required the live raid
//         to be spatially CONTAINED in the zone, which stops a raid four
//         hundred points away from lending its wick as a stop — but two raids
//         of the same pool, or two different pools inside one zone, both pass a
//         containment test. The zone already records WHICH physical event made
//         it (srcPoolId / srcRaidBar, stamped in MODULE 9). The producer now
//         requires IDENTITY as well as containment: same pool, same raid bar.
//         The stop is only ever that owned wick — with the sweep requirement
//         off the producer keeps trading, but it no longer inherits a stop from
//         an event it does not own. A zone that FLIPS side also drops its
//         provenance, because the raid that built it is on the wrong side now.
//
//   P1-2  HTF FVG POI TIER WAS FROZEN AT 1. Every HTF POI was stamped tier 1
//         at the tap and never revisited (the live re-grade explicitly skipped
//         `poiIsHtf`), so the FEM producer kept A+ eligibility after the array
//         had been eaten and after the HTF had flipped against it. f_htfTier
//         re-reads the HTF mitigation count and the HTF bias EVERY confirmed
//         bar, on the same monotone-worse ladder f_regrade uses for chart
//         zones: fresh and aligned = 1, one candle inside = 2, more = 3, and an
//         opposed HTF costs one further tier.
//
//   P1-3  POST-DISPLACEMENT INTEGRITY. The machine could lock its execution FVG
//         on the displacement bar itself, so a candle that expanded and was
//         handed straight back still advanced the setup. The gap may now only
//         be locked on a LATER bar, and that bar has to still be outside the
//         POI the expansion left (the ST_DISP reversal kill already covers the
//         MSS level). FVG_OWN_MAX still allows two bars, and a displacement
//         that leaves no gap still falls back to ST_MSS as before.
//
//   P1-4  ELITE TRAP RESIDUAL CONTAMINATION. The trap reached tier 1 / A+ in
//         v7.2 but still read its raid GRADE, its pool CLASS, its touch count
//         and its dedupe identity from `anyLoRq / anyLoKind / anyLoPool /
//         anyLoId` — the freshest raid anywhere on that side, which by
//         construction is never the pool the trap broke (a broken pool is
//         PS_DONE and f_findRaid only returns PS_RAID / PS_REACT). A ① trap
//         now grades off ITS OWN five-ingredient chain (f_trapQual, which is
//         RQ_A exactly when the trap is elite, so the A+ rung is unchanged)
//         and off the CLASS of the pool it actually broke, stamped on the
//         watch at arming time. Unknown inputs read as unknown, not as
//         somebody else's.
//
// Paid for by pure-alias elimination and redundant-guard deletion (the reclaim
// classes v7.2 proved): five alert aliases and four state-edge aliases inlined
// at their single call sites, `not na(anyLoBar/anyHiBar)` deleted from the
// confluence chronology gate (sslInPlay/bslInPlay already prove the raid bar),
// `not na(bBar/sBar)` deleted from f_htfPoi's touch counters (set and cleared
// with bTop/sTop), `not na(rbar)` deleted from f_gradeZone (f_recent is
// na-safe), and the extResBar / extSupBar / htfPd / descN aliases folded into
// their one use each.
//
// v7.2 is a PURE FORENSIC FIX PASS. No module was added, none removed, no input
// added and none removed; one input (needSweep) was retitled and re-defaulted.
// Eight defects fixed, P0 first:
//   BUG-01 P0 request.security to a timeframe BELOW the chart leaked future data
//              through lookahead_on. f_tfUp clamps every requested tf up.
//   BUG-02 P0 the confluence (S/D) producer inherited the most recent raid on its
//              side with no spatial relation to the zone it traded. Containment
//              now required, at the one site both sides pass through.
//   BUG-03 P1 zone tier 1 was partly bought with the GLOBAL chart OTE.
//   BUG-04 P1 retBeforeFail was true on the very bar that read it, so BREAKER
//              classification was missing one of its three requirements.
//   BUG-05 P1 an unowned displacement promoted a grade-C raid to grade B, which
//              is the grade-A rung's own requirement.
//   BUG-06 P1 the elite trap's advertised A+ eligibility was unreachable at the
//              default, and inverted when oteNeedAp was switched off.
//   BUG-07 P1 the trap's "the broken level must be tracked liquidity" never
//              compared the broken pool with the level that armed the trap.
//   BUG-10 P3 two alertconditions duplicated "Any LONG" / "Any SHORT".
//
// V7.1 · ENTRY-ACCURACY PASS. Four surgical changes, no architecture touched:
//   1 · POI BINDING IS CONTAINMENT (§7 / §40) — the array must hold the raid
//       extreme, not sit two ATR from it; it must not be older than poiMaxAge;
//       and the displacement must genuinely leave it (POI_LEAVE_ATR 0 → 0.10).
//   2 · DISPLACEMENT HAS FOUR REQUIREMENTS (§2) — its own body input
//       (dispBodyAtr, default 1.3 × ATR), a close beyond the MSS level, a close
//       outside the POI, and a close that HOLDS (dispCloseFrac); a candle that
//       hands the expansion back through the MSS level kills the setup.
//   3 · THE TRAP ENGINE CAN REACH TIER 1 (§15 / §16) — an ELITE trap (all five
//       ingredients) gets a tier-1 POI, is A+ eligible, and is admitted in
//       every entry mode including "Narrative only".
//   4 · LATE ENTRY + OTE ARE GRADED (§10 / §11 / §48) — A+ only on the first
//       or second touch of the execution gap; OTE returns a BAND CLASS
//       (62-70 % prime = full points + A+ · 70-79 % deep = half points, A max
//       · > 79 % = one point, never A+).
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// 1 · CRT AND TURTLE SOUP ARE GONE, IN FULL
// Removed: fifteen inputs, the Crt and Tbs UDTs and both five-stage lifecycle
// machines (each instantiated twice), the four turtle-soup extreme series, the
// two cooldown pairs, f_ctt_crtCand / f_ctt_tbsCand / f_ctt_htfCand, the HTF
// CRT/TBS request.security, the entire CTT marking layer (Ctt UDT, f_cttTxt,
// f_cttMark inlined four times, f_cttLvl, f_ctt_chart, f_ctt_htfMark), the
// PR_CRT and PR_TBS producer branches in f_prod, their consumption branches,
// their dashboard row and five alerts. Verified absent: no identifier
// containing crt / tbs / ctt / turtle survives outside prose.
//
// 2 · WHAT REPLACED THEM — CAUSALITY, NOT ANOTHER PATTERN
// The brief's rule is that confluence may not stand in for causality. The
// single worst violation in v6.4 was not CRT: it was f_gradeZone awarding a
// TIER 1 POI from two GLOBAL flags — "a raid exists somewhere on this side"
// and "a shift happened within ten bars" — neither of which had to be near the
// zone or in the right order. Tier 1 is what the grade ladder, the A+ rung and
// the minTier gate are all built on, so that one substitution mis-priced every
// decision downstream of it. It is now causal and the evidence is recorded on
// the zone (srcPoolId / srcRaidBar / srcMssBar / srcDispBar).
//
// 3 · ICT COMPONENTS ADDED
//   §5  BOS / CHoCH / MSS / CISD separated. v6.4 had mssUp = chochUp or cisdUp,
//       which made every delivery flip a structure shift. A CISD is now an MSS
//       only when it carries displacement (mssNeedDisp).
//   §6  HTF external structure published from the HTF's own pivots (hStrDir),
//       so the hierarchy is visible and an LTF shift cannot masquerade as it.
//   §7  HTF swing extremes registered as PK_HTF liquidity; target selection is
//       a RANKING (type, confluence, respect, distance) instead of "nearest".
//   §10 Premium / discount is a REVERSAL PRECONDITION, not a score term only.
//   §11 OTE measured on the setup's OWN impulse leg (raid extreme → displacement
//       extreme), not on the chart's global dealing range. A+ requires it.
//   §14 Order blocks: the last opposing candle of a displacement leg that also
//       broke structure and left an FVG. Never "every opposite candle".
//   §13 IFVG separated from BREAKER and MITIGATION by WHAT failed.
//   §17 BPR: an FVG opening inside a live opposing FVG re-tags the overlap.
//   §22 Trendline liquidity: confirmed anchors, correct slope, minimum touch
//       count, and it enters the registry only when SWEPT — so a trendline
//       break can never by itself produce anything.
//   §36 Market regime (TREND / RANGE / EXPANSION / CHOP) from price action.
//   §38 Inducement: a minor pool taken before the setup's own external raid.
//   §39 AMD / Judas as session CONTEXT, attached as an event descriptor.
//   §40 The POI stage ranks the PD ARRAY, not just the tier.
//   §48 PREMATURE / VALID / LATE. The clock starts at the FIRST touch of the
//       execution FVG; a rejection arriving later is refused outright.
//
// 4 · WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY *NOT* ADDED (§46 — do not overfit)
// No new oscillator, no candlestick-pattern library, and no second gate built
// on evidence an existing gate already reads. The regime classifier is
// reported and scored but adds NO gate, because congestion — the same
// evidence — is already wired as one.
//
// 5 · NON-REPAINTING
// Unchanged in kind, and unchanged in COUNT: six request.security call sites
// (f_bias, inlined at five timeframes; HTF context; PDH/PDL; PWH/PWL; HTF
// structure; HTF FVG POI) = ten instances, exactly as v6.4 had — the HTF
// structure feed took over the slot the CRT/TBS pair vacated. Every one reads
// an expression indexed [1] INSIDE the request with lookahead_on, which is the
// only combination that is both leak-free and stable. The new HTF structure
// feed follows the same construction and its pivots are ta.pivot*(2,2), so a
// level is visible only after the HTF candle that confirmed it has closed.
// Every state latch remains gated on `confirmed`.
//
// 6 · BUDGET (measured, same model the v6.4 audit calibrated)
// v6.4 = 31 873 source tokens ≈ 99 467 compiled of 100 256 — 789 spare.
// v7.1 — see AUDIT-v7.1-ICT.md for the measured budget.
// v7.0 = 31 555 source tokens ≈ 98 380 compiled — about 1 880 spare, with the
// whole ICT layer added. Main body 1 863 of the 2 034 the lineage is known to
// accept. If TradingView still returns CE10117, the cut order is: showTL off
// costs nothing but the trendline engine (~620 compiled); then MODULE 8B's
// liquidity marking (≈ 3 960); then the debug panel (≈ 3 680).
// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Liquidity + Supply/Demand + Price Action Setups [MTF] — v5 "CAUSAL TRADE ENGINE"
//
// Built from v4. v4 introduced the VISUAL → CONFLUENCE → TRADE ENGINE → SIGNAL
// split and a per-direction setup state machine. v4's machine was still able to
// walk its whole narrative inside ONE candle, could claim a liquidity raid that
// happened BEFORE the setup existed, never checked that the raid actually hit
// the pool the setup was hunting, and scored itself from GLOBAL "something
// happened recently" flags that belonged to no setup at all.
//
// v5 makes the narrative OWNED and CHRONOLOGICAL, and adds a hard-gate layer
// that a high score can never buy its way past:
//
//     CONTEXT → TARGET → RAID → POI → MSS → DISPLACEMENT → FVG → RETEST
//         ↓        ↓       ↓      ↓      ↓         ↓          ↓       ↓
//      ctxBar < tgtBar < raidBar ≤ poiBar < mssBar < dispBar ≤ fvgBar < retestBar
//
// ── v5 ARCHITECTURE ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  V5-1  EVENT OWNERSHIP. Every stage stamps its own bar AND the identity of
//        the object that produced it (targetPoolId, raidPoolId, poiId, mssLvl,
//        fvgBar). Nothing global can satisfy a stage any more.
//  V5-2  CHRONOLOGY IS A HARD GATE. Each transition compares bars, so the
//        machine can advance at most one stage per bar except the two pairs
//        that are physically simultaneous (raid candle = POI tap candle,
//        displacement candle = 3rd candle of its own FVG).
//  V5-3  TARGET ↔ RAID IDENTITY. The setup locks a target POOL ID at state 2
//        and state 3 only fires when THAT pool is raided. A different pool at a
//        similar price does not satisfy the setup.
//  V5-4  NO SAME-BAR RE-ARM. Any invalidation stamps resetBar; a fresh setup
//        cannot be armed on the candle that killed the previous one.
//  V5-5  RAID QUALITY GRADING (A/B/C) from penetration depth, close-back
//        strength and post-raid reaction — a 1-tick poke is no longer "a raid".
//  V5-6  POI TIER IS DYNAMIC. Tiers are re-graded every bar from mitigation,
//        HTF agreement and structure; a zone can fall T1 → T2 → T3 → dead.
//  V5-7  HARD GATES vs SOFT SCORE. Hard gates: narrative integrity, chronology,
//        ownership, RR floor, opposing-liquidity room, dedupe, tier, session /
//        volatility. Soft score only ranks what already passed.
//  V5-8  RR + OPPOSING LIQUIDITY are computed BEFORE the signal is emitted, so
//        a trade with no room to its first opposing pool is blocked, not drawn.
//  V5-9  GRADE = A+ / A / B, each with its own RR floor and its own evidence
//        requirements. A+ needs the complete owned narrative, a grade-A raid,
//        a tier-1 POI and RR ≥ the A+ floor. Score alone can never make an A+.
//  V5-10 HTF IS NON-REPAINTING BY CONSTRUCTION. Every request.security now
//        reads the LAST CLOSED HTF bar (expr[1] + lookahead_on), which is the
//        only combination that is both leak-free and stable in realtime.
//  V5-11 DEDUPE: one pool → one execution, one POI → one execution, one MSS →
//        one execution, plus the per-direction cooldown v4 already had.
//  V5-12 SESSION NARRATIVE: Asia→London and London→NY raids are recognised as
//        the liquidity story they are and scored as context, not as a filter.
//
// ── v4 DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED HERE (detail in AUDIT-v5.md) ────────────────
//  V4-BUG-1  SAME-BAR STATE CASCADE. f_setupAdvance ran the stage blocks as a
//            straight sequence of independent `if s.st == ST_x` tests inside
//            ONE call, and every stage's trigger could be true on the same
//            candle: HTF context is a standing condition, a liquidity target is
//            a standing condition, the raid/POI/MSS/displacement can all be the
//            same candle, and only state 7→8 had a `bar_index > s.fvgBar`
//            guard. A single candle could therefore walk 0→7. v5 stamps a bar
//            per stage and compares them (V5-2).
//  V4-BUG-2  RETROACTIVE RAID CLAIM. f_findRaid returned any pool raided within
//            `sweepLook` bars with `bound == 0`, and the setup was armed by
//            htfCtx on a LATER bar, so a setup born at bar 103 legally claimed
//            the raid from bar 100. v5 requires raidBar > tgtBar > ctxBar
//            ≥ bornBar.
//  V4-BUG-3  TARGET AND RAID WERE UNRELATED. State 1→2 stored `tgtPx` from the
//            nearest pool but state 2→3 called f_findRaid, which returns the
//            FRESHEST raid on that side — any pool. Target = EQL at 1.0850 and
//            raid = an unrelated swing low at 1.0910 satisfied the same setup.
//            v5 locks the pool id (V5-3).
//  V4-BUG-4  SAME-BAR RE-ARM. Every invalidation branch called f_setupReset,
//            and the very next block in the SAME call was `if s.st == ST_NONE`
//            → re-arm. A setup could die and be reborn on one candle, which
//            also reset its life clock forever.
//  V4-BUG-5  GLOBAL EVENT CONTAMINATION IN THE SCORE. f_score read
//            recentMssUp / recentDispUp / cisdUpBar / raidExtLo / sslStrong —
//            all global "did this happen in the last N bars" flags. Events from
//            a dead setup, or from the opposite side of the chart, paid the
//            current one. v5 scores setup-owned fields only.
//  V4-BUG-6  MSS COULD PRE-DATE THE RAID. State 4→5 accepted `chochUp or
//            cisdUp` with no comparison to s.liqBar, and because the whole
//            chain could run in one call the MSS was often the raid candle
//            itself. v5 requires mssBar > raidBar and a level that formed near
//            the raid, at least MSS_MIN_SEP_ATR away from the raid extreme.
//  V4-BUG-7  DISPLACEMENT WAS JUST A BIG CANDLE. State 5→6 accepted any
//            dispUpBar within dispWin, including the MSS candle itself. v5
//            requires dispBar > mssBar, a close beyond the MSS level, and a
//            close outside the POI (price actually left the origin).
//  V4-BUG-8  FVG UNRELATED TO THE DISPLACEMENT. State 6→7 accepted any bullFVG
//            within `fvgWin` (default 10) bars of the displacement. A gap ten
//            candles later, from a different leg, was locked as "the
//            displacement's FVG". v5 requires the displacement candle to be one
//            of the three candles that form the gap (bar_index - dispBar ≤ 2).
//  V4-BUG-9  HTF DATA WAS THE DEVELOPING HTF BAR. v4 removed v2's [1] from
//            every lookahead_off security call (its BUG-15). lookahead_off does
//            NOT mean "last closed bar" — it means "no future data", and the
//            forming HTF bar is not future data, so f_htfCtx / f_htfPoi /
//            f_crtTbsHtf / f_bias all read a bar that was still being built and
//            changed as it built. Worse, `newHtfBar` fires on the FIRST chart
//            bar of a new HTF candle, so the HTF CRT pattern was evaluated on
//            an HTF candle roughly one chart bar old. v5 uses expr[1] +
//            lookahead_on = exactly the last CLOSED HTF bar (V5-10).
//  V4-BUG-10 A RAID WAS A RAID AT ANY DEPTH. f_poolScan only needed `high >
//            p.px and close < p.px`, so a one-tick poke graded the same as a
//            0.6·ATR sweep with a full close-back. v5 needs raidMinAtr of
//            penetration to register at all, and grades A/B/C above that.
//  V4-BUG-11 POI TIER WAS FROZEN AT CREATION. f_gradeZone ran once, in
//            f_newZone. A T1 demand zone stayed T1 after the HTF flipped
//            bearish, after external structure broke down and after the zone
//            was 90 % mitigated. v5 re-grades every zone every confirmed bar.
//  V4-BUG-12 NO RR OR OPPOSING-LIQUIDITY GATE. MODULE 16 computed SL and TP
//            AFTER MODULE 15 had already emitted, so a signal whose structural
//            stop was 3·ATR wide with an un-raided pool 0.4·ATR overhead still
//            printed, drew a TP ladder and fired its alert. v5 computes risk
//            first and gates on it (V5-8).
//  V4-BUG-13 A+ WAS TWO CONDITIONS. `aPlusLong = prodL <= PR_MACH and tierL ==
//            1` — no RR, no raid quality, no chronology check, and tierL came
//            from the frozen creation tier of BUG-11.
//  V4-BUG-14 DEDUPE WAS COOLDOWN ONLY. After masterCd bars the same pool, the
//            same POI and the same MSS could produce a second and third entry,
//            because pool.bound was set but never consulted on re-entry and the
//            zone was only marked spent for the S/D producer.
//  V4-BUG-15 CONFLUENCE PRODUCERS HAD NO CHRONOLOGY. confGateLong was
//            `sslInPlay and (recentMssUp or mssUp) and recentDispUp` — three
//            independent global flags in any order, so "displacement, then MSS,
//            then an old sweep still in play" passed.
//  V4-BUG-16 REVERSALS WERE INVISIBLE. htfCtxLong was an OR chain including
//            `htfPd <= 0.5`, so a counter-trend long in a bearish HTF was
//            classified identically to a continuation long. v5 classifies the
//            setup as REVERSAL and demands grade-A raid + T1 POI + CHoCH.
//
// ── v5.1 · PHASE A — THE QUALITY LAYER WAS INERT AT DEFAULTS ───────────────
// An audit of v5 found that most of the "hard gates a high score can never buy
// past" evaluated TRUE unconditionally with the shipped inputs. At defaults,
// emission reduced to: a producer fired + POI tier ≤ 2 + cooldown + dedupe.
// Phase A restores the five gates that v5 claimed to have:
//  A1  RR WAS CIRCULAR. f_tp seeded t2 = entry + rrMult*risk and MODULE 15 then
//      computed RR as (t2-entry)/risk — i.e. rrMult, exactly. Defaults
//      rrMult 2.0 ≥ rrAplus 2.0 ≥ rrA ≥ rrB, so every RR floor passed on every
//      bar. In "Next level" mode f_tp never wrote t2 at all, making RR a literal
//      constant. TP2 is now the nearest opposing wall / external pool / internal
//      pool that is at least the grade-B floor away; when nothing qualifies the
//      new hTgt gate blocks the trade instead of inventing a target. TP1 is
//      capped to TP2 (v5 raised TP2 to TP1, so a 5R TP1 pool bought an A+ RR)
//      and tpClamp now clamps all three rungs, not only TP3.
//  A2  f_grade OPENED AT GR_B UNCONDITIONALLY, so a tier-3, un-graded-raid,
//      no-narrative candidate graded B and cleared the default minGrade = "B".
//      Grade B now requires a graded raid, the B RR floor and a real target;
//      below that the grade is GR_NONE and the trade does not exist.
//  A3  REVERSAL WAS 1 OF THE 4 PROMISED CONDITIONS (`not rev or mssCh`), and
//      failing it only downgraded into the grade that trades by default. It is
//      a hard block now. Separately, `not needHtfAlign or ...` made htfCtx
//      tautological, which bypassed allowRev AND the premium/discount-extreme
//      requirement; allowRev = OFF is enforced at the arming stage.
//  A4  THE ATR FALLBACK STOP BYPASSED THE RISK CLAMP. When every structural
//      level was rejected FOR BEING WIDER than maxRiskAtr, f_sl returned
//      entry ± atrStopMult*atr unchecked — a stop inside the level that
//      invalidates the thesis. f_sl reports slOk and MODULE 15 gates on it.
//  A5  OPPOSING LIQUIDITY WAS DOUBLE-COUNTED. rr = math.min(rrRaw, room) meant
//      the documented soft mode (oppLiqHard = OFF) hard-blocked anyway whenever
//      oppLiqMult ≤ rrB, and reported it as an RR failure. RR is pure now; room
//      is a gate (hard mode) or a one-step grade downgrade (soft mode).
//      "No opposing liquidity" is na, not the 99.0 sentinel v5 printed.
//  A6  RR floors and the risk clamp are normalised (rrAPn/rrAn/rrBn,
//      minRiskN/maxRiskN) so an inverted input cannot make A+ easier than A or
//      silently force every trade onto the ATR fallback, and every RR
//      comparison goes through f_geq to survive float cancellation.
//
// ── v5.2 · PHASE B — REGISTRY IDENTITY + RISK / LIFECYCLE CONSISTENCY ──────
//  B1  ENGINEERED / EXTERNAL POOLS COULD NOT REGISTER. f_poolPush refused any
//      pool within eqTol·ATR of a live same-side pool — and every EQH/EQL (the
//      mean of two pivots whose first pivot already registered as a SWING
//      pool ≤ 0.075·ATR away) and nearly every EXT pivot (the same price as
//      its earlier internal pivot) is exactly that. The registry therefore
//      almost never held PK_EQ or PK_EXT pools: raidEq*/raidExt* rarely fired,
//      the CRT/TBS location filter lost two of its legs, the liquidity score
//      paid 3 (swing) instead of 6–8, and the TP ladder's external scope was
//      empty, so hTgt blocked trades that had real external targets. A
//      duplicate that outranks a live un-raided SWING pool now UPGRADES that
//      pool in place (kind, tag, px, +touch) — id, binding, bornBar and touch
//      history are preserved, so nothing a setup owns is invalidated.
//  B2  DOUBLE STOP PADDING. CRT / TBS / TL / PA / HTF producers handed f_sl a
//      stop already padded by SL_PAD_ATR and f_sl padded every candidate
//      again — every confluence stop was 2× padded (the machine's raw liqExt
//      was correct). Producers now hand RAW levels; f_sl owns the pad.
//  B3  A TRANSIENT BLOCK NO LONGER DESTROYS THE NARRATIVE. A machine setup
//      that reached state 8 but was refused by cooldown / session / RR / room /
//      target / grade / arbitration died unconsumed on the next bar's state-9
//      cleanup. It now returns to state 7 and may retest again inside
//      retestWin. PERMANENT verdicts stay terminal: failed narrative integrity
//      and dedupe (this pool / POI / MSS already traded) still kill it.
//  B4  POST-RAID RECLAIM IS A BREAK. f_poolScan marked a post-raid close back
//      through the level PS_DONE without stamping brkBar, so the pool-break
//      flags missed it and the machine kept hunting an MSS off a failed sweep
//      until the deeper close-beyond-the-WICK test. Every close-beyond path
//      stamps brkBar now, and the machine invalidates on it.
//  B5  f_narrOk demanded only mssBar ≥ poiBar; the machine can never produce
//      the equality, so the independent gate said less than the machine does.
//      Also renamed two dashboard locals that shadowed the global freshS.
//
// ── v5.3 · PHASE C — LIVE TIER AT THE GRADE + HONEST DOCS ──────────────────
//  C1  THE GRADE READ A FROZEN POI TIER. s.poiTier was stamped once at the
//      stage-4 POI tap and never refreshed, while f_regrade decays every zone
//      every confirmed bar (V5-6). Up to retestWin bars later the entry was
//      graded — and hTier gated — on the tier the zone HAD, not the tier it
//      HAS: a T1 that fell to T2 (HTF flipped / structure flipped / 50 %
//      eaten) still bought an A+, which §6 of the audit explicitly promised
//      it could not. The machine now refreshes s.poiTier from the live zone
//      on every confirmed bar (na-safe f_zoneTier), so the grade, the hTier
//      gate and the debug panel all read the re-graded tier. The existing
//      f_zoneDead invalidation still kills the setup outright when the live
//      tier falls below minTier. HTF FVG POIs have no re-grade engine and
//      keep their fixed tier 1 — stated below as a known limit.
//  C2  LEGACY PRODUCERS WERE SILENTLY DEAD AT DEFAULTS. The trendline and
//      price-action producers carry no POI, so their tier is 3 — and the
//      default minTier = 2 hard-blocks tier 3, so "All engines (legacy)"
//      added producers that could never emit and nothing said so. Behaviour
//      KEPT (quality over frequency); the entryMode and minTier tooltips now
//      state the interaction: legacy producers trade only with minTier = 3.
//  C3/C5  Two comments said the opposite of what the code does (the MODULE 11
//      wFired latch IS one-candidate-per-watch by design; pool registration
//      order does NOT prevent same-bar raids — pivot construction does).
//  C4  The tpClamp tooltip claimed all three rungs are capped in every mode;
//      the clamp exists in Liquidity-ladder mode only. Tooltip now says so.
//  Known-and-left in Phase C: poiFresh stays frozen at the tap (5 soft points
//  only) · a second, deeper wick into an already-raided pool does not upgrade
//  its raid grade (raid identity is the FIRST raid, by design) · an HTF POI
//  has no dedupe id (the pool and MSS dedupes still bind FEM re-entries).
//
// ── v5.4 · PHASE D — RUNTIME (TradingView "heavy script" warning) ──────────
//  Pure performance. NO signal, gate, grade, input, alert or drawing changes —
//  every value the engine trades on is bit-identical to v5.3.
//  D1  ZONE GEOMETRY IS CACHED. box.get_top / box.get_bottom are drawing-API
//      reads and this script called them from every per-bar loop: the zone
//      update loop, the next-POI scan, the machine's POI selection, f_nextZone,
//      f_oppLiq and four overlap scans — dozens of API reads per bar. A zone's
//      box coordinates never change after creation (verified: no box.set_top /
//      box.set_bottom exists in the file; flips restyle, they do not move), so
//      Zone now carries `top` / `bot` fields stamped once in f_newZone and the
//      hot paths read those. Display code still uses the box.
//  D2  THE RISK STACK NO LONGER SCANS ON EVERY BAR. f_tp (→ f_nextPool ×2,
//      f_nextZone, f_oppLiq) plus the standalone f_oppLiq room check ran
//      unconditionally for BOTH directions on EVERY bar — ~12 full pool+zone
//      scans per bar to price signals that exist on almost none of them. The
//      scans are gated on `prodX > 0 or barstate.islast` (the established
//      pattern: the gate is passed INTO the function, never a ternary around
//      the call — Pine evaluates both ternary branches). Correctness: okLong /
//      okShort already require prodX > 0, and the only other consumers of
//      RR / room / TP are the dashboard and debug panel, which draw on the
//      last bar only — where the gate is true.
//  What Phase D cannot fix: the 9 request.security instances (5 bias TFs +
//  context + PDH/PDL + HTF CRT/TBS + HTF POI) are a fixed cost — Pine forbids
//  conditional security calls. If the warning persists, the ranked removal
//  levers in AUDIT-v5.md §9 apply (price-action producers → trendlines →
//  verbose debug → order-flow zigzag → HTF CRT/TBS, which also drops one
//  security call), and chart-side: disable the MTF dashboard rows you do not
//  use is NOT possible per-row — but showOF, showStruct chips and showDev off
//  reduce drawing churn.
//
// ── v5.5 · PHASE E — EVENT IDENTITY, THE FOUR MISSING ENGINES, AND 9 DEFECTS ─
// A forensic re-audit of v5.4 (AUDIT-v5.md §14) found nine defects that survived
// Phases A–D and six subsystems the operator brief specifies that this lineage
// had never built. Both are addressed here. Two legacy producers were REMOVED to
// pay for it, with reasons (§40 requires the reasons, not the silence).
//
// ── the nine defects ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  E1  THE RAID CANDLE COULD BE ITS OWN REACTION. f_poolScan's PS_RAID →
//      PS_REACT test was not compared to raidBar, and that path lifts the raid
//      grade RQ_C → RQ_B. So a shallow poke whose own candle carried an
//      unrelated internal CHoCH / CISD was re-graded B on the spot — and
//      RQ_B is exactly what f_grade needs to reach grade A. Same family as
//      V4-BUG-6/7 (an event confirming itself) and V4-BUG-5 (a global flag
//      paying a specific setup). The reaction must now be a LATER bar.
//      NOTE, and this is deliberate: Setup.liqQual is still FROZEN at the
//      stage-3 bind. It looks like V5.3-C1's frozen POI tier and it must NOT
//      get the same treatment — pool.rq improves off GLOBAL flags, so
//      refreshing it would pipe V4-BUG-5 contamination straight into the grade.
//  E2  THE S/D PRODUCER SCORED THE WRONG ZONE'S FRESHNESS. freshL := nbFresh /
//      freshS := nsFresh read the NEXT buy / sell POI from MODULE 9's scan,
//      which is not necessarily sdZoneL / sdZoneS. Now f_zoneFresh(sdZoneL).
//  E3  CROSS-PRODUCER STOP LEAK. lvlPoiL = not na(poiBotL) ? poiBotL :
//      sdBotLong — but PR_SD already writes poiBotL from sdBotLong, so the
//      fallback could ONLY fire for a producer with no POI at all (CRT / turtle
//      soup / trap), handing f_sl an unrelated zone edge as stop candidate 4.
//      A producer with no POI now simply has no POI stop candidate.
//  E4  THE REASON LINE NAMED THE WRONG EVENT. reasonLong / reasonShort and both
//      entry labels read upS.liqTag / upS.sbWindow for EVERY producer, so five
//      of the seven printed the machine setup's raid — usually blank or stale.
//      Event ownership is resolved once now (evTagL / evIdL / evPxL / sbWinL).
//  E5  documented, not "fixed": a setup OUTLIVES the registry retirement of its
//      own bound pool. Only spent / brkBar invalidate; the time- and
//      distance-based retirements are silent, and with reactWin = 6 against the
//      machine's own 12 + 10 + 2 + 25 the pool is routinely dead before the
//      entry. Killing the setup there would kill nearly every setup, because
//      the two clocks are deliberately different — so the DAMAGE is fixed
//      instead, in E7.
//  E6  EIGHT WRITE-ONLY UDT FIELDS removed: Setup.tgtPx / tgtKind / liqPx /
//      liqDepth / dispExt / retestPen, Pool.reactBar, Zone.mitig. Zone.mitig
//      was also COMPUTED every confirmed bar and never read — f_regrade's
//      "50 % mitigated → never better than T2" runs off z.state, not mitig.
//  E7  DEDUPE BY PRICE, NOT ONLY BY ID. E5's real consequence: a retired pool's
//      level re-registers as a NEW pool with a NEW id, so "one liquidity pool →
//      one execution" silently stopped applying to the same physical level.
//      f_dedupeOk now also refuses a second entry within eqTol·ATR of the last
//      executed level on that side, inside setupLife bars.
//  E8  f_blockReason and f_reason were rebuilt on EVERY historical bar — about
//      twenty str.tostring calls and their concatenations, both sides — while
//      their only consumers are the debug table (last bar) and the entry label.
//      Pine evaluates both ternary branches, so the gate had to go INSIDE, the
//      same pattern Phase D used for the risk scanners.
//  E9  neutralised as a side effect: f_poolPush's B1 upgrade moves q.px, which
//      stranded Setup.tgtPx. That field is gone (E6) and evPx reads a pool that
//      is already past PS_VAL, which the upgrade can never re-price.
//
// ── the six subsystems the brief asked for and v5.4 did not have ────────────
//  E · EVENT IDENTITY + DESCRIPTORS (§7 / §8). ONE physical event = ONE Pool =
//      one id. Pool carries `desc` / `descN`, seeded at the raid with its own
//      grade (WICK / SWEEP / DEEP RAID); every engine that recognises the same
//      raid APPENDS a descriptor via f_evTag. Descriptors are worth ONE capped
//      allowance of at most 6 points, between all of them, in a score block
//      that is structurally unable to reach LIQUIDITY — which is still paid
//      exactly once, from that event's own raid grade, pool class and touch
//      history. A sweep that is also a CRT low raid, a turtle-soup extreme, a
//      TWS wave 2 and a bear trap is ONE event with FOUR descriptors, and it
//      now prints that way on the dashboard, the debug panel and the entry
//      reason instead of reading as four confirmations.
//  E1 · CRT IS A STATE MACHINE (§12). v5.4 tested three candles in one boolean.
//      Now: LC_IDLE → LC_ARMED (the reference candle has CLOSED; hi/lo/mid/size
//      stored) → LC_SWEPT (penetration graded CP_TINY / CP_MEAN / CP_DEEP off
//      the SAME raidMinAtr / raidDeepAtr the pool registry uses) → LC_RECL (a
//      later close back through the MID — a different event from the sweep
//      candle's own close-back-inside) → LC_CONF (a later displacement) ·
//      LC_INVAL when the sweep becomes a break. CP_TINY can reach LC_CONF but
//      is never tradable: §12's one-tick-wick rule, enforced not asserted.
//  E2 · TURTLE SOUP IS A COMPOSER (§13). Same lifecycle, with the reference
//      window, the extreme's minimum age, the penetration grading, a decisive
//      reclaim distance and a USER-CHOSEN confirmation rule (reclaim only /
//      reclaim + displacement / reclaim + CISD) as separate stages.
//  E3 · TWS (§14) — three waves: interaction → a deeper, REJECTED manipulation
//      → reclaim with displacement. §14 calls TWS a context engine, so it can
//      never emit, never writes a stop and never ranks: it attaches a
//      descriptor and nothing else.
//  E4 · LIQUIDITY RUN ENGINE (§11). v5.4 had `runUp`/`runDn`: a weak close
//      through ONE level. Now pool A broken → pool B broken = RN_CONT, with an
//      internal → external flag, RN_EXH when the run RAIDS the next pool
//      instead of breaking it, and RN_REV only after exhaustion AND an opposing
//      shift WITH displacement. This carries §11's hard rule: fading a run that
//      is still RN_CONT costs a grade, or is blocked.
//  E5 · CONGESTION FILTER (§29), from price action only (§39 forbids the
//      oscillator): compressed range · most bars overlapping the previous bar ·
//      almost no net travel for the distance covered · structure broken in BOTH
//      directions. Two of four = congested → one grade down, or blocked.
//  E6 · TRAP ENGINE (§15 / §16). v5.4's ① watch, promoted: the broken level must
//      be TRACKED liquidity (a registry pool actually broke — §16's "crowded,
//      obvious liquidity" is what separates a trap from a plain failed
//      breakout), and the trap is GRADED A/B/C from obvious liquidity,
//      breakout depth, reclaim speed, reclaim decisiveness and displacement.
//      A graded trap sets the producer's POI tier; it pays NO liquidity points.
//
// ── removed, with reasons (§40) ─────────────────────────────────────────────
//  R1  THE DYNAMIC TRENDLINE ENGINE (v5.4 MODULE 10 + PR_TL). Reasons: it is
//      not liquidity or PD-array logic; V5.3-C2 already established that it
//      carries no POI, therefore tier 3, therefore it CANNOT emit at the
//      default minTier = 2 — it was display-only in every shipped
//      configuration; and it is AUDIT-v5.md §9's own second removal lever. Its
//      two plotchars, its [LIQ] alert and its [SIGNAL] alert go with it.
//  R2  PRICE-ACTION PATTERNS ③ INSIDE BAR and ④ DOJI REVERSAL. Reasons: neither
//      contains any liquidity content — they are shape matching, which §39
//      rules out; both were tier 3 and equally unable to emit at defaults; and
//      ④ carried four rolling built-ins (math.sum, ta.sma, ta.highest,
//      ta.lowest) for a producer that could never fire. Their two [SIGNAL]
//      alerts go with them. ① and ② are KEPT — ① became the trap engine above,
//      and ② (shrinking wicks = absorption, a continuation) is kept for
//      backward compatibility and stays the weakest producer in the file.
//
// Kept intact: the machine, FEM, S/D retest, the registry, zones, the FVG
// lifecycle, breaker / mitigation flips, rejection blocks, EQH/EQL, dealing
// range + OTE, killzones, Silver Bullet, volatility windows, CISD, IFC, HTF
// POI, order-flow halves, session liquidity, opening prices, the dashboard, the
// TP ladder, and every remaining alert NAME (including ① and ②).
// Budget after Phase E: 57 / 64 outputs (was 58), 9 request.security instances
// (unchanged), token proxy +7 % on v5.4 — the removals paid for most of the
// build, and no new loop or security call was added.
//
// Every v4 feature is preserved except R1 / R2 above: zones, FVG lifecycle,
// rejection blocks, breaker / mitigation flips, EQH/EQL, dealing range + OTE,
// killzones, Silver Bullet, scheduled volatility windows, trendlines, CRT,
// turtle soup, CISD, IFC, HTF POI, FEM, order-flow halves, session liquidity,
// opening prices, the MTF dashboard, the TP ladder and all 37 alerts.
//
//
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v5.6 · LIQUIDITY-ONLY FORENSIC UPGRADE
//  A liquidity DETECTION + MARKING pass. Nothing outside the liquidity layer
//  was edited: not the setup machine, not the S/D or FVG lifecycles, not the
//  order / breaker / mitigation blocks, not CISD, not BOS / CHoCH / MSS, not
//  OTE, not the TP / SL ladder, not the sessions, not the HTF engine, not the
//  score, the grades, the gates, the dedupe, the dashboard or a single alert.
//
//  WHAT WAS ALREADY RIGHT (and was left alone). v5.5 already had a real pool
//  REGISTRY with a lifecycle (CREATED → VALIDATED → RAIDED → REACTION → DONE),
//  stable pool identity, raid-vs-break separation, raid grading A/B/C, one
//  event = one pool + descriptors, non-repainting pivot construction, session
//  and PDH/PDL pools, bindable-raid lookup and a liquidity-run engine. It
//  TRACKED liquidity well. It MARKED it barely at all.
//
//  L1  NO NOMENCLATURE ON THE CHART. A pool was an anonymous line: no BSL/SSL,
//      no level name, no strength, no internal/external, no lifecycle text.
//      The only two liquidity labels in the file were the "target BSL / SSL"
//      pair drawn on the last bar. → MODULE 8B, display-only.
//  L2  EQUALS ONLY EVER COMPARED THE IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING PIVOT. One slot per
//      side (lastPhVal / lastPlVal), so a triple top with an offset middle leg,
//      or a level re-tested two pivots later, produced NO EQ pool. → ring of
//      eqScanN pivots, each with its own reaction extreme; every matching leg
//      fused into ONE pool at their mean, tagged EQH×3 / EQL×4.
//  L3  NO PREVIOUS-WEEK LIQUIDITY AT ALL. The single most obvious external
//      stop pool on any intraday chart was unmarked. → PWH / PWL, read from
//      the last CLOSED weekly bar.
//  L4  CLUSTERING DESTROYED CLUSTER INFORMATION. f_poolPush refused or merged
//      any duplicate inside eqTol·ATR, so four stacked highs became one pool
//      that did not know it was four. → a confluence MASK on the pool (which
//      level TYPES resolved here) plus a last-bar grouping pass that names the
//      stop pool "BSL CLUSTER ×4" and boxes its price span.
//  L5  NO STRENGTH. touches and kind existed and were never combined, so a
//      lone minor pivot rendered exactly like a PDH sitting on an EQH.
//      → WEAK / MODERATE / STRONG / EXTREME, from kind, confluence, fused
//      legs, touches, stacking, external position and age.
//  L6  INTERNAL vs EXTERNAL WAS A KIND, NOT A POSITION. PK_EXT meant "an
//      extPiv pivot made this", which is a different statement and goes stale
//      the moment price trades away. → classified against the LIVE dealing
//      range, which is why the marking layer sits after MODULE 8.
//  L7  SWEPT LIQUIDITY STILL DREW AS RESTING. f_sesTrack's Asia / London / NY
//      lines are created with extend.right and never touched again, so a
//      session high taken hours ago still extended into the future — the one
//      place the chart contradicted the registry. → the session lines inherit
//      their pool's state (✓ SWEPT / ⇡ BROKEN) without f_sesTrack being edited.
//  L8  NO MARK FOR AN EXTERNAL RAID. Swing sweeps (◆), EQ raids (◈) and
//      session raids (▵▿) were marked; a PDH / PWH / external-swing raid — the
//      top of the priority ladder — was not. → ◇.
//  L9  THE CAP EVICTED THE OLDEST POOL. A previous-day high that had stood
//      untouched all session died before a three-bar-old minor swing.
//      → optional weakest-first eviction (lqPrunePri, default OFF).
//  L10 TWO HARD-CODED NUMBERS IN THE SWEEP TEST. The wick-vs-body rule was
//      close ≤ high − 0.5·range, with no absolute close-back leg, and the
//      equality tolerance was pure ATR with no tick floor for quantised
//      instruments. → raidRejFrac, raidBackAtr, eqTickMin; all defaulted to
//      the v5.5 values.
//
//  L11 THE TOKEN BUDGET. v5.6 first compiled to 103 094 tokens against a limit
//      of 100 256. The cause was not the new code volume: Pine INLINES a
//      user-defined function at every call site before counting, and f_poolPush
//      is a ~690-token body that v5.5 called FOURTEEN times — ~9 700 tokens for
//      one function, with every token added inside it costing fourteen. All
//      registrations now enqueue and the queue drains through ONE call site
//      immediately before the lifecycle scan (same calls, same order, nothing
//      in between reads the registry, so the behaviour is identical). v5.6 now
//      compiles LIGHTER than v5.5 despite everything it adds.
//  L12 THE BUDGET, SECOND PASS — AND A CORRECTION TO L11. At 102 129 tokens
//      the f_setupReset call sites inside f_setupAdvance (19 of them, in a
//      function instantiated twice) were collapsed to one `kill` flag and one
//      reset site. The count moved by THREE tokens. So the L11 theory is
//      wrong: TradingView's "compiled tokens" is not per-call-site inlining,
//      it is close to linear in SOURCE tokens (comments excluded). The L11
//      queue still helped — it removed fourteen 7-argument call sites — but
//      for the reason that they were source, not because f_poolPush was
//      copied. The kill flag is kept (it is the cleaner shape); the real
//      savings here are textual and behaviour-neutral:
//        · MODULE 18 dashboard rows go through one f_dRow helper instead of
//          two fully-spelled table.cell calls per row;
//        · MODULE 15's ten producer-common field writes (rq / kind / touches /
//          rev / mssCh / mssOk / dispBd / mssB / dispB / poolId) are written
//          once per side after the producer chain instead of in each of the
//          four non-machine blocks;
//        · f_poolScan's mirrored buy-side / sell-side branches are one branch
//          driven by a ±1 sign, with the comparisons that could differ by a
//          float ulp kept in their original per-side form;
//        · dead code removed: f_join / f_add (never called), emaTrend /
//          chartBias / the trendLen input (the Doji producer they served was
//          removed in R2), the fvgWin input (the machine uses FVG_OWN_MAX),
//          recentSsl / recentBsl / recentIBosUp / recentIBosDn / sigRisk
//          (write-only aliases).
//
//  DEFAULTS ARE v5.5-IDENTICAL. Everything in group ④a can change what the
//  REGISTRY contains, and the registry feeds the trade engine — so every one of
//  those inputs ships at the value that reproduces v5.5 exactly (eqScanN 1,
//  eqTickMin 0, raidRejFrac 0.5, raidBackAtr 0, lqPrunePri OFF) with ONE
//  exception, called out in its tooltip: showPW registers PWH/PWL as pools, and
//  a new pool kind is a new target and a new bindable raid. Turn showPW off for
//  output identical to v5.5. Group ④b is pure annotation and can never move a
//  signal.
//
//  NON-REPAINTING. No new lookahead. PWH/PWL uses the same construction as
//  PDH/PDL — the [1] is INSIDE the request, so only a COMPLETED weekly bar is
//  ever visible. Every new pool source is registered on a CONFIRMED bar from a
//  pivot that had already been confirmed pivLen bars earlier. The marking layer
//  reads state and draws; it never back-dates a classification.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v5.7 · ICT DETECTION + MARKING, INLINE (MODULE 16B) — DISPLAY-ONLY
//  Scope lock: nothing outside MODULE 16B was edited. No entry, exit, TP/SL,
//  liquidity, S/D, FVG, CRT, TBS, TWS, session, HTF, score, gate, grade, dedupe,
//  dashboard, plot or alert changed. MODULE 16B READS upS/dnS, zones and the
//  registry; it WRITES only labels and its own UDT.
//
//  WHY IT IS SMALL. v5.6 compiles at ~98 500 of TradingView's 100 256 compiled
//  tokens (measured 2026-09-09) and sits at the CE10295 global-scope cap. The
//  full ICT layer (~16 200 tokens) lives in 10.ict-forensic-layer-v1.pine as a
//  companion overlay. This inline module is the subset that fits the ~1 750
//  token headroom, chosen for ICT value per token:
//    ICT MSS ↑/↓   the machine's OWNED post-raid shift (state 4 → 5), i.e. a
//                  structure shift that happened AFTER a graded raid of the
//                  locked target — not any CHoCH. Chronology already enforced.
//    ICT SEQ       raid → MSS → displacement → FVG completed (state 7 lock),
//                  tagged with OTE when the lock candle sits in the range OTE.
//    ICT IFVG      an FVG zone that was closed through and flipped (MODULE 9's
//                  BREAKER / MITIGATION flip) — the inversion FVG, named as such.
//                  Needs showFlip = ON (a flipped-off zone is deleted instead).
//    ICT JUDAS     London / NY open: a registry-graded raid of a SESSION pool
//                  within ictJWin bars of the open, then a shift or displacement
//                  closing back through the raided level within ictJWin bars.
//    ICT AMD · PO3 the Judas, then a close through the OPPOSITE extreme of the
//                  raided session's range (accumulation → manipulation →
//                  distribution) within 4 × ictJWin bars of the raid.
//  Not included inline (budget): order block, BPR, raid-leg OTE, named raid
//  labels, displacement marks, PD-array-at-price — all in file 10.
//
//  NON-REPAINTING: every write is inside `confirmed`; every input it reads is
//  itself confirmed-bar state. No new request.security. No back-dating.
//  OBJECTS: labels only, created once per event on the confirming bar; they
//  rely on the same 500-label GC every existing chip uses. IFVG ids are held
//  in a 40-slot ring so no zone is tagged twice.
//  COMPILE RISK, stated plainly: this cannot be compiled locally. If TradingView
//  returns CE10117, delete the blocks marked ICT-CUT-1, then -2, then -3, in
//  that order — each is self-contained.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v5.8 · CRT / TBS / TWS FORENSIC MARKING (V5.8-CTT) — DISPLAY-ONLY
//  Scope lock: only CRT / TBS / TWS MARKING changed. The three V5.5-E state
//  machines (f_crtAdvance, f_tbsAdvance, f_twsAdvance), every cand* / SL /
//  score / gate / alert consumer, the HTF CRT/TBS producer and f_evTag are
//  byte-identical to v5.7. What changed:
//    CTT-1  TWS was confirmed (twsConfL/S) but NEVER drawn — only an event tag
//           and an alert. It is marked now: "TWS BUY" / "TWS SELL".
//    CTT-2  The "CRT tiny" / "tsoup tiny" chips printed a one-tick poke as a
//           pattern. CP_TINY no longer earns a CRT / TBS mark or a sweep chip;
//           it stays an event DESCRIPTOR (f_evTag), which is what §12 asks.
//    CTT-3  Vocabulary: "CRT BULL / CRT BEAR", "TBS BUY / TBS SELL",
//           "TWS BUY / TWS SELL", plus a quality tag (MEDIUM / HIGH / VERY HIGH).
//    CTT-4  Confluence: engines confirming on the same side within cttWin bars
//           share ONE label that is UPDATED to "CRT + TBS", "CRT + TWS",
//           "TBS + TWS" or "CRT + TBS + TWS" — no stacked chips, no duplicates.
//    CTT-5  The CRT reference range is drawn ONCE on confirmation as
//           CRT HIGH / CRT EQ / CRT LOW from the stored reference candle; it is
//           frozen at the sweep so it never moves. Capped at cttMaxRng sets,
//           oldest deleted first (own arrays, not the 500-object GC).
//  NON-REPAINTING: all reads are confirmed-bar lifecycle state, all writes are
//  inside `confirmed`. No new request.security. No back-dating of labels.
//  COMPILE: with MODULE 16B present TradingView returned CE10117 — 101 135
//  compiled tokens against the 100 256 cap (879 over; the prediction was
//  ~1 400). Step 1 of the cut order was applied: MODULE 16B (ICT-CUT-1/2/3,
//  ~1 500 tokens) is removed — the full ICT layer runs standalone as
//  10.ict-forensic-layer-v1.pine. Expected now ≈ 99 600. If it STILL fails:
//    2. delete CTT-CUT-1 (CRT HIGH / EQ / LOW lines, ~550);
//    3. delete CTT-CUT-2 (the whole marker) and restore the three v5.7 chips
//       from file 9 lines 3283-3294.
