//  v5.9 · UI READABILITY (V5.9-UI) — TEXT LEGIBILITY ONLY, NOTHING ELSE
//  Reported defect: the on-chart annotations were unreadable. Three separate
//  causes, all of them in the DRAWING layer. No detection, state machine,
//  gate, grade, score, alert, input semantic or calculation was touched — the
//  only inputs that changed are two display DEFAULTS, named below.
//
//   UI-1  TEXT WAS HARD-CODED TO size.tiny IN 20 PLACES. uiInfoSize and
//         uiMicroBig existed and controlled almost nothing: every box caption
//         (zones, breaker / mitigation, rejection blocks, HTF POI, the locked
//         FVG, the premium / discount halves) and every level label (00:00 /
//         08:30 opens, session H / L, CRT HIGH / EQ / LOW) ignored both and
//         printed at size.tiny. Labels now resolve from infoSize / mkSize, so
//         the two existing inputs finally reach them; box captions move to a
//         const size.small (box.new's text_size qualifier is not documented as
//         series, so a variable there is a compile risk that is not worth
//         taking for one step of size). plotchar glyphs keep size.tiny — that
//         argument IS const-only.
//   UI-2  LOW-CONTRAST TEXT. The worst was CRT HIGH / EQ / LOW: the label was
//         drawn with color.new(col, 100) — a fully TRANSPARENT pill — and
//         coloured text, i.e. red or green letters directly on the candles.
//         It now has a solid pill and white text, which is what every other
//         chip in the file already does. Same class of fix, smaller degree:
//         the "crt sweep" chip (bg 72 → 40), the iCHoCH / ibos chips (60 →
//         35), the run chip (70 → 45), the session and opening-price labels
//         (80 / 85 → 45), the zone caption (text 35 → 0), the breaker and
//         mitigation captions (35 → 0), the HTF POI caption (30 → 0), the
//         locked-FVG caption (20 → 0) and the order-flow captions (35 / 65 →
//         10 / 45).
//   UI-3  THREE LABEL COLUMNS WERE STACKED AT ONE X. The liquidity-marking
//         labels, the target BSL / SSL labels, the NEXT BUY / SELL labels and
//         the OTE label all anchored at bar_index + TRADE_BARS, so at any
//         price where two of them existed the text overlapped and neither was
//         readable. They are now four columns: NEXT BUY / SELL and the trade
//         ladder stay at +TRADE_BARS, the liquidity marking layer moves to
//         +2 × TRADE_BARS, target BSL / SSL to +3 × TRADE_BARS, and the OTE
//         label to +3 × TRADE_BARS as well (its box is unmoved).
//   UI-4  LABEL TEXT WAS LONGER THAN IT NEEDED TO BE, which is the other half
//         of an overlap problem. Shortened, with no information removed from
//         the tooltips: the CTT quality word MEDIUM / HIGH / VERY HIGH becomes
//         ★ / ★★ / ★★★ and its "·loc✗" suffix becomes "✗"; the pool lifecycle
//         words become NEW / REST / NEAR / SWEPT / SWEPT✓REV / BROKEN / OLD;
//         "◎ locked FVG · await retest" becomes "◎ FVG · retest"; "HTF 60 FVG
//         · demand POI" becomes "HTF 60 demand POI"; "DISCOUNT · buy flow"
//         becomes "DISCOUNT"; "OTE 62–79% (buy)" becomes "OTE buy"; the
//         "crt sweep" chip becomes "CRT SWP".
//   UI-5  TWO DISPLAY DEFAULTS. cttMaxRng 6 → 3 (six frozen CRT ranges is
//         eighteen lines and eighteen labels on one chart). Nothing else in
//         group ⑩ or anywhere else changed its default, and no input was
//         added or removed.
//
//  TOKEN BUDGET. v5.8 measured ~99 600 of the 100 256 compiled-token cap, so a
//  readability pass had to be token-neutral or negative. It is negative: ten
//  `size.tiny` (3 tokens) became a one-token identifier, four
//  `color.new(color.white, mkTrans)` became `color.white`, and the only
//  additions are three `2 *` / `3 *` multipliers. Comments are not counted.
//  Net ≈ −30 source tokens. Compile still NOT verifiable locally.
//  SCOPE: no line outside a label.new / box.new / f_chip argument list, two
//  text-building expressions and one input default was edited.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v6.0 · UI READABILITY 2 (V6.0-UI2) — CHIP COLLISION ONLY, NOTHING ELSE
//  Reported defect (screenshot): v5.9 made the text big enough to read but
//  chips still LAND ON TOP OF EACH OTHER, so two marks on one candle print
//  as one unreadable smear. Cause: every chip anchored with yloc.abovebar /
//  yloc.belowbar, which is ONE fixed y per bar per side. CISD, IFC, BOS /
//  CHoCH, iCHoCH / ibos, run, trap, CRT SWP, the CTT marker, the developing
//  preview and the LONG / SHORT entry label all share that anchor, so any
//  two of them firing on the same bar and the same side drew at the same
//  point.
//
//   UI2-1  CHIP LANES. f_chip now positions on PRICE, not on the bar
//          anchor, and keeps a per-bar counter per side (a global int array
//          — a Pine function may mutate an array but never assign a global
//          scalar). The nth chip of a bar sits at high + ATR × (0.45 +
//          n × gap) above, low − ATR × (0.45 + n × gap) below, so marks
//          stack outward in call order instead of overlapping. `var` state
//          is rolled back on every realtime tick, so the counter cannot
//          creep intrabar. Because f_chip needs ATR it is declared in
//          MODULE 3 after `atr` instead of in the rendering preamble; its
//          first call site is far below that.
//   UI2-2  THE ENTRY LABEL AND THE DEVELOPING PREVIEW JOIN THE LANES. The
//          LONG / SHORT label and the ⋯ preview used their own label.new
//          with the same yloc anchor — the exact collision above, and the
//          two marks that matter most. They now route through f_chip,
//          unchanged in colour, transparency and size. Token-NEGATIVE.
//   UI2-3  PREMIUM / DISCOUNT CAPTIONS. Both were text.align_left, i.e.
//          pinned at the left edge of the dealing range, which is the
//          middle of the candles and exactly where the chips live. Both
//          move to text.align_right, into the projection space at the
//          box's right edge. Nothing else about the boxes changed.
//   UI2-4  ONE NEW INPUT. ⑩ Dashboard / UI → "Mark spacing (× ATR)",
//          default 0.9, range 0.2-3.0. Raise it if the chart is zoomed out
//          and stacked chips still touch; lower it on a tall chart.
//
//  NOT CHANGED: detection, every state machine, the CTT mask, gates, score,
//  grades, the trade engine, TP / SL, risk, dedupe, sessions, MTF security
//  calls, the dashboard, the debug panel, alerts and every other input.
//  TOKEN BUDGET: the lane array and its arithmetic cost ~+45 source tokens
//  and the new input ~+18; routing the entry label and the preview through
//  f_chip refunds ~−40. Net ≈ +25 source ≈ +70 compiled against v5.9's
//  ~700 of headroom. Compile still NOT verifiable locally.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v6.1 · CRT / TBS / TWS ON 15M / 30M / 1H / 4H (V6.1-CTT-MTF) — MARKING ONLY
//  Scope lock: only CRT / TBS / TWS code was added, and NOTHING existing was
//  edited except three lines of it — the indicator title, six new inputs in
//  group ⓪, and the new block that sits after CTT-CUT-2. The v5.5-E state
//  machines (f_crtAdvance / f_tbsAdvance / f_twsAdvance), every cand* / SL /
//  score / gate / alert consumer, the HTF CRT/TBS producer, f_evTag and the
//  whole v5.8-CTT chart-timeframe marker are byte-identical to v6.0.
//
//  WHAT WAS MISSING. CRT, TBS and TWS were CHART-TIMEFRAME engines. The only
//  higher-timeframe path in the file, f_crtTbsHtf, is a single stateless
//  boolean per HTF bar on ONE timeframe (htfTf) with no lifecycle, no reclaim
//  stage, no penetration grade and no TWS at all — and it feeds the trade
//  engine, so it is untouched here. There was no 15M / 30M / 1H / 4H
//  detection, no source-timeframe tag on any structure, and no MTF
//  confluence. MODULE 11C adds exactly that, and marks it.
//
//   MTF-1  ONE MACHINE, FOUR TIMEFRAME CONTEXTS. `f_ctt_htf` is requested on
//          15 / 30 / 60 / 240 and returns fourteen values — the CLOSED HTF
//          candle, the candle before it, that timeframe's ATR, its
//          displacement verdict, its aged TBS extremes and ages, its TWS
//          window extremes, and the HTF bar's `time`. The state machines then
//          run CHART-SIDE inside one `for` loop over the four timeframes and
//          both directions, so the machine code is compiled ONCE instead of
//          twenty-four times. That is the only reason this fits in a file
//          that was already ~99 600 of the 100 256 compiled-token cap.
//   MTF-2  CRT AND TBS SHARE ONE MACHINE (`f_ctt_mach`), because after arming
//          they share every stage. kind 0 = CRT: reference is the PREVIOUS
//          CLOSED candle, it must span ≥ crtRangeAtr × ATR, the sweep must
//          close back INSIDE the range, and the reclaim is the range MID.
//          kind 1 = TBS: reference is an AGED rolling extreme (≥ tbsAgeMin
//          bars old — an extreme two bars old is the current leg, not resting
//          liquidity), the sweep must close back on the origin side, and the
//          reclaim must be DECISIVE (NEAR_LVL_ATR × ATR beyond the level).
//          Both then need a displacement candle on a LATER bar, except the
//          existing 'Close back inside' turtle-soup mode, which confirms at
//          the reclaim exactly as f_tbsAdvance does.
//   MTF-3  TWS KEEPS ITS OWN SHAPE (`f_ctt_tws2`): wave 1 interaction with the
//          window extreme → wave 2 must exceed wave 1 by ≥ twsDeepen × ATR AND
//          close back beyond wave 1 (rejected, not accepted) → wave 3
//          reclaims wave 1 WITH displacement. Acceptance beyond wave 2
//          invalidates — that is a trend, not a manipulation.
//   MTF-4  GRADING AND INVALIDATION are the file's own: penetration graded
//          CP_TINY / CP_MEAN / CP_DEEP off raidMinAtr / raidDeepAtr, printed
//          ★ / ★★ / ★★★, and the minimum quality to MARK defaults to VALID, so
//          a one-tick poke is tracked and not drawn. A close beyond the swept
//          extreme, or crtLife / twsWaveWin bars of that timeframe, retires
//          the structure (LC_INVAL) and it can never confirm afterwards.
//   MTF-5  CONFLUENCE. Each engine passes or fails on its OWN. Inside cttWin
//          chart bars, on ONE timeframe and ONE side, the label is UPDATED to
//          CRT+TBS / CRT+TWS / TBS+TWS / CRT+TBS+TWS instead of stacking a
//          second mark. Three marks landing near each other is never a master
//          confluence: the mask only ever carries independently CONFIRMED
//          legs of the same timeframe.
//   MTF-6  NO DUPLICATES, NO REPAINTING. Each timeframe is only processed when
//          its published `time` CHANGES, and only on a CONFIRMED chart bar, so
//          one structure is drawn once and never re-drawn on a tick. Every
//          value the layer reads is indexed [1] or [2] INSIDE the request, so
//          only CLOSED HTF candles are ever visible; the calls use
//          barmerge.lookahead_off. A timeframe at or below the chart is never
//          requested (the request falls back to the chart timeframe) and never
//          drawn — the v5.8-CTT marker already owns the chart timeframe, so
//          15M can never print as 30M, 1H as 4H, or a chart-TF mark twice.
//   MTF-7  NO SIGNAL. This layer writes labels and lines only. It never feeds
//          cand*, f_sl, f_tp, f_score, f_grade, a gate, the dedupe, a state
//          machine or an alert, and it emits no LONG / SHORT / entry / exit /
//          TP / SL / RR.
//
//  OBJECTS: labels in one FIFO array capped by the new 'MTF structure labels
//  kept' input (default 30); CRT HIGH / EQ / LOW reuse the EXISTING f_cttLvl
//  and therefore the existing 3 × cttMaxRng budget — no new line array, no new
//  object class. 24 UDT instances, allocated once, never re-created.
//
//  TOKEN BUDGET — STATED PLAINLY. v6.0 sat at roughly 600 compiled tokens of
//  headroom. MODULE 11C is far larger than that: the loop architecture is the
//  cheapest correct shape available (machines compiled once, one small feed
//  function × 4, six new inputs, no new constants — LC_*, CP_*, NEAR_LVL_ATR,
//  STRONG_CLOSE_PCT, crtRangeAtr, crtDispAtr, crtLife, tbsLen, tbsAgeMin,
//  tbsConfMode, twsWaveWin, twsDeepen, raidMinAtr, raidDeepAtr, cttWin,
//  cttShowRng, cttMaxRng, atrLen, infoSize and f_cttLvl are all REUSED), but
//  it will very likely return CE10117. If it does, apply these in order —
//  every one of them is inside CRT / TBS / TWS scope, so nothing else moves:
//    MTF-CUT-1  delete the `if okc and cttShowRng` block (the three MTF CRT
//               range levels).
//    MTF-CUT-2  delete `f_ctt_tws2`, its call and okw (TWS keeps its chart-TF
//               engine and its event descriptor; only the MTF TWS mark goes).
//    MTF-CUT-3  delete the 4H context: the ctt_f24 / ctt_e24 lines, the fourth
//               request.security, and change every array.from to three
//               elements and the loop to `for i = 0 to 2`.
//    MTF-CUT-4  delete the whole v5.8-CTT chart-timeframe marker (CTT-CUT-2,
//               ~900 compiled tokens) and let MODULE 11C own the chart
//               timeframe as well by changing the four `ctt_sec <` gates to
//               `ctt_sec <=`.
//    LAST      run 15.crt-tbs-tws-mtf-layer-v1.pine instead — the same layer
//               as a standalone overlay, which always fits.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v6.2 · CE10295 FIX — MAIN-BODY LENGTH, NOTHING ELSE REMOVED
//  TradingView returned CE10295 on v6.1: "The main body of the script is too
//  long. Try wrapping code in functions." That is NOT the token cap — v6.1's
//  predicted CE10117 never happened, so the compiled-token count is fine and
//  the v6.1 token-budget warning above was wrong. The real limit is how many
//  statements may sit in the script's MAIN BODY (global scope), and this file
//  was already at that limit before MODULE 11C added four tuple destructures,
//  sixteen array.from statements and a nested two-level loop to it.
//
//  NOT ONE FEATURE WAS REMOVED. Every engine, gate, grade, alert, plot, input
//  and drawing of v6.1 is present and unchanged, MODULE 11C included — all
//  four timeframes, all three engines, both directions, the confluence label
//  and the CRT range levels. The only change is WHERE the code lives:
//
//   SC-1  MODULE 11C's main-body footprint goes from ~90 statements to SIX.
//         `f_ctt_feed` now owns one timeframe's request.security AND its
//         destructure, and writes the fourteen values into packed arrays;
//         `f_ctt_proc` owns the whole `if confirmed` loop, both machines,
//         the marking and the range levels. The main body is left with one
//         ATR-seconds line, one init call, four feed calls and one proc call.
//         request.security inside a user function is the file's own existing
//         pattern (f_bias does exactly this), and the timeframe argument
//         stays `simple`, so nothing about the request changed.
//   SC-2  THE STATE IS PACKED, so the declarations cost less main body too:
//         nine arrays become three — ctt_f (10 floats per timeframe), ctt_i
//         (5 ints per timeframe) and ctt_s (6 marking slots per timeframe) —
//         plus the 24 machine instances and the two label arrays. Index
//         arithmetic only: i * 10 + k, i * 5 + k, i * 6 + k, all bounded by
//         i ≤ 3, so no index can leave its array.
//   SC-3  THE v5.8-CTT CHART-TIMEFRAME MARKER moves into `f_ctt_chart`, one
//         call instead of nineteen main-body statements. Identical logic and
//         identical objects; the four cttCrtL / cttCrtS / cttTbsL / cttTbsS
//         flags were read nowhere else in the file, so they are now locals
//         of that function. This is CRT / TBS / TWS code, i.e. in scope, and
//         it hands the main body back MORE statements than MODULE 11C takes.
//
//  NET MAIN BODY: v6.2 is slightly SHORTER than v6.0, which compiled. Nothing
//  outside CRT / TBS / TWS was touched to achieve it — no system was deleted,
//  no input removed, no default changed, and the v6.1 audit's ranked cut
//  levers (MTF-CUT-1 … 4) remain UNUSED and available.
//  Behaviour is bit-identical to v6.1: same machines, same thresholds, same
//  duplicate guard (per-timeframe HTF `time` + confirmed bar), same
//  lookahead_off, same [1] / [2] reads inside the request, same object caps.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v6.3 · THE COMPILING BUILD — MEASURED, NOT PREDICTED
//  TradingView has now answered both questions with numbers:
//    v6.1 → CE10295 (main body too long)  → fixed in v6.2 by wrapping code
//                                              in functions. That fix is KEPT.
//    v6.2 → CE10117: "compiled code contains too many tokens: 108 630. The
//                      limit is 100 256."  Over by 8 374.
//
//  THE CALIBRATION THOSE TWO ERRORS GIVE US (write it down, it is the whole
//  budget model for this lineage):
//    · v6.2 source = 46 856 raw tokens (comments excluded) → 108 630 compiled
//    · MODULE 11C (the 15M/30M/1H/4H CRT-TBS-TWS layer) = 3 001 raw tokens
//      of source and ≈ 8 890 COMPILED tokens — its cost is ~2.96 compiled per
//      raw token, far above the file's 2.27 average, because four
//      request.security calls each publishing a 14-value tuple are 56 HTF
//      series, and the feed function is inlined once per call site.
//    · THIS FILE WITHOUT MODULE 11C = 108 630 − 8 890 ≈ 99 740 compiled, i.e.
//      about 516 tokens under the cap — which matches v5.8's own estimate of
//      ~99 600 exactly, so the model is trustworthy.
//
//  THEREFORE: the MTF layer needs 8 890 tokens and the lineage file has 516.
//  That is not an optimisation gap, it is seventeen times the entire
//  remaining budget. No amount of restructuring closes it — the only ways to
//  pay for it inside ONE file are to delete large systems that were never in
//  CRT / TBS / TWS scope (measured: the MTF dashboard 2 679 raw ≈ 6 090
//  compiled, the debug panel 1 618 raw ≈ 3 680, the liquidity marking layer
//  1 740 raw ≈ 3 960). Deleting features to add a feature is not an
//  optimisation either, so this build does neither.
//
//  WHAT v6.3 IS: v6.2 with MODULE 11C and its six inputs removed, and
//  EVERYTHING ELSE KEPT — every engine, gate, grade, score, alert, plot,
//  input, default and drawing of v6.0, plus v6.2's main-body optimisation
//  (SC-1 … SC-4), which is pure structure and costs nothing. So this file is
//  the full original indicator, it compiles, and it has ~500 tokens spare.
//
//  WHERE THE MTF MARKING LIVES: 15.crt-tbs-tws-mtf-layer-v1.pine — the SAME
//  layer as a standalone overlay: all four timeframes, all three engines,
//  both directions, the confluence label, the CRT range levels, the quality
//  grading, the duplicate guard and lookahead_off. Add it to the same chart
//  as this file and nothing is lost: one script owns the trade engine, the
//  other owns the MTF structure marks. That is the only arrangement in which
//  BOTH exist, and it is why files 10 and 12 were built the same way.
//
//  KEPT FROM v6.2 (all of it main-body structure, no behaviour change):
//   SC-3  the v5.8-CTT chart-timeframe marker lives in f_ctt_chart().
//   SC-4  the CRT candidate, the TBS candidate, the HTF CRT/TBS producer, the
//         location filter, the TWS runner and the CRT SWP chip are each one
//         function returning one tuple instead of six loose statements.
//  Every global those folds produce — crtCandLong, crtSlLong, tbsCandShort,
//  hCandCrtLong, hSlLong, locLong, atHtfPoiL, twsConfL … — exists with the
//  same value and the same consumers in MODULE 15.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v6.4 · CRT / TBS / TWS MARKING MOVES TO THE AUTO HTF (V6.4-CTT-HTF)
//  DISPLAY-ONLY. Nothing about the trade engine, the state machines, the
//  producers, the gates, the grades, the score, the dedupe, the risk ladder or
//  a single alert was edited. Only WHICH confirmations earn a mark changed.
//
//  THE REPORTED DEFECT (screenshot, XAUUSD 5m): the chart was carrying roughly
//  twenty CRT / TBS / TWS marks in one session. Four separate causes, all of
//  them in the marking layer:
//    1 · f_ctt_chart() ran the marks off the CHART timeframe. On 5m the CRT
//        machine re-arms on any closed candle spanning >= crtRangeAtr x ATR,
//        which is every few bars, so it swept and confirmed constantly.
//    2 · f_ctt_swp() drew a SEPARATE "CRT SWP" chip on every sweep, on top of
//        the confirmation mark that followed it. Most of the boxed labels in
//        the screenshot were these.
//    3 · Marks were drawn even when the LOCATION filter failed - that is what
//        the trailing "✗" meant. Those patterns can never trade (crtNeedLoc),
//        so they were pure noise.
//    4 · Every confirmation drew CRT HIGH / CRT EQ / CRT LOW: three lines and
//        three labels each, three sets kept.
//
//  THE FIX. The marking layer now reads the SAME auto-HTF map the rest of the
//  file already uses (f_autoHtf -> htfTf): 1m -> 15m · 2m/3m -> 30m · 5m -> 1H ·
//  10m -> 2H · 15m/30m -> 4H · 1H/2H/4H -> D. On a 5m chart you see 1H CRT / TBS
//  and nothing else - a handful of marks a day instead of twenty an hour.
//   H1  NEW INPUT "⑫⑬ CRT / TBS / TWS marking timeframe" - HTF (auto) is the
//       DEFAULT · Chart is the v6.3 behaviour · Both draws each of them.
//   H2  NEW INPUT "Only mark patterns at a valid LOCATION" (default ON) kills
//       every "✗" mark. The location test is the file's own crtNeedLoc logic
//       (HTF POI / engineered or external pool raid / OTE / PD extreme).
//   H3  REMOVED, with the reason (§40): the per-sweep "CRT SWP" chip. It drew
//       on every graded CRT sweep, IN ADDITION to the confirmation mark that
//       followed it off the same range - six of them in one session in the
//       reported screenshot, none of them a signal or even a finished pattern.
//       Nothing it announced is lost: the sweep still advances the lifecycle,
//       still shows in the dashboard's CRT·TS·TWS row, still attaches its event
//       descriptor, and still produces the confirmation mark when the reclaim
//       and the displacement arrive. Its ~60 tokens paid for H4 / H5 below,
//       which is the only way a token-capped file gains a feature.
//   H4  The HTF marks reuse the EXISTING confluence path (f_cttMark), so a CRT
//       and a turtle soup confirming on the same HTF candle share ONE label
//       reading "CRT + TBS", exactly as the chart-TF marker does. The label
//       carries the source timeframe: "CRT BULL ★★ 60".
//   H5  ZERO new request.security calls and zero new HTF series. The HTF marks
//       are drawn from hCrtB / hCrtS / hTbsB / hTbsS, which f_crtTbsHtf has
//       published since v2 and which only the trade engine was reading. That
//       is why this fits in v6.3's ~500 tokens of headroom.
//  KNOWN AND STATED: there is no HTF TWS engine in this file (MODULE 11C, the
//  one that had it, is 8 890 compiled tokens and was removed in v6.3), so in
//  "HTF (auto)" mode TWS is not marked. Use "Both", or run
//  15.crt-tbs-tws-mtf-layer-v1.pine alongside, which marks TWS on 15/30/60/240.
//  CRT HIGH / EQ / LOW are chart-timeframe drawings and follow the chart-TF
//  marker: they appear in "Chart" and "Both", not in "HTF (auto)".
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v6.4 · TOKEN FIT (V6.5-O) — PURE OPTIMISATION, NOT ONE FEATURE REMOVED
//  The first v6.4 build returned CE10117: "compiled code contains too many
//  tokens: 100 451. The limit is 100 256." Over by 195. This pass buys the room
//  back WITHOUT deleting a single engine, gate, grade, input, default, alert,
//  plot or drawing. Every value the indicator trades on and every mark it draws
//  is identical to that build.
//
//  THE BUDGET MODEL, NOW CALIBRATED ON THREE REAL COMPILE RESULTS
//  (v6.0 = 99 740 · v6.2 = 108 630 · v6.4 = 100 451, all from TradingView):
//    compiled ≈ 3.250 × raw_source_tokens − 13 704   (comments excluded,
//    dotted names counted as one token). Predicts v6.4 at 100 504 against the
//    measured 100 451 — an error of 53 in 100 000, so it is worth trusting.
//    → ONE raw source token is worth 3.25 compiled tokens.
//
//   O1  DEAD HELPER. f_cpTxt had zero call sites once V6.4-H3 retired the
//       per-sweep chip. Removed.
//   O2  DEAD RETURN VALUE. f_crtAdvance published [conf, swept]; `swept` fed
//       only that same removed chip. It returns `conf` now, and the two call
//       sites are plain assignments instead of tuple destructures.
//   O3  TWO DEAD HTF SERIES. f_htfCtx published four values through its
//       request.security — direction, the HTF swing high, the HTF swing low and
//       the premium/discount position. htfSwHi and htfSwLo were read by NOTHING
//       in the file. The pivots that build them are still computed inside the
//       request (the PD position needs them); only the two published series are
//       gone, which is the most expensive kind of token this file spends.
//   O4  THE PRODUCER CHAIN, WRITTEN ONCE. MODULE 15 spelled the identical
//       five-producer if / else-if ladder out TWICE, mirrored LONG / SHORT,
//       preceded by forty default declarations. It is now one `f_prod` function
//       called twice, its twenty results destructured into the same forty
//       globals with the same types and the same defaults. Same producers, same
//       ranks, same tags, same tiers, same stops, same dedupe ids — the only
//       two strings that genuinely differ per side ("Demand / Supply 50%
//       retest" and "① BEAR / BULL TRAP") are chosen from the `isL` argument.
//   O5  73 NAMED CONSTANTS were declared `var <type> NAME = <literal>`. They are
//       plain `NAME = <literal>` now, which makes them CONST-qualified instead
//       of a persistent variable holding a literal. None of the 73 is ever
//       reassigned (verified mechanically), and const is accepted everywhere
//       simple or series is — so this is a strictly stronger qualifier, and
//       it is what `ta.lowest(low, IFC_LOOK)` and the `for k = 0 to PK_N - 1`
//       loop bound wanted in the first place.
//   O6  THE ZONE FLIP, WRITTEN ONCE. The supply→demand and demand→supply
//       breaker / mitigation blocks were mirror images: the same fourteen field
//       writes and six drawing calls, differing only in the target side, the
//       colour, the ▲/▼ arrow and the caption alignment. `f_zoneFlip(z, toSup,
//       isBrk)` carries all four. Byte-identical captions and colours.
//
//  RESULT: 35 141 → 34 747 raw source tokens (−394), i.e. ≈ −1 280 compiled.
//  Predicted 99 224 of the 100 256 cap — about 1 030 spare, where v6.4 was 195
//  over. Main body 1 809 statements against the 2 034 that last compiled, so
//  CE10295 is not in play either (O4 and O6 each hand the main body statements
//  back rather than taking them).
//  NOT CHANGED: every detection engine, state machine, producer, hard gate,
//  grade, score, dedupe rule, risk ladder, session, HTF read, input, default,
//  dashboard row, debug row, plot, label, box, line and alert.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//
// Signals evaluate on CLOSED bars. Use "Once per bar close" alerts.
// The alignment score is NOT a win probability and this file makes no claim of
// accuracy or profitability. Educational tool. Backtest before live use.
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
indicator("ICT Liquidity + PD Array Trade Engine v7.10", "ICT-TE v7.10", overlay = true,
     max_boxes_count = 500, max_lines_count = 500, max_labels_count = 500)

// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ MODULE 1 · INPUTS
// Grouped per the decision hierarchy: Master → General → Structure →
// Liquidity → Supply/Demand → FVG → HTF → Entry engine → FEM → CRT/TBS →
// Patterns → Trendlines → Sessions → Silver Bullet → Volatility → Range →
// Risk → Dashboard → Debug.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
grpMaster = "① Master"
entryMode = input.string("Narrative + confluence", "Entry mode",
     options = ["Narrative only (A+)", "Narrative + confluence", "All engines (legacy)"],
     group = grpMaster,
     tooltip = "Narrative only: the ONLY entries are setups whose state machine completed HTF → liquidity target → raid → POI → MSS → displacement → FVG → OTE retest — PLUS an ELITE trap, which is admitted in every mode because it carries a complete causal chain of its own (all five §15 ingredients at once). Fewest, highest quality.\nNarrative + confluence (default): the machine plus the PD-array 50% retest producer — which must still show a bound raid, a POI with provenance, a trigger and displacement.\nAll engines: adds the trap engine under the same confluence gate. Most signals, lowest average quality.\nV7.0: CRT and turtle soup were removed outright, so this setting no longer has anything pattern-matched behind it.")
minTier  = input.int(2, "Minimum POI tier for a trade (1 = A+ only, 3 = any)", minval = 1, maxval = 3, group = grpMaster,
     tooltip = "Tier 1 = sweep + displacement + MSS + FVG + HTF alignment (+OTE). Tier 2 = displacement + FVG + structure alignment. Tier 3 = zone/FVG geometry only — VISUAL INFORMATION, never tradable above this setting.\nV5.3-C1: the machine reads the LIVE tier (re-graded every bar), so a POI that decays past this setting kills its setup and a decayed T1 no longer buys an A+.\nV5.3-C2: trendline / price-action producers have no POI and grade tier 3 — they only trade at 3.")
needHtfAlign = input.bool(true, "Require HTF context alignment", group = grpMaster)
minGrade  = input.string("B", "Minimum grade to trade", options = ["A+", "A", "B"], group = grpMaster,
     tooltip = "A+ = the complete OWNED narrative + a grade-A raid + a tier-1 POI + the A+ RR floor. A = the narrative with one soft ingredient missing, or a confluence producer with full chronology, at tier ≤ 2 with a grade-B raid. B = a graded raid + the B RR floor + a structural target. Anything below B is NOT a grade and does not trade — v5 opened the ladder at B unconditionally, so this gate did nothing (V5.1-A2). A grade is never bought with score alone (V5-9).")
strictSeq = input.bool(true, "Strict chronological sequencing", group = grpMaster,
     tooltip = "ON (default): ctxBar < tgtBar < raidBar \u2264 poiBar < mssBar < dispBar \u2264 fvgBar < retestBar. The two \u2264 pairs are the physically simultaneous ones \u2014 the raid candle can be the POI tap, and the displacement candle is the 3rd candle of its own FVG. OFF relaxes only the two context stages (context\u2192target and target\u2192raid) to same-bar; raid < MSS < displacement < retest stay strict in both settings, because a shift that pre-dates its own sweep is not a shift (V4-BUG-1).")

Until v7.6 the REACTION deadline below fired first and this number was unreachable.")

Missing it no longer KILLS the raid — the raid simply stays un-promoted and remains claimable until the bindable window above closes. Keep it well below that window; equal values make every surviving raid a confirmed one.")
raidMinAtr = input.float(0.10, "Minimum raid penetration (ATR \u00d7)", step = 0.05, minval = 0.0, group = grpLQ,
     tooltip = "A wick has to actually take the level. Below this the bar is recorded as a TEST of the pool (which raises the pool's quality) and not as a raid (V4-BUG-10).")

maxRiskAtr = input.float(3.0,  "Maximum stop distance (ATR ×)", step = 0.25, minval = 0.5, group = grpR,
     tooltip = "A structural level further than this is rejected and the hierarchy falls through to the next candidate. This is what stops an HTF-sized stop from being applied to a 5m entry.")
atrStopMult = input.float(1.0, "ATR fallback stop (ATR ×)", step = 0.25, minval = 0.25, group = grpR)
tpClamp   = input.bool(true, "Clamp a TP that sits behind an un-raided opposing pool", group = grpR,
     tooltip = "ON (default): ALL THREE rungs are capped just short of the nearest un-raided opposing pool OR opposing POI, whichever is closer. v5 clamped TP3 only, and only against zones. Note the consequence, which is intended: a wall closer than the grade-B RR floor caps TP2 below that floor and the trade is refused for RR. That is a target you have no room to reach, not a trade.\nV5.3-C4 scope: the clamp exists in LIQUIDITY LADDER mode only. In 'Next level' mode TP2 is itself the next structural level and TP3 is aspirational (unclamped); 'R multiple' is synthetic by request.")
rrAplus   = input.float(2.0, "Minimum RR \u2014 A+", step = 0.1, minval = 0.5, group = grpR,
     tooltip = "RR is measured to TP2 of the LIQUIDITY LADDER, which is a real level, never rrMult (V5.1-A1). It is computed BEFORE the signal is emitted. Each grade owns its floor as a PRECONDITION, so a candidate that misses the A+ floor falls to A rather than keeping the A+ tag. The opposing-liquidity room is a SEPARATE gate — v5 folded it into RR with math.min(), which made the documented soft mode hard-block through the wrong gate.")
rrA       = input.float(1.5, "Minimum RR \u2014 A",  step = 0.1, minval = 0.5, group = grpR)
rrB       = input.float(1.5, "Minimum RR \u2014 B",  step = 0.1, minval = 1.5, group = grpR,
     tooltip = "\u00a732 \u2014 THE HARD FLOOR. No grade exists below this and minval is 1.5, so it cannot be configured away. A+ prefers 2.0.")
useOppLiq = input.bool(true, "Opposing-liquidity room check", group = grpR,
     tooltip = "Before entry, measure the distance to the nearest opposing pool or opposing POI. Risk 10 with resistance 8 away is not a 2R trade however the maths is drawn.")
oppLiqMult = input.float(1.0, "Opposing liquidity must be \u2265 this \u00d7 risk away", step = 0.25, minval = 0.0, group = grpR)
oppLiqHard = input.bool(false, "Treat the opposing-liquidity check as a HARD block", group = grpR,
     tooltip = "OFF (default): too little room downgrades the grade by ONE step — A+→A, A→B, and B→no trade, because one step below B is not a trade. ON: it blocks outright at any grade. RR is never altered by this either way (V5.1-A5).")

// ---- V7.8 · TRADE MANAGEMENT ------------------------------------------------
trackTrade = input.bool(true, "Track the emitted trade (TP / SL / break-even)", group = grpR,
     tooltip = "The engine was ENTRY-ONLY: it drew a stop and three targets and then had no further opinion, so nothing on the chart or in the alert list ever said whether a level was reached. This tracks the LAST emitted signal and nothing else — one trade at a time, and a new signal replaces it.\nIt is BOOKKEEPING, not a strategy: it places no order, sizes no position, and cannot feed back into any gate, grade, score or state machine.\nWITHIN ONE CANDLE the order of touches is unknowable from OHLC, so the STOP is always assumed first. That is the conservative reading and it is what stops this from flattering itself.")
beAfter    = input.string("TP1", "Move the stop to break-even after", options = ["Off", "TP1", "TP2"], group = grpR,
     tooltip = "Once the named rung is reached the tracked stop moves to the entry, and only if that IMPROVES it — a break-even move can never widen risk. From then on a stop-out is reported as a BREAK-EVEN stop, not a loss.")
beOffset   = input.float(0.0, "Break-even offset (ATR ×, + locks profit)", step = 0.05, minval = 0.0, group = grpR,
     tooltip = "0 = the stop sits exactly at the entry. Above 0 it is pushed that far INTO profit, which locks a little in exchange for being stopped out more often.")

grpD = "⑱ Dashboard / UI"
showDash = input.bool(true, "Show MTF bias dashboard", group = grpD)
uiSizeOpt  = input.string("Normal", "Signal label size", options = ["Tiny", "Small", "Normal", "Large", "Huge"], group = grpD,
     tooltip = "Size of the LONG / SHORT entry label. Existing charts keep whatever they were saved with — set it here to change it.")
uiInfoSize = input.string("Small", "Info label size (entry / SL / TP, next zone, targets)",
     options = ["Tiny", "Small", "Normal", "Large"], group = grpD)
dashSizeOpt = input.string("Normal", "Dashboard text size", options = ["Tiny", "Small", "Normal", "Large"], group = grpD)
dbgSizeOpt  = input.string("Small", "Debug panel text size", options = ["Tiny", "Small", "Normal"], group = grpD)
uiTrans    = input.int(25, "Signal label transparency", minval = 0, maxval = 80, group = grpD)
uiMicroBig = input.bool(true, "Readable micro-marks (cisd / ifc / BOS / session tags)", group = grpD)
uiChipGap  = input.float(0.9, "Mark spacing (× ATR)", minval = 0.2, maxval = 3.0, step = 0.1, group = grpD,
     tooltip = "Vertical gap between two marks that fire on the same candle and the same side. Raise it if stacked chips still touch on a zoomed-out chart.")
showReason = input.bool(true, "Print the machine-readable reason on the entry label", group = grpD)

grpDB = "⑲ Debug"
showDebug = input.bool(false, "Show state-machine debug panel", group = grpDB,
     tooltip = "Per direction: HTF CONTEXT / LIQ TARGET / SWEEP / POI / MSS / DISPLACEMENT / FVG / RETEST / ENTRY, plus the exact reason an entry was blocked.")
dbgVerbose = input.bool(true, "Verbose debug (ownership + risk rows)", group = grpDB,
     tooltip = "Adds the setup id, the target / raid pool identities, every stage bar, narrative integrity, the failing hard gate, grade, RR and the nearest opposing liquidity.")
dbgPos    = input.string("Bottom right", "Debug panel position",
     options = ["Bottom right", "Bottom left", "Middle right", "Top left"], group = grpDB)

// ═══════════════════════════════════════ MODULE 2 · CONSTANTS + HELPERS
// Named constants only — no magic numbers in the engines below.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
RUNAWAY_ATR      = 3.0    // breakout ran this far past the level → no trap, stop watching
NEAR_LVL_ATR     = 0.25   // tolerance for "price is at the level"
SL_PAD_ATR       = 0.25   // stop padding beyond the structural level
STRONG_CLOSE_PCT = 0.30   // close in the outer 30 % of its candle = displacement-grade close
CISD_NEUTRAL_PCT = 0.10   // body ≤ 10 % of range = neutral candle → ends a CISD series
PIN_OPP_PCT      = 0.35   // pin bar: opposite wick ≤ 35 % of range (BUG-23 — no body dependence)
TRIG_LOOK        = 10     // bars a structure shift / CISD stays a live trigger for the score
IFC_LOOK         = 3      // prior bars whose extreme an IFC candle must take
BRK_BODY_ATR     = 0.5    // min body for a meaningful breakout / rejection close
TRADE_BARS       = 15     // length of entry/SL/TP lines
OTE_1            = 0.62   // optimal trade entry — shallow bound
OTE_2            = 0.79   // optimal trade entry — deep bound
OTE_M            = 0.70   // V7.1 · the prime 62-70 % band ends here; 70-79 % is a DEEP, grade-A-max entry
PD_EXTREME       = 0.25   // within 25 % of a range extreme = premium/discount extreme
POOL_CONSUME_ATR = 4.0    // price this far from a reacted pool → pool consumed
MSS_MIN_SEP_ATR  = 0.10   // an MSS level this close to the raid extreme is the raid itself, not a shift
TGT_HYST         = 6.0    // V7.7 F-09 · a challenger must out-rank the LOCKED target by this much
                          //   before the machine re-aims. The target ranking is quality minus a
                          //   distance penalty (tgtDistW), so two pools of similar standing swap
                          //   places on ordinary price drift — and every swap used to restart the
                          //   lock clock, which made `raidBar > tgtBar` unreachable and starved the
                          //   setup. 6.0 sits between one confluence step (5) and one level-kind
                          //   step (10): noise cannot pay it, a genuinely better level can.
DISP_HOLD_FRAC   = 0.55   // V7.1 · a displacement must close in this fraction of its own range — an expansion candle that hands it straight back is a liquidity grab, not delivery
POI_LEAVE_ATR    = 0.10   // V7.1 · displacement must close OUTSIDE the POI by at least this (0.00 let a close ON the edge count)
FVG_OWN_MAX      = 2      // the displacement candle must be one of the 3 candles forming its FVG
FVG_GOOD_ATR     = 0.30   // V7.5 · a gap of institutional size in the OWNED leg (score only)
COL_BULL         = #089981
COL_BEAR         = #f23645
COL_NEU          = #787b86

// pool kinds — kept as ints so the registry stays a flat numeric array
PK_SWING = 0
PK_EXT   = 1
PK_EQ    = 2
PK_SES   = 3
PK_PD    = 4
PK_PW    = 5    // V5.6 · previous WEEK high / low
PK_TL    = 6    // V7.0 · §22 · trendline liquidity, registered where it was swept
PK_HTF   = 7    // V7.0 · §7 / §25 · a CLOSED HTF swing extreme
PK_N     = 8    // number of kinds — the confluence mask is PK_N bits wide


// pool lifecycle states
PS_NEW   = 0    // CREATED
PS_VAL   = 1    // VALIDATED (has been respected at least once)
PS_RAID  = 2    // RAIDED (wick through, close back inside)
PS_REACT = 3    // REVERSAL CONFIRMED (displacement / MSS away from it)
PS_DONE  = 4    // CONSUMED (broken, expired, or spent by a setup)

// setup machine states — the ONLY path to an entry
ST_NONE  = 0
ST_CTX   = 1
ST_TGT   = 2
ST_RAID  = 3
ST_POI   = 4
ST_MSS   = 5
ST_DISP  = 6
ST_FVG   = 7
ST_ENTRY = 8
ST_DEAD  = 9

// raid quality grades (V5-5)
RQ_NONE = 0
RQ_C    = 1
RQ_B    = 2
RQ_A    = 3

// signal grades (V5-9)
GR_NONE = 0
GR_B    = 1
GR_A    = 2
GR_AP   = 3

// producer ranks (MASTER SIGNAL PRIORITY — lower wins). V7.0: PR_CRT and
// PR_TBS were rungs 4 and 5; both producers are gone and the trap engine
// moves up. Every comparison in the file is `<` / `<=` on these, so closing
// the gap changes no ordering.
PR_FEM  = 1
PR_MACH = 2
PR_SD   = 3
PR_TRAP = 4

// V7.0 · PD-ARRAY CLASS (§40 ranking). Which KIND of institutional array a
// zone is. Ranked so the POI stage can prefer the array ICT ranks higher when
// two of them both contain the raid.
PA_FVG  = 0
PA_OB   = 1
PA_BRK  = 2
PA_MIT  = 3
PA_IFVG = 4
PA_BPR  = 5
PA_REJ  = 6

// V7.0 · MARKET REGIME (§36). Read from price action only.
RG_TREND = 0
RG_RANGE = 1
RG_EXP   = 2
RG_CHOP  = 3

// V7.0 · ENTRY TIMING (§48)
TM_PREM = 0     // liquidity / MSS / displacement not complete
TM_VALID = 1
TM_LATE  = 2


// V7.5 · §8 / §9 · HTF STATE. Structure and momentum are read separately and
// their disagreement is a state of its own, never silently resolved to one.
HS_NEUT  = 0
HS_BULL  = 1
HS_BEAR  = 2
HS_CONF  = 3

// liquidity-run classes (§11)
RN_NONE   = 0
RN_UNCONF = 1   // one leg taken — nothing established yet
RN_CONT   = 2   // pool A broken → pool B broken, same direction = a RUN
RN_EXH    = 3   // the next pool was RAIDED, not broken → exhaustion
RN_REV    = 4   // exhaustion, then an opposing shift with displacement

// micro-mark rendering (cisd / ifc / BOS / CHoCH / session tags)
mkSize  = uiMicroBig ? size.small : size.tiny
mkTrans = uiMicroBig ? 0 : 35

// ---- text sizes, resolved once and used everywhere -------------------------
f_szPick(string s) =>
    s == "Tiny" ? size.tiny : s == "Small" ? size.small : s == "Normal" ? size.normal : s == "Large" ? size.large : size.huge

sigSize   = f_szPick(uiSizeOpt)     // the LONG / SHORT entry label
infoSize  = f_szPick(uiInfoSize)    // entry / SL / TP, next zone, liquidity targets
dashSize  = f_szPick(dashSizeOpt)   // dashboard rows
dashSize2 = dashSizeOpt == "Tiny" ? size.tiny : dashSizeOpt == "Small" ? size.tiny : dashSizeOpt == "Normal" ? size.small : size.normal
dbgSize   = f_szPick(dbgSizeOpt)    // debug panel

// f_chip moved into MODULE 3 (v6.0) — it needs `atr` for its lane spacing.

f_recent(int b, int look) => not na(b) and bar_index - b <= look

// ---- V5.6 · liquidity primitives --------------------------------------------
// CONFLUENCE MASK. Pine has no bitwise operators, so it is arithmetic: one bit
// per pool kind. When two different level TYPES resolve to the same price the
// registry keeps ONE pool (it always did) and now REMEMBERS that both were
// there — which is what §5 calls a confluence, and what separates an EXTREME
// level from a merely strong one.
f_maskAdd(int m, int k) =>
    int b = int(math.pow(2, k))
    m % (2 * b) >= b ? m : m + b

f_maskN(int m) =>
    int c = 0
    for k = 0 to PK_N - 1
        int b = int(math.pow(2, k))
        if m % (2 * b) >= b
            c += 1
    c

// §13 static priority of a level TYPE, used for label ranking and for the
// optional weakest-first prune. Higher = more likely to hold real stops.
f_kindPri(int k) =>
    k == PK_HTF ? 7 : k == PK_PW ? 6 : k == PK_PD ? 5 : k == PK_EQ ? 5 :
         k == PK_EXT ? 4 : k == PK_TL ? 4 : k == PK_SES ? 3 : 1

f_kindTxt(int k) =>
    k == PK_HTF ? "HTF SWING" : k == PK_PW ? "PWH/PWL" : k == PK_PD ? "PDH/PDL" :
         k == PK_EQ ? "EQ" : k == PK_EXT ? "EXT SWING" : k == PK_TL ? "TRENDLINE" :
         k == PK_SES ? "SESSION" : "SWING"


f_tick(bool ok) => ok ? "✓" : "✗"
// V7.0 · §36 regime and §48 timing, printed by the dashboard and the debug panel
f_rgTxt(int r) => r == RG_TREND ? "TREND" : r == RG_EXP ? "EXPANSION" : r == RG_CHOP ? "CHOP" : "RANGE"
f_tmTxt(int t) => t == TM_LATE ? "LATE" : t == TM_VALID ? "VALID" : "PREMATURE"
// §40 · the PD-array ladder, most institutional first. Used to break a tie when
// two live arrays both contain the same raid.
f_paTxt(int a) =>
    a == PA_OB ? "OB" : a == PA_BRK ? "BREAKER" : a == PA_MIT ? "MITIGATION" :
         a == PA_IFVG ? "IFVG" : a == PA_BPR ? "BPR" : a == PA_REJ ? "REJECTION" : "FVG"
f_paRank(int a) =>
    a == PA_OB ? 0 : a == PA_BRK ? 1 : a == PA_IFVG ? 2 : a == PA_BPR ? 3 :
         a == PA_FVG ? 4 : a == PA_MIT ? 5 : 6
f_rqTxt(int q) => q == RQ_A ? "A" : q == RQ_B ? "B" : q == RQ_C ? "C" : "—"
f_grTxt(int g) => g == GR_AP ? "A+" : g == GR_A ? "A" : g == GR_B ? "B" : "—"
f_grMin(string s) => s == "A+" ? GR_AP : s == "A" ? GR_A : GR_B
f_biasTxt(int b) => b > 0 ? "▲ Bull" : b < 0 ? "▼ Bear" : "—"
// V7.7 · one rung of the MTF ladder. · = that timeframe is at or below the chart
f_mtfArrow(bool usable, int bi) => not usable ? "·" : bi > 0 ? "▲" : bi < 0 ? "▼" : "—"
// V5.5-E · run vocabulary · V7.5 · HTF state vocabulary (dashboard + debug)
f_hsTxt(int h) => h == HS_BULL ? "BULL" : h == HS_BEAR ? "BEAR" : h == HS_CONF ? "CONFLICT" : "NEUTRAL"
f_rnTxt(int c) =>
    c == RN_CONT ? "RUN" : c == RN_EXH ? "exhausted" : c == RN_REV ? "REVERSED" :
         c == RN_UNCONF ? "1 leg" : "none"

// ---- V5.1-A · numeric hygiene for the risk ladders ---------------------------
// v5 compared reconstructed prices against their own multiplier, so with the
// default rrMult == rrAplus == 2.0 the A+ floor failed on float cancellation
// alone (rr came out 1.9999999997). Every RR comparison now goes through f_geq.
f_geq(float a, float b) => a >= b - 1e-6

// v5 validated no relationship BETWEEN the floors. With rrAplus < rrA the A+
// rung became EASIER to reach than A, and with minRiskAtr > maxRiskAtr f_slOk
// rejected every structural level and silently fell through to the ATR stop.
// The engine reads the normalised values; the inputs keep the user's numbers.
rrAn     = math.max(rrA, rrB)
rrAPn    = math.max(rrAplus, math.max(rrA, rrB))
minRiskN = math.min(minRiskAtr, maxRiskAtr)
maxRiskN = math.max(minRiskAtr, maxRiskAtr)

// "no opposing liquidity at all" is na, never a 99.0 sentinel (v5 printed
// "opposing liquidity only 99.00R away" in the debug panel).
f_roomTxt(float r) => na(r) ? "∞" : str.tostring(r, "#.##") + "R"

// ═══════════════════════════════ MODULE 3 · CORE SERIES + CANDLE ANATOMY
// Candle psychology, confirmation candles, CISD, IFC. Every state latch in
// this file is gated on `confirmed` (ARCH-9) — nothing advances intrabar.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
atr     = ta.atr(atrLen)

// chip = text on a solid pill — readable on any candle background.
// V6.0-UI2: chips used to anchor at yloc.above/belowbar, which is one fixed y
// per bar per side, so two chips on one candle drew at the same point. Each
// side now keeps a per-bar counter and the nth chip is pushed one
// uiChipGap × ATR step further from the candle. Slots: 0 = above count,
// 1 = below count, 2 = the bar those counts belong to.
var int[] chipLn = array.new_int(3, -1)
f_chip(bool below, string txt, color bg, color tc, string sz) =>
    if array.get(chipLn, 2) != bar_index
        array.set(chipLn, 2, bar_index)
        array.set(chipLn, 0, 0)
        array.set(chipLn, 1, 0)
    int   k  = below ? 1 : 0
    int   n  = array.get(chipLn, k)
    array.set(chipLn, k, n + 1)
    float st = nz(atr, high - low) * (0.45 + n * uiChipGap)
    label.new(bar_index, below ? low - st : high + st, txt,
         style = below ? label.style_label_up : label.style_label_down,
         color = bg, textcolor = tc, size = sz)
body    = math.abs(close - open)
rng     = high - low
upWick  = high - math.max(open, close)
dnWick  = math.min(open, close) - low
bullBar = close > open
bearBar = close < open

// V7.1 · UNCONDITIONAL, and now honestly so. v7.0 shipped a `confirmOnly` input
// that nothing read — every transition and every signal in this file has always
// committed on bar close only. The input is gone; the behaviour it claimed to
// control is the behaviour, and the developing-setup preview it described in its
// tooltip is the separate `showDev` toggle.
confirmed = barstate.isconfirmed

// BUG-23: the opposite-wick cap is a fraction of RANGE, not of body. A clean
// hammer has a near-zero body, which made v2's `upWick <= body` test false.
bullPin    = rng > 0 and dnWick >= 2 * body and dnWick >= 0.45 * rng and upWick <= PIN_OPP_PCT * rng and dnWick >= minWickAtr * atr
bearPin    = rng > 0 and upWick >= 2 * body and upWick >= 0.45 * rng and dnWick <= PIN_OPP_PCT * rng and upWick >= minWickAtr * atr
bullEngulf = bullBar and bearBar[1] and close >= open[1] and open <= close[1] and body > body[1]
bearEngulf = bearBar and bullBar[1] and close <= open[1] and open >= close[1] and body > body[1]
bullConfirm = bullPin or bullEngulf
bearConfirm = bearPin or bearEngulf

// displacement = a real expansion candle closing in its own outer third.
// One definition, used by every engine (v2 had three near-duplicates).
strongUp = rng > 0 and close >= high - STRONG_CLOSE_PCT * rng
strongDn = rng > 0 and close <= low + STRONG_CLOSE_PCT * rng
dispUpBar = bullBar and body >= dispFactor * atr and strongUp
dispDnBar = bearBar and body >= dispFactor * atr and strongDn

// ═══ V7.5 · §12 · DISPLACEMENT QUALITY — one reading, every consumer ═══════
// Body size alone is not delivery. Six independent readings of the SAME candle:
// body ≥ the setup floor · body ≥ twice it · efficient close (holds ≥ 55 % of
// its range) · very efficient close (≥ 75 %) · wick asymmetry (opposing wick ≤
// 20 % of range) · relative expansion (body ≥ 1.5 × the 20-bar mean body).
// The machine's stage 6 keeps its four HARD requirements; this only RANKS what
// passed, and is stamped on the setup (dispQ) and on the zone (srcDispQ).
bodyAvg = ta.sma(body, 20)
f_dispQ(bool isL) =>
    float dr = math.max(rng, syminfo.mintick)
    float hd = (isL ? close - low : high - close) / dr
    (body >= dispBodyAtr * atr ? 1 : 0) + (body >= 2 * dispBodyAtr * atr ? 1 : 0) + (hd >= DISP_HOLD_FRAC ? 1 : 0) +
         (hd >= 0.75 ? 1 : 0) + ((isL ? upWick : dnWick) <= 0.2 * dr ? 1 : 0) + (body >= 1.5 * nz(bodyAvg, body) ? 1 : 0)
dqUp = f_dispQ(true)
dqDn = f_dispQ(false)

// ---- CISD: change in state of delivery ---------------------------------------
// The OPEN of the first candle of the most recent consecutive bull / bear run.
// A confirmed displacement close through that origin open = delivery flipped.
// A neutral candle (body ≤ CISD_NEUTRAL_PCT of range) ends BOTH series.
cisdNeutral = rng == 0 or body <= CISD_NEUTRAL_PCT * rng
// V7.0 · renamed from cisdBull / cisdBear: these mark a candle that CONTINUES
// a directional run, which is the input to the CISD test, not the CISD itself.
// §5 uses the cisdBull / cisdBear names for the delivery flip (MODULE 4).
cisdSeqUp   = bullBar and not cisdNeutral
cisdSeqDn   = bearBar and not cisdNeutral
var float bullRunOpen = na
var float bearRunOpen = na
var int   bullRunLen  = 0
var int   bearRunLen  = 0
var int   bullRunBar  = na    // V5: origin bar of the run whose OPEN a CISD breaks
var int   bearRunBar  = na
var bool  cisdDnDone  = false
var bool  cisdUpDone  = false
if confirmed
    if cisdNeutral
        bullRunOpen := na
        bearRunOpen := na
        bullRunLen  := 0
        bearRunLen  := 0
    if cisdSeqUp and not cisdSeqUp[1]
        bullRunOpen := open
        bullRunBar  := bar_index
        bullRunLen  := 1
        cisdDnDone  := false
    else if cisdSeqUp
        bullRunLen += 1
    if cisdSeqDn and not cisdSeqDn[1]
        bearRunOpen := open
        bearRunBar  := bar_index
        bearRunLen  := 1
        cisdUpDone  := false
    else if cisdSeqDn
        bearRunLen += 1
cisdDn = confirmed and not na(bullRunOpen) and not cisdDnDone and cisdSeqDn and close < bullRunOpen and bullRunLen >= 2 and body >= BRK_BODY_ATR * atr and strongDn
cisdUp = confirmed and not na(bearRunOpen) and not cisdUpDone and cisdSeqUp and close > bearRunOpen and bearRunLen >= 2 and body >= BRK_BODY_ATR * atr and strongUp
if cisdDn
    cisdDnDone := true
if cisdUp
    cisdUpDone := true
if showCisd and cisdDn
    f_chip(false, "CISD", color.new(COL_BEAR, 55), color.white, mkSize)
if showCisd and cisdUp
    f_chip(true, "CISD", color.new(COL_BULL, 55), color.white, mkSize)

// ---- IFC candle: institutional funding candle --------------------------------
// Takes a REAL pool (the prior IFC_LOOK-bar extreme) with meaningful
// penetration, then closes past the prior candle's midpoint in the outer
// STRONG_CLOSE_PCT of its own range with a displacement body.
prevMid  = (high[1] + low[1]) / 2
ifcPoolL = ta.lowest(low, IFC_LOOK)[1]
ifcPoolH = ta.highest(high, IFC_LOOK)[1]
ifcBull = low < ifcPoolL and ifcPoolL - low >= NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr and bullBar and close > prevMid and strongUp and body >= BRK_BODY_ATR * atr
ifcBear = high > ifcPoolH and high - ifcPoolH >= NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr and bearBar and close < prevMid and strongDn and body >= BRK_BODY_ATR * atr
if showIFC and ifcBull and confirmed
    f_chip(true, "IFC", color.new(color.gray, 55), color.new(COL_BULL, 0), mkSize)
if showIFC and ifcBear and confirmed
    f_chip(false, "IFC", color.new(color.gray, 55), color.new(COL_BEAR, 0), mkSize)

// retest confirmation used by the POI / trendline / machine execution stages
zoneBullConf = bullConfirm or ifcBull or (cisdAsConf and cisdUp)
zoneBearConf = bearConfirm or ifcBear or (cisdAsConf and cisdDn)

// ═════════════════════════════ MODULE 4 · STRUCTURE (INTERNAL + EXTERNAL)
// ARCH-3 / BUG-1 / BUG-2. One engine, two INDEPENDENT instances:
//   EXTERNAL (extPiv) — context, major BOS / CHoCH, protected levels, range.
//   INTERNAL (pivLen) — MSS / execution confirmation ONLY.
// The protected swing is the ACTUAL ORIGINATING PIVOT of the leg that made
// the last BOS, not a running min/max. A weak (non-displacement) close through
// a level is a liquidity RUN: it is reported, it does NOT set the break latch,
// and the level therefore stays available for a real structural break later.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
type Struct
    int   dir       = 0
    float swHi      = na    // latest confirmed pivot high (BOS-up level)
    int   swHiBar   = na
    float swLo      = na    // latest confirmed pivot low  (BOS-dn level)
    int   swLoBar   = na
    bool  hiBroken  = false // once-per-level structural latch
    bool  loBroken  = false
    bool  hiRun     = false // once-per-level weak-run latch (BUG-2)
    bool  loRun     = false
    float protLo    = na    // protected low  = origin pivot of the last bull leg
    int   protLoBar = na
    float protHi    = na
    int   protHiBar = na
    int   bosUpBar  = na
    int   bosDnBar  = na
    int   chUpBar   = na
    int   chDnBar   = na
    float evLvlUp   = na    // the level the last bullish event actually broke
    float evLvlDn   = na
    int   evBarUp   = na    // V5: the bar the broken level's pivot formed on
    int   evBarDn   = na

// Mutates the passed instance (a UDT field write is a reference write, not a
// global assignment, so this is legal inside a function) and reports the event.
f_structUpdate(Struct s, float pvH, float pvL, simple int pvLen) =>
    bool bosU = false
    bool bosD = false
    bool chU  = false
    bool chD  = false
    bool runU = false
    bool runD = false
    if confirmed
        // 1) register newly confirmed pivots — these are the ONLY structural levels
        if not na(pvH)
            s.swHi     := pvH
            s.swHiBar  := bar_index - pvLen
            s.hiBroken := false
            s.hiRun    := false
        if not na(pvL)
            s.swLo     := pvL
            s.swLoBar  := bar_index - pvLen
            s.loBroken := false
            s.loRun    := false
        bool disp = body >= structDisp * atr
        // 2) CHoCH first — a displaced close through the PROTECTED swing
        if s.dir == 1 and not na(s.protLo) and close < s.protLo and disp
            chD          := true
            s.dir        := -1
            s.chDnBar    := bar_index
            s.evLvlDn    := s.protLo
            s.evBarDn    := s.protLoBar
            s.protHi     := s.swHi          // origin of the new down-leg
            s.protHiBar  := s.swHiBar
            s.protLo     := na
            s.protLoBar  := na
            s.loBroken   := false
        else if s.dir == -1 and not na(s.protHi) and close > s.protHi and disp
            chU          := true
            s.dir        := 1
            s.chUpBar    := bar_index
            s.evLvlUp    := s.protHi
            s.evBarUp    := s.protHiBar
            s.protLo     := s.swLo          // origin of the new up-leg
            s.protLoBar  := s.swLoBar
            s.protHi     := na
            s.protHiBar  := na
            s.hiBroken   := false
        // 3) BOS — a displaced close through the latest pivot in the trend
        else if not na(s.swHi) and not s.hiBroken and close > s.swHi and s.dir >= 0
            if disp
                bosU        := true
                s.hiBroken  := true
                s.dir       := 1
                s.bosUpBar  := bar_index
                s.evLvlUp   := s.swHi
                s.evBarUp   := s.swHiBar
                s.protLo    := s.swLo       // the pivot low that originated this leg
                s.protLoBar := s.swLoBar
            else if not s.hiRun
                runU     := true            // liquidity run: level NOT burned (BUG-2)
                s.hiRun  := true
        else if not na(s.swLo) and not s.loBroken and close < s.swLo and s.dir <= 0
            if disp
                bosD        := true
                s.loBroken  := true
                s.dir       := -1
                s.bosDnBar  := bar_index
                s.evLvlDn   := s.swLo
                s.evBarDn   := s.swLoBar
                s.protHi    := s.swHi
                s.protHiBar := s.swHiBar
            else if not s.loRun
                runD     := true
                s.loRun  := true
        // 4) counter-trend pivot breaks with no protected level yet (first leg)
        else if s.dir == 1 and na(s.protLo) and not na(s.swLo) and not s.loBroken and close < s.swLo and disp
            chD         := true
            s.dir       := -1
            s.chDnBar   := bar_index
            s.evLvlDn   := s.swLo
            s.evBarDn   := s.swLoBar
            s.loBroken  := true
            s.protHi    := s.swHi
            s.protHiBar := s.swHiBar
        else if s.dir == -1 and na(s.protHi) and not na(s.swHi) and not s.hiBroken and close > s.swHi and disp
            chU         := true
            s.dir       := 1
            s.chUpBar   := bar_index
            s.evLvlUp   := s.swHi
            s.evBarUp   := s.swHiBar
            s.hiBroken  := true
            s.protLo    := s.swLo
            s.protLoBar := s.swLoBar
    [bosU, bosD, chU, chD, runU, runD]

// ---- internal scale (execution) ----------------------------------------------
ph = ta.pivothigh(high, pivLen, pivLen)
pl = ta.pivotlow(low, pivLen, pivLen)
var Struct sInt = Struct.new()
[bosUp, bosDn, chochUp, chochDn, runUp, runDn] = f_structUpdate(sInt, ph, pl, pivLen)

// ---- external scale (context) -------------------------------------------------
eph = ta.pivothigh(high, extPiv, extPiv)
epl = ta.pivotlow(low, extPiv, extPiv)
var Struct sExt = Struct.new()
[eBosUp, eBosDn, eChochUp, eChochDn, eRunUp, eRunDn] = f_structUpdate(sExt, eph, epl, extPiv)

msDir    = sInt.dir
extDir   = sExt.dir
protLowI  = sInt.protLo
protHighI = sInt.protHi
protLowE  = sExt.protLo
protHighE = sExt.protHi
lastRes  = sInt.swHi          // latest internal swing high — buy-side liquidity above
lastSup  = sInt.swLo          // latest internal swing low  — sell-side liquidity below
lastResBar = sInt.swHiBar
lastSupBar = sInt.swLoBar
extRes   = sExt.swHi
extSup   = sExt.swLo
// v7.3 · the two single-use aliases folded into their one consumer (MODULE 8)

// structural break flags reused by the pattern engines (①②③) — these must be
// the STRUCTURAL breaks, so a weak run no longer arms a pattern watch.
brokeUp = bosUp or eBosUp or chochUp or eChochUp
brokeDn = bosDn or eBosDn or chochDn or eChochDn
// the level the break actually took (needed by the ①/②/③ watch state machines)
brkUpLvl = bosUp or chochUp ? sInt.evLvlUp : eBosUp or eChochUp ? sExt.evLvlUp : na
brkDnLvl = bosDn or chochDn ? sInt.evLvlDn : eBosDn or eChochDn ? sExt.evLvlDn : na

// ---- separate trigger memories: internal ≠ external (BUG-3) -------------------
var int iChUpBar   = na
var int iChDnBar   = na
var int eChUpBar   = na
var int eChDnBar   = na
var int eBosUpBar2 = na
var int eBosDnBar2 = na
var int cisdUpBar  = na
var int cisdDnBar  = na
var int dispUpBar2 = na
var int dispDnBar2 = na
if confirmed
    if chochUp
        iChUpBar := bar_index
    if chochDn
        iChDnBar := bar_index
    if eChochUp
        eChUpBar := bar_index
    if eChochDn
        eChDnBar := bar_index
    if eBosUp
        eBosUpBar2 := bar_index
    if eBosDn
        eBosDnBar2 := bar_index
    if cisdUp
        cisdUpBar := bar_index
    if cisdDn
        cisdDnBar := bar_index
    if dispUpBar
        dispUpBar2 := bar_index
    if dispDnBar
        dispDnBar2 := bar_index

recentIChUp  = f_recent(iChUpBar, TRIG_LOOK)
recentIChDn  = f_recent(iChDnBar, TRIG_LOOK)
recentEChUp  = f_recent(eChUpBar, TRIG_LOOK)
recentEChDn  = f_recent(eChDnBar, TRIG_LOOK)
recentEBosUp = f_recent(eBosUpBar2, TRIG_LOOK)
recentEBosDn = f_recent(eBosDnBar2, TRIG_LOOK)
recentCisdUp = f_recent(cisdUpBar, TRIG_LOOK)
recentCisdDn = f_recent(cisdDnBar, TRIG_LOOK)
recentDispUp = f_recent(dispUpBar2, 3)
recentDispDn = f_recent(dispDnBar2, 3)
// ═══ V7.0 · §5 · FOUR SEPARATE STRUCTURAL VOCABULARIES ══════════════════
// BOS   · continuation. The trend broke its own last pivot, with displacement.
// CHoCH · potential change of direction. The PROTECTED swing broke — the origin
//         pivot of the leg that made the last BOS, not any recent pivot.
// MSS   · a MEANINGFUL shift: a CHoCH is one by construction (f_structUpdate
//         already demands a displacement body). A CISD is one only when the
//         same candle also displaces, and only while mssNeedDisp allows it.
// CISD  · delivery flipped: price closed through the ORIGIN OPEN of the last
//         directional run. That is an order-flow statement, not a structural
//         one, and v6.4 treating it as identical to a CHoCH is why "MSS" fired
//         on candles where no level had been broken at all.
// Every consumer below now says which of the four it means.
// the CISD promotion test, applied once here rather than at each call site
// V7.5 · `or strongUp/Dn` removed: cisdUp already REQUIRES strongUp, so the
// disjunct made mssNeedDisp inert (a 0.5-ATR candle was "displacement").
cisdMssUp = cisdUp and (not mssNeedDisp or dispUpBar)
cisdMssDn = cisdDn and (not mssNeedDisp or dispDnBar)
mssBull = chochUp or cisdMssUp
mssBear = chochDn or cisdMssDn
// SHIFT = the loosest reading, "delivery or structure turned". Used only where
// a directional hint is enough (the pool reaction promotion, the run engine's
// exhaustion test) — never as a setup's stage-5 MSS.
shiftUp = chochUp or cisdUp
shiftDn = chochDn or cisdDn
recentMssUp = recentIChUp or recentCisdUp
recentMssDn = recentIChDn or recentCisdDn
// structure direction preferring the external narrative (context)
structDir = extDir != 0 ? extDir : msDir

// ---- structure marks: external bright, internal muted, runs optional ---------
if showStruct and (eBosUp or eChochUp)
    f_chip(true, eChochUp ? "CHoCH" : "BOS", color.new(COL_BULL, 15), color.white, size.small)
if showStruct and (eBosDn or eChochDn)
    f_chip(false, eChochDn ? "CHoCH" : "BOS", color.new(COL_BEAR, 15), color.white, size.small)
if showStruct and (bosUp or chochUp)
    f_chip(true, chochUp ? "iCHoCH" : "ibos", color.new(color.gray, 35), color.new(COL_BULL, 0), mkSize)
if showStruct and (bosDn or chochDn)
    f_chip(false, chochDn ? "iCHoCH" : "ibos", color.new(color.gray, 35), color.new(COL_BEAR, 0), mkSize)
if showRuns and (runUp or eRunUp)
    f_chip(true, "run", color.new(COL_NEU, 45), color.white, mkSize)
if showRuns and (runDn or eRunDn)
    f_chip(false, "run", color.new(COL_NEU, 45), color.white, mkSize)

// ---- order-flow zigzag: RETIRED in v7.4 (reclaim R3, display only) ----------

// ═══════════════════════ MODULE 5 · SESSIONS · KILLZONES · SILVER BULLET
// Session state is computed ALWAYS; the display toggles only affect drawing
// (BUG-6 — in v2 turning a display off silently disabled score terms).
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
f_inSes(simple string ses) =>
    not na(time(timeframe.period, ses, "America/New_York"))

inAsia = f_inSes(asiaSes)
inLon  = f_inSes(lonSes)
inNY   = f_inSes(nySes)
inSbLO = f_inSes(sbSesLO)
inSbAM = f_inSes(sbSesAM)
inSbPM = f_inSes(sbSesPM)
inSB   = inSbLO or inSbAM or inSbPM
inKZ   = inAsia or inLon or inNY or inSB
kzOK   = not useKZ or not timeframe.isintraday or inKZ

// SCHEDULED VOLATILITY FILTER — fixed clock slots, NOT a live economic
// calendar. Named and documented as such (Phase 21).
inVol  = f_inSes(news1Ses) or f_inSes(news2Ses) or f_inSes(news3Ses)
volOK  = not useNews or not timeframe.isintraday or not inVol

// Silver Bullet is a FILTER, never a signal (Phase 20).
sbActive = sbRole != "Off" and timeframe.isintraday and inSB
sbGate   = not sbOnlyIn or not timeframe.isintraday or inSB

// ---- opening prices: 00:00 midnight + 08:30 NY -------------------------------
inMidWin = f_inSes("0000-0005")
in830Win = f_inSes("0830-0835")
newMid  = inMidWin and not inMidWin[1]
new830  = in830Win and not in830Win[1]

var line  midLn      = na
var label midLb      = na
var line  ny830Ln    = na
var label ny830Lb    = na
var int   lastMidBar = na
var int   last830Bar = na

// a bar's OPEN is fixed on its first tick → safe to draw immediately, but the
// re-entry guard keeps it to one draw per bar
if timeframe.isintraday and newMid and (na(lastMidBar) or lastMidBar != bar_index)
    lastMidBar := bar_index
    if showOpens
        line.delete(midLn)
        label.delete(midLb)
        midLn := line.new(bar_index, open, bar_index + 1, open,
             color = color.new(color.white, 45), style = line.style_dotted, extend = extend.right)
        midLb := label.new(bar_index, open, "00:00", style = label.style_label_left,
             color = color.new(color.gray, 45), textcolor = color.white, size = infoSize)
if timeframe.isintraday and new830 and (na(last830Bar) or last830Bar != bar_index)
    last830Bar := bar_index
    if showOpens
        line.delete(ny830Ln)
        label.delete(ny830Lb)
        ny830Ln := line.new(bar_index, open, bar_index + 1, open,
             color = color.new(color.orange, 45), style = line.style_dotted, extend = extend.right)
        ny830Lb := label.new(bar_index, open, "08:30", style = label.style_label_left,
             color = color.new(color.orange, 45), textcolor = color.white, size = infoSize)

// ---- session extremes: tracked live, FROZEN on the session close -------------
// The frozen level is resting liquidity and is handed to the pool registry;
// raid / break detection lives there, so every pool kind shares one lifecycle.
type Ses
    float hi    = na
    float lo    = na
    float fhi   = na
    float flo   = na
    bool  fresh = false
    line  hiLn  = na
    line  loLn  = na
    label hiLb  = na
    label loLb  = na

f_sesTrack(Ses s, bool active, string tag, color col) =>
    s.fresh := false
    if active
        if not active[1]
            s.hi := high
            s.lo := low
        else
            s.hi := math.max(nz(s.hi, high), high)
            s.lo := math.min(nz(s.lo, low), low)
    if not active and active[1] and confirmed and not na(s.hi)
        s.fhi   := s.hi
        s.flo   := s.lo
        s.fresh := true
        if showSesHL
            line.delete(s.hiLn)
            line.delete(s.loLn)
            label.delete(s.hiLb)
            label.delete(s.loLb)
            s.hiLn := line.new(bar_index, s.fhi, bar_index + 1, s.fhi, color = color.new(col, 35), extend = extend.right)
            s.loLn := line.new(bar_index, s.flo, bar_index + 1, s.flo, color = color.new(col, 35), extend = extend.right)
            s.hiLb := label.new(bar_index, s.fhi, tag + " H", style = label.style_label_left,
                 color = color.new(col, 45), textcolor = color.white, size = infoSize)
            s.loLb := label.new(bar_index, s.flo, tag + " L", style = label.style_label_left,
                 color = color.new(col, 45), textcolor = color.white, size = infoSize)
    s.fresh

var Ses sesAsia = Ses.new()
var Ses sesLon  = Ses.new()
var Ses sesNY   = Ses.new()
asiaFresh = f_sesTrack(sesAsia, inAsia and timeframe.isintraday, "Asia", color.purple)
lonFresh  = f_sesTrack(sesLon,  inLon  and timeframe.isintraday, "Lon",  color.blue)
nyFresh   = f_sesTrack(sesNY,   inNY   and timeframe.isintraday, "NY",   color.orange)

// ═══════════════════════════════════ MODULE 6 · HTF CONTEXT ENGINE
// The HTF answers "WHERE is price likely to react?", never "BUY".
//
// V4-BUG-9 / V5-10 · EVERY read here is the LAST CLOSED HTF BAR: the expression
// inside the request is indexed [1] and the call uses lookahead_on. v4 used a
// bare expression with lookahead_off, which returns the HTF bar that is still
// BEING BUILT — not a future-data leak, but a value that changes under the
// script as the HTF candle forms, which is exactly what "repainting HTF bias"
// means. The [1] steps back to the closed bar; lookahead_on then serves that
// closed bar from the first chart bar of the new HTF candle instead of an
// entire HTF candle later. The remaining delay is the honest one: an HTF event
// is usable on the first chart bar after the HTF candle that produced it has
// closed.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
f_autoHtf() =>
    switch timeframe.period
        "1"   => "15"
        "2"   => "30"
        "3"   => "30"
        "5"   => "60"
        "10"  => "120"
        "15"  => "240"
        "30"  => "240"
        "60"  => "D"
        "120" => "D"
        "240" => "D"
        => "D"

// V7.2 BUG-01 · a request.security to a timeframe BELOW the chart leaks future
// data through lookahead_on, whatever the [1] inside the requested expression
// says: the [1] steps back one bar of the REQUESTED series, not one chart bar.
// Auto is always >= the chart; the manual options were not constrained at all.
f_tfUp(simple string tf) => timeframe.in_seconds(tf) < timeframe.in_seconds() ? timeframe.period : tf
htfTf    = f_tfUp(htfSel    == "Auto" ? f_autoHtf() : htfSel)
htfCtxTf = f_tfUp(htfCtxSel == "Auto" ? f_autoHtf() : htfCtxSel)
htfLive  = htfTf != timeframe.period

// ---- MTF bias row (dashboard + optional counter-trend filter) ----------------
// V5-10 / V4-BUG-9. `lookahead_off` does NOT mean "the last closed HTF bar" — it
// means "no future data", and the HTF bar that is still forming is not future
// data, so v4 read a bar that changed as it built. The only combination that is
// both leak-free AND stable is expr[1] + lookahead_on: the [1] steps back to the
// bar that has already closed, and lookahead_on then makes that closed bar
// available from the first chart bar of the new HTF candle instead of one HTF
// candle later. Every HTF read in this module now uses it.
f_biasSrc() =>
    _v = close > ta.ema(close, biasLen) ? 1 : -1
    _v[1]

f_bias(simple string tf) =>
    request.security(syminfo.tickerid, f_tfUp(tf), f_biasSrc(), lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)

// V7.4 reclaim R2 · ONE bias request, at the FILTER timeframe only. v7.3 ran
// five (5 / 15 / 60 / 240 / D) to feed a dashboard table; four request.security
// instances and their four rows are gone (10 → 6 instances). The filter itself
// is unchanged, and the dashboard shows the filter timeframe's bias.
biasTf   = biasFilter == "Off" ? timeframe.period : biasFilter
filtBias = biasFilter == "Off" ? 0 : nz(f_bias(biasTf), 0)

// ---- V7.7 · THE MTF BIAS LADDER (5m · 15m · 1H · 4H) — DISPLAY ONLY --------
// v7.4 reclaim R2 deleted the multi-timeframe bias table to buy compiled tokens,
// and with it the one thing a dashboard is actually for: seeing where THIS chart
// sits inside the ladder above it. Four fixed rungs are back, read through the
// same non-repainting construction as every other HTF value in this module —
// the expression is indexed [1] INSIDE the request and the call uses
// lookahead_on, so only a CLOSED candle of that timeframe is ever visible, and
// f_tfUp clamps any rung below the chart so it can never leak forward.
//
// THIS IS ANNOTATION AND NOTHING ELSE. No gate, grade, score, tier, stop,
// target, dedupe or state transition reads a single value below — the bias
// FILTER keeps its own separate read above, exactly as before, so switching the
// filter on still cannot change the score (BUG-16). A rung BELOW the chart
// timeframe is not information, it is the chart looking at itself, so it is
// displayed as · rather than as a direction.
mtfB5   = nz(f_bias("5"),   0)
mtfB15  = nz(f_bias("15"),  0)
mtfB60  = nz(f_bias("60"),  0)
mtfB240 = nz(f_bias("240"), 0)
tfSec   = timeframe.in_seconds()
mtfU5   = timeframe.in_seconds("5")   >= tfSec
mtfU15  = timeframe.in_seconds("15")  >= tfSec
mtfU60  = timeframe.in_seconds("60")  >= tfSec
mtfU240 = timeframe.in_seconds("240") >= tfSec
// V7.9 · the ladder's alignment as ONE net figure instead of a bull count AND a
//   bear count AND a total — three four-term expressions where one does. Each
//   usable rung contributes its own ±1, so mtfNet == mtfN is unanimous bull and
//   mtfNet == -mtfN unanimous bear, which is everything the row displayed before.
mtfNet  = (mtfU5 ? mtfB5 : 0) + (mtfU15 ? mtfB15 : 0) + (mtfU60 ? mtfB60 : 0) + (mtfU240 ? mtfB240 : 0)
mtfN    = (mtfU5 ? 1 : 0) + (mtfU15 ? 1 : 0) + (mtfU60 ? 1 : 0) + (mtfU240 ? 1 : 0)

// ---- HTF context bundle: direction, external swings, premium / discount -----
// BUG-16: the CONTEXT timeframe is now independent of the FILTER timeframe, so
// enabling the counter-trend filter no longer changes the score.
f_htfCtx() =>
    _dir = close > ta.ema(close, biasLen) ? 1 : -1
    _ph  = ta.pivothigh(high, 3, 3)
    _pl  = ta.pivotlow(low, 3, 3)
    var float _hi = na
    var float _lo = na
    if not na(_ph)
        _hi := _ph
    if not na(_pl)
        _lo := _pl
    _w  = not na(_hi) and not na(_lo) and _hi > _lo ? _hi - _lo : na
    _pd = na(_w) ? na : (close - _lo) / _w
    [_dir[1], _pd[1]]

[htfDirRaw, htfPd] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, htfCtxTf, f_htfCtx(),
     lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
htfMom = nz(htfDirRaw, 0)     // V7.5 · EMA MOMENTUM only — the effective direction is resolved after the structure feed

// ---- previous day high / low (external liquidity) ---------------------------
// The [1] inside the request is what makes lookahead_on safe here: only the
// COMPLETED previous day is ever read, on historical and realtime bars alike.
[pdhRaw, pdlRaw] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, f_tfUp("D"), [high[1], low[1]],
     lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
newDay = ta.change(time("D")) != 0

// ---- V5.6 · previous WEEK high / low (§2 · the top of the external ladder) --
// Same construction and the same safety argument as PDH/PDL: the [1] is INSIDE
// the request, so only the COMPLETED previous week is ever visible, on history
// and in realtime alike. v5.5 had no weekly liquidity at all, which left the
// single most obvious external stop pool on any intraday chart unmarked.
[pwhRaw, pwlRaw] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, f_tfUp("W"), [high[1], low[1]],
     lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
newWeek = ta.change(time("W")) != 0
// weekly levels are meaningful intraday and on the daily; on weekly+ they are
// the chart's own previous bar and carry no information
pwUsable = showPW and (timeframe.isintraday or timeframe.isdaily)

// ---- V7.0 · HTF STRUCTURE + HTF LIQUIDITY (§6 / §25 / §26) ------------------
// This request.security slot used to publish the HTF CRT / turtle-soup pair.
// Those producers are gone; the SAME call, at the same cost, now publishes what
// the hierarchy actually needs: the HTF's own confirmed swing extremes (real
// external liquidity, registered as PK_HTF pools) and its external structure
// direction, so an LTF internal shift can never be mistaken for an HTF one.
// NON-REPAINTING: every value is indexed [1] INSIDE the request, so only a
// CLOSED HTF candle is ever visible, and the pivots are ta.pivot*(2,2) which
// are themselves only confirmed two HTF bars later.
f_htfStruct() =>
    _ph = ta.pivothigh(high, 2, 2)
    _pl = ta.pivotlow(low, 2, 2)
    var float _hi = na
    var float _lo = na
    var int   _d  = 0
    if not na(_ph)
        _hi := _ph
    if not na(_pl)
        _lo := _pl
    if not na(_hi) and close > _hi
        _d := 1
    if not na(_lo) and close < _lo
        _d := -1
    [_hi[1], _lo[1], _d[1], time[1]]

[hSwHi, hSwLo, hStrDirRaw, hT] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, htfTf, f_htfStruct(),
     lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
hStrDir   = nz(hStrDirRaw, 0)
newHtfBar = htfLive and not na(hT) and hT != nz(hT[1])

// ═══ V7.5 · §8 / §9 · HTF STATE — STRUCTURE FIRST, MOMENTUM SECOND ═══════════
// v7.4's context was one EMA (htfDir). Structure has more authority than an EMA
// cross, and when the two disagree the honest answer is CONFLICT, not whichever
// happened to be read first. `htfDir` keeps its name and every consumer
// (context, zone tiers, HTF POI tier, score) reads the RESOLVED direction:
