// agreement → that side · no HTF structure yet → momentum · conflict → 0.
htfConf  = hStrDir != 0 and htfMom != 0 and hStrDir != htfMom
htfDir   = htfConf ? 0 : hStrDir != 0 ? hStrDir : htfMom
htfState = htfConf ? HS_CONF : htfDir > 0 ? HS_BULL : htfDir < 0 ? HS_BEAR : HS_NEUT


// ---- HTF FVG POI (the fractal model's "where") ------------------------------
// BUG-17: displacement required, HTF-direction filter, and the bar/touch
// counters are reset when the POI dies.
f_htfPoi() =>
    _atr = ta.atr(atrLen)
    _dir = close > ta.ema(close, biasLen) ? 1 : -1
    _dU  = close[1] > open[1] and math.abs(close[1] - open[1]) >= hPoiDisp * _atr
    _dD  = close[1] < open[1] and math.abs(close[1] - open[1]) >= hPoiDisp * _atr
    var float bTop = na
    var float bBot = na
    var int   bBar = na
    var int   bIn  = 0
    var float sTop = na
    var float sBot = na
    var int   sBar = na
    var int   sIn  = 0
    // V7.6 · F-09 · EDGE-TRIGGERED, like the chart zones' mitN. v7.5 incremented on
    //   EVERY HTF candle whose range reached into the array, which is dwell time, not
    //   mitigation: price parked just inside retired the POI in hPoiMaxTouch candles
    //   without ever leaving it. A mitigation is one entry from fully outside.
    var bool  bInZ = false
    var bool  sInZ = false
    // V7.7 · F-04 · THE ARRAY NEEDS AN IDENTITY, NOT JUST COORDINATES. `bIn` / `sIn`
    //   are the mitigation counters f_htfTier reads, and they RESET to 0 whenever this
    //   feed stamps a new gap or retires the old one. A FEM setup holds the OLD array's
    //   coordinates but compares its stored `poiIn0` against the LIVE counter, so the
    //   moment the feed moved on, `max(0 - in0 - 1, 0)` collapsed to 0 and a mitigated,
    //   decayed HTF POI silently sprang back to tier 1 — and FEM is the highest-ranked
    //   producer in the file, so that wrong tier won every arbitration it entered.
    //   One monotonic counter per side fixes it: the setup records the id it bound and
    //   dies when the feed no longer serves that same array (MODULE 12).
    var int   bSeq = 0
    var int   sSeq = 0
    if low > high[2] and _dU and (not hPoiNeedDir or _dir > 0)
        bTop := low
        bBot := high[2]
        bBar := bar_index
        bIn  := 0
        bInZ := false
        bSeq += 1
    if high < low[2] and _dD and (not hPoiNeedDir or _dir < 0)
        sBot := high
        sTop := low[2]
        sBar := bar_index
        sIn  := 0
        sInZ := false
        sSeq += 1
    bool bNow = not na(bTop) and bar_index > bBar and low <= bTop
    if bNow and not bInZ
        bIn += 1
    bInZ := bNow
    bool sNow = not na(sTop) and bar_index > sBar and high >= sBot
    if sNow and not sInZ
        sIn += 1
    sInZ := sNow
    // V7.7 · F-04 · retirement is an identity change too — an expired / traded-through
    //   HTF array is not the array the setup bound, and the id says so.
    if not na(bBot) and (close < bBot or bar_index - bBar > hPoiMaxAge or bIn > hPoiMaxTouch)
        bTop := na
        bBot := na
        bBar := na
        bIn  := 0
        bSeq += 1
    if not na(sTop) and (close > sTop or bar_index - sBar > hPoiMaxAge or sIn > hPoiMaxTouch)
        sTop := na
        sBot := na
        sBar := na
        sIn  := 0
        sSeq += 1
    [bTop[1], bBot[1], sTop[1], sBot[1], bIn[1], sIn[1], bSeq[1], sSeq[1]]

[hPoiBTop, hPoiBBot, hPoiSTop, hPoiSBot, hPoiBIn, hPoiSIn, hPoiBSeq, hPoiSSeq] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, htfTf, f_htfPoi(),
     lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)

// HTF context verdict, per direction. This is CONTEXT — it enables a setup, it
// never triggers one.
//
// V4-BUG-16: v4 OR'd four unrelated conditions together, so a long taken while
// the HTF was bearish looked exactly like a long taken with the HTF. v5 answers
// two separate questions:
//   CONTINUATION — the closed HTF agrees, and price is on the correct side of
//                  the HTF dealing range (or sitting in an HTF POI).
//   REVERSAL     — the closed HTF disagrees. Allowed only from an HTF premium /
//                  discount EXTREME or an opposing HTF FVG POI, and MODULE 15
//                  additionally demands a grade-A raid, a tier-1 POI and a
//                  CHoCH-grade MSS before such a setup may be graded above B.
// "at a location" = inside an HTF FVG POI ± the containment slack (Phase 14 /
// BUG-20 / V7.4 F-13). One definition, read by the context verdict below, the
// score (§26) and the debug panel.
f_atHtfPoi() =>
    bool aL = not na(hPoiBTop) and low  <= hPoiBTop + poiBindAtr * atr and high >= hPoiBBot - poiBindAtr * atr
    bool aS = not na(hPoiSTop) and high >= hPoiSBot - poiBindAtr * atr and low  <= hPoiSTop + poiBindAtr * atr
    [aL, aS]
[atHtfPoiL, atHtfPoiS] = f_atHtfPoi()
htfContLong  = htfDir > 0 and (na(htfPd) or htfPd <= 0.5 or atHtfPoiL)   // V7.4 F-13
htfContShort = htfDir < 0 and (na(htfPd) or htfPd >= 0.5 or atHtfPoiS)
// V7.6 · F-01 · A REVERSAL NEEDS PRICE TO BE AT THE ARRAY, NOT THE ARRAY TO EXIST.
//   v7.5 read `not na(hPoiBTop)` — "the HTF currently holds a live bullish FVG
//   somewhere". On a liquid instrument that is true nearly always, so the disjunct
//   swallowed the premium / discount EXTREME requirement the allowRev tooltip
//   advertises and htfCtxLong became tautological: a long setup armed on every bar
//   of a bearish HTF, occupied the single long slot for setupLife bars and could
//   never grade (revOk demands CHoCH + T1 + RQ_A + pdExt). The continuation verdict
//   one line above already used the LOCATION test; both verdicts use it now.
htfRevLong   = allowRev and htfDir < 0 and ((not na(htfPd) and htfPd <= PD_EXTREME) or atHtfPoiL)
htfRevShort  = allowRev and htfDir > 0 and ((not na(htfPd) and htfPd >= 1 - PD_EXTREME) or atHtfPoiS)
// "this setup is fighting the HTF" — carried into the setup and into the grade
htfIsRevLong  = not htfContLong  and htfDir < 0
htfIsRevShort = not htfContShort and htfDir > 0
// V5.1-A3b: `not needHtfAlign or ...` made the whole clause tautological, so
// turning the HTF alignment requirement OFF also switched off allowRev AND the
// premium/discount-EXTREME requirement that htfRevLong carries — counter-HTF
// setups armed with neither. Alignment-off now means "continuation OR a
// qualified reversal OR flat/neutral HTF", not "anything at all".
// V7.5 · a CONFLICT HTF may still arm a setup unless htfConfMode = Block; the
// candidate is then downgraded or held to A+ by the grade / gate layer.
// V7.6 · F-10 · a CONFLICT arms only the side the HTF STRUCTURE favours. v7.5 armed
//   BOTH, so the least readable HTF state generated the most candidates and held
//   both setup slots hostage for setupLife bars. htfConf guarantees hStrDir != 0, so
//   exactly one side arms; which one is decided by the layer that has authority (§8).
htfCtxLong   = htfContLong  or htfRevLong  or (htfConf and htfConfMode != "Block" and hStrDir > 0) or (not needHtfAlign and not htfIsRevLong)
htfCtxShort  = htfContShort or htfRevShort or (htfConf and htfConfMode != "Block" and hStrDir < 0) or (not needHtfAlign and not htfIsRevShort)

// ONE tolerance for equality, dedupe and clustering, evaluated ONCE per bar as
// a global rather than as a function: f_poolPush is inlined 16 times, so a
// series variable read costs one token there where the expression costs seven.
// ATR-driven as before, with an optional tick floor for quantised instruments
// (§3). At eqTickMin = 0 it is exactly the v5.5 expression, eqTol * atr.
lqTol   = math.max(eqTol * atr, eqTickMin * syminfo.mintick)
lqReact = math.max(lqTol * 2, 0.5 * atr)   // the v5.5 EQH/EQL reaction floor

// ═════════════════════════════════ MODULE 7 · LIQUIDITY REGISTRY (ARCH-2)
// Every meaningful level becomes a POOL with a real lifecycle:
//   CREATED → VALIDATED → RAIDED → REACTION CONFIRMED → CONSUMED
// A raid is BOUND to at most one setup (BUG-10), dies if there is no reaction
// inside reactWin, and dies the moment price closes the wrong side of the
// level (which is what made v2's "in play" test wrong, BUG-9).
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
type Pool
    float  px         = na
    int    kind       = 0
    bool   buySide    = true
    int    bornBar    = na
    int    state      = 0
    int    raidBar    = na
    float  raidExt    = na
    bool   raidStrong = false
    int    brkBar     = na     // bar the level was BROKEN (close beyond) on
    int    bound      = 0      // setup id that claimed this raid (0 = free)
    int    id         = 0      // V5-3: stable identity — a setup binds to THIS pool
    int    touches    = 0      // V5: prior reactions/tests = how real the level is
    int    lastTouch  = na
    int    rq         = 0      // V5-5: raid grade RQ_A / RQ_B / RQ_C
    bool   spent      = false  // an entry was already produced from this pool
    line   ln         = na
    string tag        = ""
    string desc       = ""     // V5.5-E · §7/§8 · descriptors of THIS ONE event
    int    descN      = 0
    // ---- V5.6 · registry bookkeeping. kmask records WHICH level types resolved to
    //      this one price (§5 confluence) and legs how many pivots were fused into it;
    //      both are read by the TARGET RANKING and by the weakest-first prune.
    int    kmask      = 0      // confluence: which level TYPES resolved here
    int    legs       = 1      // how many pivots were fused into this level

var pools = array.new<Pool>()
// a function may not assign to a global scalar, but it may mutate a global
// array — so the id sequence lives in a one-element array
var poolSeq = array.new<int>(1, 0)

// na-safe accessors (see the module note): the object is a parameter, and the
// field is only touched inside an `if` that has already proved it exists.
f_poolSpent(Pool p) =>
    bool sp = false
    if not na(p)
        sp := p.spent
    sp

f_poolTouch(Pool p) =>
    int tc = 0
    if not na(p)
        tc := p.touches
    tc

// V5.2-B4: na-safe read of the bar a pool was BROKEN on (close beyond the
// level). The machine invalidates a setup whose raided pool was reclaimed.
f_poolBrkBar(Pool p) =>
    int bb = na
    if not na(p)
        bb := p.brkBar
    bb

// V5.5-E · §7 / §8 · EVENT IDENTITY AND DESCRIPTORS.
// ONE physical market event = ONE Pool = one id. A single sell-side raid that is
// simultaneously a CRT low sweep, a turtle-soup extreme, a TWS wave-2 and a bear
// trap is ONE liquidity event carrying FOUR DESCRIPTORS — it is not four
// independent confirmations. Descriptors are appended here and printed on the
// dashboard, the debug panel and the entry reason, and they are worth a CAPPED
// context bonus in f_score. They can never pay a single LIQUIDITY point: that
// block is paid once, from this pool's own raid grade, class and touch history.
// That is the anti-inflation rule made structural instead of asserted.
f_evTag(Pool p, string d) =>
    bool added = false
    if not na(p) and d != ""
        if not str.contains(p.desc, d)
            p.desc  := p.desc == "" ? d : p.desc + "+" + d
            p.descN += 1
            added   := true
    added

f_poolDesc(Pool p) =>
    string dd = ""
    if not na(p)
        dd := p.desc
    dd

f_poolDescN(Pool p) =>
    int dn = 0
    if not na(p)
        dn := p.descN
    dn

// V5.5-E7 · na-safe price read. The pool dedupe compares PRICE as well as id,
// because a retired pool's level can re-register under a brand-new id.
f_poolPx(Pool p) =>
    float px = na
    if not na(p)
        px := p.px
    px

f_poolState(Pool p) =>
    int st = -1
    if not na(p)
        st := p.state
    st

// V7.5 · registry lookup by IDENTITY. `go` gates the scan inside (the body is
// inlined at every call site and a ternary around the call would not skip it).
f_poolById(bool go, int id) =>
    Pool r = na
    if go and id != 0 and array.size(pools) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
            Pool p = array.get(pools, i)
            if p.id == id
                r := p
                break
    r


f_poolVisible(int kind) =>
    kind == PK_SES ? false :
         kind == PK_TL ? (showPools and showTL) :
         kind == PK_HTF ? (showPools and htfLiqReg) :
         kind == PK_PW ? (showPools and showPW) :
         kind == PK_EQ ? (showPools and showEq) :
         kind == PK_PD ? (showPools and showPD) :
         showPools

f_poolCol(int kind, bool buySide) =>
    kind == PK_HTF ? color.new(color.fuchsia, 25) :
         kind == PK_TL ? color.new(color.aqua, 35) :
         kind == PK_EQ ? (buySide ? color.new(COL_BEAR, 25) : color.new(COL_BULL, 25)) :
         kind == PK_PD ? color.new(color.yellow, 35) :
         kind == PK_PW ? color.new(color.orange, 25) :
         kind == PK_EXT ? (buySide ? color.new(COL_BEAR, 40) : color.new(COL_BULL, 40)) :
         color.new(COL_NEU, 62)

f_poolStyle(int kind) =>
    kind == PK_EQ ? line.style_dotted : kind == PK_SWING ? line.style_dashed : line.style_solid

// V5.6-L12 · the two statements every registry path repeated. A touch counts
// once per eqMinSep bars; a retired / raided line stops extending and restyles.
f_poolTouchInc(Pool p) =>
    if na(p.lastTouch) or bar_index - p.lastTouch >= eqMinSep
        p.touches   += 1
        p.lastTouch := bar_index
    true

f_lnEnd(line ln, string sty) =>
    line.set_extend(ln, extend.none)
    line.set_x2(ln, bar_index)
    line.set_style(ln, sty)
    true

// registers a pool unless a live pool of the same side already sits there.
// V5.2-B1: a duplicate that OUTRANKS a live un-raided SWING pool upgrades that
// pool in place (kind, tag, px, +touch) instead of being refused. EQH/EQL and
// EXT levels sit by construction at prices where a swing pool already lives,
// so the old refusal meant the registry almost never held engineered or
// external pools at all. Identity (id, bound, bornBar, touches) is preserved,
// and a pool that has already been RAIDED is never re-classified.
// V5.6: the refusal / upgrade decision is UNCHANGED. Two things are added, and
// neither can alter it — the tolerance gains an optional tick floor (0 by
// default → the identical eqTol * atr expression), and the surviving pool
// records the KIND of every level that resolved to it, so "PDH sitting on an
// EQH sitting on a major swing" is one pool that knows it is three things.
f_poolPush(float px, int kind, bool buySide, string tag, int startBar, bool validated, int legs) =>
    bool ok = not na(px)
    if ok and array.size(pools) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
            Pool q = array.get(pools, i)
            if q.state < PS_DONE and q.buySide == buySide and math.abs(q.px - px) <= lqTol
                ok := false
                q.kmask := f_maskAdd(q.kmask, kind)
                q.legs  := math.max(q.legs, legs)
                if kind != PK_SWING and q.kind == PK_SWING and q.state <= PS_VAL
                    q.kind  := kind
                    q.tag   := tag
                    q.px    := px
                    q.state := PS_VAL
                    f_poolTouchInc(q)
                    line.set_y1(q.ln, px)
                    line.set_y2(q.ln, px)
                    line.set_color(q.ln, f_poolVisible(kind) ? f_poolCol(kind, buySide) : color.new(color.gray, 100))
                    line.set_style(q.ln, f_poolStyle(kind))
                    line.set_width(q.ln, kind == PK_EQ or kind == PK_PD or kind == PK_PW ? 2 : 1)
                break
    if ok
        array.set(poolSeq, 0, array.get(poolSeq, 0) + 1)
        Pool p = Pool.new(px = px, kind = kind, buySide = buySide, bornBar = startBar,
             state = validated ? PS_VAL : PS_NEW, tag = tag, id = array.get(poolSeq, 0),
             touches = validated ? 1 : 0, kmask = f_maskAdd(0, kind), legs = legs)
        // the line is always created (never na → no setter guards anywhere) and
        // simply drawn fully transparent when its display group is off
        p.ln := line.new(startBar, px, bar_index + 1, px,
             color = f_poolVisible(kind) ? f_poolCol(kind, buySide) : color.new(color.gray, 100),
             style = f_poolStyle(kind), width = kind == PK_EQ or kind == PK_PD or kind == PK_PW ? 2 : 1,
             extend = extend.right)
        array.push(pools, p)
        // §13/§19 · CAP. v5.5 always evicts slot 0 — the OLDEST pool — which is
        // backwards for liquidity: a previous-day high that has stood untouched
        // all session is the most crowded level on the chart and the first to
        // die. With lqPrunePri ON the weakest un-raided pool goes instead.
        // OFF is the v5.5 eviction order, byte for byte.
        while array.size(pools) > poolMaxN
            int victim = 0
            if lqPrunePri
                float worst = 1e18
                for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
                    Pool o = array.get(pools, i)
                    // a live raid is evidence in flight and is evicted last
                    float w = f_kindPri(o.kind) * 10 + f_maskN(o.kmask) * 6 + math.min(o.touches, 4) * 4 +
                         (o.state == PS_RAID or o.state == PS_REACT ? 100 : 0) + (o.bound != 0 ? 100 : 0) +
                         i * 0.001
                    if w < worst
                        worst  := w
                        victim := i
            Pool v = array.get(pools, victim)
            line.delete(v.ln)
            array.remove(pools, victim)
    ok

// ═══ V5.6-BUDGET · THE REGISTRATION QUEUE ═══════════════════════════════════
// Pine INLINES a user-defined function at every call site before it counts
// tokens. f_poolPush above is a ~690-token body and v5.5 called it fourteen
// times: ~9 700 compiled tokens for one function, and every token added inside
// it cost fourteen. v5.6 tipped that over the 100 256 limit.
//
// The fix is structural, not cosmetic. Every registration now enqueues, and the
// queue is drained through ONE call site just before the lifecycle scan. Order
// is preserved exactly — it is the same calls in the same sequence — and
// nothing between the registrations reads `pools`, so the behaviour is
// identical. One inlined copy instead of sixteen also left enough headroom to
// keep the weakest-first prune, the confluence mask and the fused-leg count,
// all of which had to be stripped when the budget was tight.
var qPx   = array.new<float>()
var qKind = array.new<int>()
var qSide = array.new<bool>()
var qTag  = array.new<string>()
var qBar  = array.new<int>()
var qVal  = array.new<bool>()
var qLeg  = array.new<int>()

f_qPush(float px, int kind, bool buySide, string tag, int startBar, bool validated, int legs) =>
    array.push(qPx, px)
    array.push(qKind, kind)
    array.push(qSide, buySide)
    array.push(qTag, tag)
    array.push(qBar, startBar)
    array.push(qVal, validated)
    array.push(qLeg, legs)
    0

f_qFlush() =>
    if array.size(qPx) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(qPx) - 1
            bool pushed = f_poolPush(array.get(qPx, i), array.get(qKind, i), array.get(qSide, i),
                 array.get(qTag, i), array.get(qBar, i), array.get(qVal, i), array.get(qLeg, i))
        array.clear(qPx)
        array.clear(qKind)
        array.clear(qSide)
        array.clear(qTag)
        array.clear(qBar)
        array.clear(qVal)
        array.clear(qLeg)
    0

// PHASE 7 lifecycle scan — one pass per confirmed bar over the whole registry.
// The `confirmed` guard lives INSIDE the function on purpose: Pine evaluates
// both branches of a ternary, so gating the CALL would still have mutated pool
// state on every intrabar tick.
f_poolScan() =>
    int raided = 0
    if confirmed and array.size(pools) > 0
        for i = array.size(pools) - 1 to 0
            Pool p = array.get(pools, i)
            if p.state == PS_DONE
                if poolLife > 0 and bar_index - p.bornBar > poolLife + 50
                    line.delete(p.ln)
                    array.remove(pools, i)
                continue
            if poolLife > 0 and bar_index - p.bornBar > poolLife
                p.state := PS_DONE
                f_lnEnd(p.ln, line.style_dotted)
                continue
            // ---- penetration handling: RAID (wick, close back) vs BREAK
            //      V4-BUG-10 / V5-5. A raid needs raidMinAtr of real penetration
            //      and is then graded A / B / C from depth and rejection. Below
            //      that depth the bar is a TEST: it raises the pool's quality
            //      (price respected the level) and the pool stays hunting.
            // V5.6-L12 · ONE branch for both sides. `sg` is +1 for buy-side
            // liquidity (above price, taken by the HIGH) and -1 for sell-side
            // (taken by the LOW); `pex` is the candle extreme on the pool's side.
            // Every sign product below is exact (×±1.0), so `sg * (ext - p.px)
            // > 0` IS `high > p.px` / `low < p.px`. The two comparisons whose
            // float rearrangement could differ by an ulp — the rejection leg and
            // the approach band — are kept in their original per-side form.
            float sg  = p.buySide ? 1.0 : -1.0
            float pex = p.buySide ? high : low
            if sg * (pex - p.px) > 0
                if sg * (close - p.px) < 0
                    float pen = sg * (pex - p.px) / atr
                    // §11 · WICK vs BODY, now configurable. Leg 1 is the
                    // v5.5 relative test (the close sits back from the swept
                    // extreme by a fraction of the raid candle's own range);
                    // leg 2 is an optional ABSOLUTE close-back in ATR, which
                    // is what actually kills one-tick pokes on instruments
                    // whose bar ranges swing wildly. Defaults reproduce v5.5.
                    bool  rej = (p.buySide ? close <= high - raidRejFrac * rng : close >= low + raidRejFrac * rng) and
                         (raidBackAtr <= 0 or sg * (p.px - close) >= raidBackAtr * atr)
                    if p.state <= PS_VAL and pen >= raidMinAtr
                        p.state      := PS_RAID
                        p.raidBar    := bar_index
                        p.raidExt    := pex
                        p.raidStrong := rej
                        p.rq         := pen >= raidDeepAtr and rej ? RQ_A : pen >= raidDeepAtr or rej ? RQ_B : RQ_C
                        p.desc       := p.rq == RQ_A ? "DEEP RAID" : p.rq == RQ_B ? "SWEEP" : "WICK"
                        p.descN      := 1
                        raided += 1
                        f_lnEnd(p.ln, line.style_dashed)
                    else if p.state <= PS_VAL
                        p.state := PS_VAL
                        f_poolTouchInc(p)
                    else
                        p.raidExt := p.buySide ? math.max(nz(p.raidExt, high), high) : math.min(nz(p.raidExt, low), low)
                else
                    p.state  := PS_DONE           // BROKEN → expansion, not a raid
                    p.brkBar := bar_index
                    f_lnEnd(p.ln, line.style_dotted)
                    continue
            else if p.state <= PS_VAL and (p.buySide ? high >= p.px - NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr : low <= p.px + NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr)
                p.state := PS_VAL                 // approached and respected
                f_poolTouchInc(p)
            // ---- reaction / staleness: a raid does NOT stay valid forever
            // ═══ V7.7 · F-01 · TWO CLOCKS, NOT ONE ═══════════════════════════════
            //   v7.6 retired an UN-REACTED PS_RAID at `reactWin` (6 bars), and the
            //   `sweepLook` branch below was only reachable from PS_REACT. So the
            //   window the sweepLook tooltip advertises ("a raid can seed a setup for
            //   this many bars", 15) and the `mssWin` the machine's stage 3 → 4 relies
            //   on (12) were both DEAD: every raid that did not produce a chart-wide
            //   displacement inside six bars was retired, and the setup holding it died
            //   with "target pool broken or expired". That killed the most ordinary ICT
            //   sequence there is — raid, retracement, THEN the POI tap and the shift.
            //   The two clocks are separate now and each means what it says:
            //     reactWin  · the REACTION deadline. After it the raid can no longer be
            //                 promoted to PS_REACT — it is a sweep nothing answered.
            //     sweepLook · the CLAIM window. How long a setup may still bind it.
            //   The consumption test (price walked POOL_CONSUME_ATR away) now applies to
            //   both states, which it always should have: a raid price has left behind
            //   is spent whether or not anything reacted to it.
            if p.state == PS_RAID or p.state == PS_REACT
                if sg * (close - p.px) > 0
                    p.state  := PS_DONE               // raid failed → became a break
                    p.brkBar := bar_index             // V5.2-B4: a reclaim IS a break
                else if bar_index - p.raidBar > sweepLook or math.abs(close - p.px) > POOL_CONSUME_ATR * atr
                    p.state := PS_DONE                // claim window closed, or spent by distance
                else if p.state == PS_RAID and bar_index > p.raidBar and bar_index - p.raidBar <= reactWin and (p.buySide ? (dispDnBar or shiftDn) : (dispUpBar or shiftUp))
                    // V5.5-E1 · THE RAID CANDLE CANNOT BE ITS OWN REACTION. v5.4
                    // ran this test on the raid bar too, so a shallow poke whose
                    // candle happened to carry an unrelated internal CHoCH / CISD
                    // was promoted PS_RAID → PS_REACT immediately AND had its raid
                    // grade lifted RQ_C → RQ_B — which is exactly what f_grade
                    // needs for grade A. Same family as V4-BUG-6/7 (an event
                    // confirming itself) and V4-BUG-5 (a global flag paying a
                    // specific setup).
                    // V7.2 BUG-05 · the reaction promotes the pool STATE, never the
                    //   raid GRADE: rq describes the raid candle (depth x rejection)
                    //   and rq >= RQ_B is the grade-A rung's own requirement.
                    p.state    := PS_REACT
    raided

// ---- pivot memory (EQH / EQL construction + reaction depth) -----------------
// V5.6 · §3 / §2 (double + TRIPLE tops). v5.5 held exactly ONE previous pivot
// per side, so equality was only ever tested against the pivot immediately
// before. A triple top whose middle leg overshoots by half an ATR, or a level
// re-tested two pivots later, produced no engineered pool at all — one of the
// reasons the registry rarely held PK_EQ levels (the v5.2-B1 note above says
// the same thing from the other end).
//
// The memory is now a ring of `eqScanN` pivots, each carrying its OWN reaction
// extreme (the deepest pullback since THAT pivot — a single global `sinceHiMin`
// is only correct for the most recent one). ALL stored legs that satisfy
// tolerance + separation + reaction are fused into ONE pool at their mean and
// tagged with the leg count. eqScanN = 1 reduces to the v5.5 expression exactly:
// one slot, one comparison, mean of the pair, tag "EQH" / "EQL".
//
// NON-REPAINTING: `ph` / `pl` are ta.pivothigh/low(pivLen, pivLen), so a leg is
// only ever read pivLen bars after it printed, and the pool is registered on
// the confirming bar with bornBar back-dated to the pivot. No future reference.
var phVal = array.new<float>()
var phBar = array.new<int>()
var phMin = array.new<float>()    // deepest pullback since that pivot
var plVal = array.new<float>()
var plBar = array.new<int>()
var plMax = array.new<float>()
// v5.5's lastPhVal / lastPhBar / lastPlVal / lastPlBar / sinceHiMin /
// sinceLoMax were the one-slot version of the ring above. Nothing outside this
// block ever read them, so they are gone rather than left as write-only state
// (same housekeeping rule as V5.5-E6).
if confirmed
    if array.size(phMin) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(phMin) - 1
            array.set(phMin, i, math.min(array.get(phMin, i), low))
    if array.size(plMax) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(plMax) - 1
            array.set(plMax, i, math.max(array.get(plMax, i), high))

if confirmed and not na(ph)
    int   pbH   = bar_index - pivLen
    float sum   = ph
    int   n     = 1
    int   first = pbH
    if array.size(phVal) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(phVal) - 1
            float v = array.get(phVal, i)
            int   b = array.get(phBar, i)
            // BUG-7 (preserved): ATR tolerance AND time separation AND a real
            // reaction between the legs. The pool sits at the MEAN of the legs,
            // never at the highest of them.
            if math.abs(ph - v) <= lqTol and pbH - b >= eqMinSep
                bool reactH = not eqNeedReact or (not na(array.get(phMin, i)) and math.min(ph, v) - array.get(phMin, i) >= lqReact)
                if reactH
                    sum   += v
                    n     += 1
                    first := math.min(first, b)
    if n >= 2
        f_qPush(sum / n, PK_EQ, true, n > 2 ? "EQH×" + str.tostring(n) : "EQH", first, true, n)
    array.push(phVal, ph)
    array.push(phBar, pbH)
    array.push(phMin, ta.lowest(low, pivLen))
    while array.size(phVal) > eqScanN
        array.shift(phVal)
        array.shift(phBar)
        array.shift(phMin)
    f_qPush(ph, PK_SWING, true, "SH", pbH, false, 1)
if confirmed and not na(pl)
    int   pbL   = bar_index - pivLen
    float sumL  = pl
    int   nL    = 1
    int   firstL = pbL
    if array.size(plVal) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(plVal) - 1
            float v = array.get(plVal, i)
            int   b = array.get(plBar, i)
            if math.abs(pl - v) <= lqTol and pbL - b >= eqMinSep
                bool reactL = not eqNeedReact or (not na(array.get(plMax, i)) and array.get(plMax, i) - math.max(pl, v) >= lqReact)
                if reactL
                    sumL   += v
                    nL     += 1
                    firstL := math.min(firstL, b)
    if nL >= 2
        f_qPush(sumL / nL, PK_EQ, false, nL > 2 ? "EQL×" + str.tostring(nL) : "EQL", firstL, true, nL)
    array.push(plVal, pl)
    array.push(plBar, pbL)
    array.push(plMax, ta.highest(high, pivLen))
    while array.size(plVal) > eqScanN
        array.shift(plVal)
        array.shift(plBar)
        array.shift(plMax)
    f_qPush(pl, PK_SWING, false, "SL", pbL, false, 1)

// external swings, session extremes and PDH / PDL are pre-VALIDATED pools —
// known resting liquidity, not candidates
if confirmed and not na(eph)
    f_qPush(eph, PK_EXT, true, "EXT-H", bar_index - extPiv, true, 1)
if confirmed and not na(epl)
    f_qPush(epl, PK_EXT, false, "EXT-L", bar_index - extPiv, true, 1)
if asiaFresh
    f_qPush(sesAsia.fhi, PK_SES, true,  "Asia H", bar_index, true, 1)
    f_qPush(sesAsia.flo, PK_SES, false, "Asia L", bar_index, true, 1)
if lonFresh
    f_qPush(sesLon.fhi, PK_SES, true,  "Lon H", bar_index, true, 1)
    f_qPush(sesLon.flo, PK_SES, false, "Lon L", bar_index, true, 1)
if nyFresh
    f_qPush(sesNY.fhi, PK_SES, true,  "NY H", bar_index, true, 1)
    f_qPush(sesNY.flo, PK_SES, false, "NY L", bar_index, true, 1)
if confirmed and newDay and timeframe.isintraday
    f_qPush(pdhRaw, PK_PD, true,  "PDH", bar_index, true, 1)
    f_qPush(pdlRaw, PK_PD, false, "PDL", bar_index, true, 1)

// ---- V5.6 · PWH / PWL ------------------------------------------------------
// Registered as ordinary PK_PW pools rather than hand-drawn. v5.6 first shipped
// a bespoke line + label + sweep tracker for these, which duplicated ~250
// compiled tokens of machinery the registry already owns. As pools they inherit
// all of it: raid vs break, penetration grading, the lifecycle, the ◇ mark and
// the MODULE 8B annotation — and they become real targets, which is what a
// previous-week extreme is.
if confirmed and newWeek and pwUsable and not na(pwhRaw) and not na(pwlRaw)
    f_qPush(pwhRaw, PK_PW, true,  "PWH", bar_index, true, 1)
    f_qPush(pwlRaw, PK_PW, false, "PWL", bar_index, true, 1)

// ═══ V7.0 · §7 / §25 · HTF SWING LIQUIDITY ════════════════════════════
// The single most relevant external liquidity an intraday chart has, and v6.4
// tracked none of it. hSwHi / hSwLo are CLOSED HTF pivots (see MODULE 6), so
// registering them here is not a look-ahead: the level existed, closed and was
// confirmed on the HTF before this chart bar opened. Once registered they are
// ordinary pools — same raid grading, same lifecycle, same marking, and they
// outrank everything else in f_kindPri because that is what they are.
if confirmed and newHtfBar and htfLiqReg
    f_qPush(hSwHi, PK_HTF, true,  "HTF-H " + htfTf, bar_index, true, 1)
    f_qPush(hSwLo, PK_HTF, false, "HTF-L " + htfTf, bar_index, true, 1)

// ═══ V7.0 · §22 · DYNAMIC TRENDLINE LIQUIDITY ════════════════════════
// The v5.4 trendline engine was removed in v5.5 (header R1) because it drew
// decoration and then let a BREAK of that decoration fire a trade. This one is
// built the other way round and can never emit anything by itself:
//   1 · anchors are two CONFIRMED external pivots of the same side, so the line
//       is only ever drawn behind price, never projected off an unconfirmed leg;
//   2 · the slope has to be the right way round — descending highs for a
//       resistance line, ascending lows for a support line. A line through two
//       random pivots is not a trendline;
//   3 · it must be TOUCHED tlMinTouch times. Untouched geometry holds no stops,
//       and stops are the only reason a trendline matters;
//   4 · only a SWEEP registers it — taken and closed back inside. At that moment
//       the projected price is frozen and pushed into the registry as a PK_TL
//       pool, which is what makes a moving line into a fixed liquidity event.
// From there it is indistinguishable from any other pool: it must still be
// raided by a setup that locked it, still needs a POI, an MSS, displacement and
// an FVG. "A trendline break alone must NOT generate a trade" is structural.
f_trendline() =>
    var float tlHp1 = na
    var float tlHp2 = na
    var int   tlHb1 = na
    var int   tlHb2 = na
    var int   tlHn  = 0
    var bool  tlHhit = false
    var float tlLp1 = na
    var float tlLp2 = na
    var int   tlLb1 = na
    var int   tlLb2 = na
    var int   tlLn  = 0
    var bool  tlLhit = false
    if showTL and confirmed
        // V7.7 · F-08 · A NEW ANCHOR IS NOT A NEW TRENDLINE. v7.6 zeroed the touch
        //   counter on EVERY new external pivot, and at extPiv = 12 those print often
        //   enough that `tlMinTouch` (2) was almost never reached before the reset —
        //   so PK_TL pools were effectively never registered, and a whole §22 engine
        //   with its own kind priority and score allowance sat dead on the chart.
        //   Rolling the anchor forward along a line of the SAME slope is a re-anchor,
        //   not a new line: the respect price has already shown it carries over. Only
        //   a slope REVERSAL is a different line and starts the count again.
        if not na(eph)
            bool tlHsame = not na(tlHp1) and eph < tlHp1     // still making lower highs
            tlHp2 := tlHp1
            tlHb2 := tlHb1
            tlHp1 := eph
            tlHb1 := bar_index - extPiv
            tlHn  := tlHsame ? tlHn : 0
            tlHhit := false
        if not na(epl)
            bool tlLsame = not na(tlLp1) and epl > tlLp1     // still making higher lows
            tlLp2 := tlLp1
            tlLb2 := tlLb1
            tlLp1 := epl
            tlLb1 := bar_index - extPiv
            tlLn  := tlLsame ? tlLn : 0
            tlLhit := false
    tlHy = showTL and not na(tlHp2) and not na(tlHp1) and tlHb1 > tlHb2 and tlHp1 < tlHp2 ? tlHp1 + (tlHp1 - tlHp2) / (tlHb1 - tlHb2) * (bar_index - tlHb1) : na
    tlLy = showTL and not na(tlLp2) and not na(tlLp1) and tlLb1 > tlLb2 and tlLp1 > tlLp2 ? tlLp1 + (tlLp1 - tlLp2) / (tlLb1 - tlLb2) * (bar_index - tlLb1) : na
    if confirmed and not na(tlHy) and not tlHhit
        if high >= tlHy - NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr and high <= tlHy + NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr and close < tlHy
            tlHn += 1
        else if high > tlHy and close < tlHy and tlHn >= tlMinTouch
            tlHhit := true
            f_qPush(tlHy, PK_TL, true, "TL-H ×" + str.tostring(tlHn), bar_index, true, 1)
    if confirmed and not na(tlLy) and not tlLhit
        if low <= tlLy + NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr and low >= tlLy - NEAR_LVL_ATR * atr and close > tlLy
            tlLn += 1
        else if low < tlLy and close > tlLy and tlLn >= tlMinTouch
            tlLhit := true
            f_qPush(tlLy, PK_TL, false, "TL-L ×" + str.tostring(tlLn), bar_index, true, 1)
    0
tlN = f_trendline()

// V5.3-C5 · registration runs first so the scan SEES this bar's new levels.
// That ordering does not itself prevent same-bar raids — pivot construction
// does: a SWING/EQ pool confirms only after pivLen lower/higher bars, so the
// confirming bar cannot be beyond it. A same-bar raid of a freshly frozen
// session or PDH/PDL level is a REAL event and is allowed to register.
poolFlush = f_qFlush()
poolRaids = f_poolScan()

// ---- one pass for LIQUIDITY INTENT (Phase 6) + this bar's pool events -------
float  tgtBuy     = na     // best live buy-side pool ABOVE price  → bearish intent
float  tgtSell    = na     // best live sell-side pool BELOW price → bullish intent
// V7.0 · §7 · the RANK of the pool currently holding each slot. v6.4 compared
// prices ("is this one nearer?"), which is why a three-bar-old minor swing
// outranked a previous-week high standing four ATR away. The comparison is now
// LEVEL QUALITY minus a distance penalty, and a pool further than tgtMaxAtr is
// not a draw on price at all, however good it looks.
float  tgtBuyW    = -1e18
float  tgtSellW   = -1e18
// V7.0 · §38 · INDUCEMENT. The most recent raid of an INTERNAL (minor swing)
// pool on each side, harvested from the loop that already walks the registry.
// A setup whose external raid was preceded by one of these took the liquidity
// that was engineered to pay for it.
int    indLoBar   = -1
int    indHiBar   = -1
int    indLoId    = 0
int    indHiId    = 0
string tgtBuyTag  = ""
string tgtSellTag = ""
Pool   tgtBuyPool  = na    // V5-3: the machine locks the OBJECT, not the price
Pool   tgtSellPool = na
bool   sesHiBroke = false
bool   sesLoBroke = false
bool   poolBrkUp  = false
bool   poolBrkDn  = false
// V5.5-E4 · what the liquidity-run engine needs, harvested from the loop that
// already walks the registry — no second scan
int    brkUpKind  = -1
int    brkDnKind  = -1
float  brkUpPx    = na
float  brkDnPx    = na
int    brkUpId    = 0      // V7.4 F-04 · identity of the pool broken this bar
int    brkDnId    = 0
if array.size(pools) > 0
    for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
        Pool p = array.get(pools, i)
        // §38 · inducement bookkeeping
        if (p.state == PS_RAID or p.state == PS_REACT) and p.kind == PK_SWING and not na(p.raidBar)
            if p.buySide and p.raidBar > indHiBar
                indHiBar := p.raidBar
                indHiId  := p.id
            if not p.buySide and p.raidBar > indLoBar
                indLoBar := p.raidBar
                indLoId  := p.id
        if p.state <= PS_VAL and not p.spent and p.touches >= poolMinTouch
            float dAtr = math.abs(p.px - close) / math.max(atr, syminfo.mintick)
            if dAtr <= tgtMaxAtr
                float w = f_kindPri(p.kind) * 10.0 + f_maskN(p.kmask) * 5.0 +
                     math.min(p.touches, 4) * 4.0 + p.legs * 2.0 - dAtr * tgtDistW
                if p.buySide and p.px > close and w > tgtBuyW
                    tgtBuyW    := w
                    tgtBuy     := p.px
                    tgtBuyTag  := p.tag
                    tgtBuyPool := p
                if not p.buySide and p.px < close and w > tgtSellW
                    tgtSellW    := w
                    tgtSell     := p.px
                    tgtSellTag  := p.tag
                    tgtSellPool := p
        if p.brkBar == bar_index
            if p.buySide
                poolBrkUp  := true
                brkUpKind  := p.kind
                brkUpPx    := p.px
                brkUpId    := p.id
                sesHiBroke := sesHiBroke or p.kind == PK_SES
            else
                poolBrkDn  := true
                brkDnKind  := p.kind
                brkDnPx    := p.px
                brkDnId    := p.id
                sesLoBroke := sesLoBroke or p.kind == PK_SES

// ---- bindable raid lookup (the setup machine's liquidity stage) --------------
// dir 1 (long) wants a SELL-side pool that has been raided; dir -1 wants a
// buy-side one. Freshest qualifying raid wins; already-bound raids are skipped.
// Returns the POOL OBJECT, never an array index: f_poolScan can remove pools,
// which would silently re-point a stored index at a different pool.
// V7.2 BUG-07 · the trap engine's first ingredient claims "the broken level was
//   TRACKED liquidity". v7.1 only asked whether SOME pool of that side closed
//   through on the arming bar and never compared it with the level that armed the
//   trap. These two say it properly, at registry tolerance.
tPoolU = poolBrkUp and math.abs(brkUpPx - brkUpLvl) <= lqTol
tPoolD = poolBrkDn and math.abs(brkDnPx - brkDnLvl) <= lqTol

f_findRaid(int dir) =>
    Pool   bp   = na
    float  bpx  = na
    int    bbar = na
    float  bext = na
    bool   bstr = false
    int    bknd = 0
    string btag = ""
    int    bid  = 0
    int    brq  = 0
    // V7.7 · F-05 · `bbnd` is gone. The one consumer was f_gradeZone's ownership test,
    //   and that test now lives inside f_raidAtZone where it belongs — it has to be
    //   applied per CANDIDATE pool, not to whichever raid happened to be freshest.
    //   Write-only state does not survive in this file (the V5.5-E6 housekeeping rule).
    if array.size(pools) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
            Pool p = array.get(pools, i)
            bool sideOk = dir == 1 ? not p.buySide : p.buySide
            if sideOk and (p.state == PS_RAID or p.state == PS_REACT) and not p.spent and f_recent(p.raidBar, sweepLook)
                if na(bp) or p.raidBar > bbar
                    bp   := p
                    bpx  := p.px
                    bbar := p.raidBar
                    bext := p.raidExt
                    bstr := p.raidStrong
                    bknd := p.kind
                    btag := p.tag
                    bid  := p.id
                    brq  := p.rq
    [bp, bpx, bbar, bext, bstr, bknd, btag, bid, brq]

// ANY live raid (bound or free). Zone grading, the confluence producers, the
// dashboard and the debug panel read these. The state machine does NOT: it binds
// to the POOL OBJECT it locked as its target at state 2, so "the freshest raid
// anywhere on this side" can never satisfy a setup any more (V4-BUG-3).
[anyLoPool, anyLoPx, anyLoBar, anyLoExt, anyLoStrong, anyLoKind, anyLoTag, anyLoId, anyLoRq] = f_findRaid(1)
[anyHiPool, anyHiPx, anyHiBar, anyHiExt, anyHiStrong, anyHiKind, anyHiTag, anyHiId, anyHiRq] = f_findRaid(-1)

// V7.4 E2-A · "Any qualifying pool" target mode. The freshest FREE, unspent raid
// on the side that happened strictly AFTER the lock bar and whose level priority
// clears tgtMinPri. Returns the POOL OBJECT (never an index), like f_findRaid.
f_anyRaid(int dir, int minBar, int minPri) =>
    Pool bp   = na
    int  bbar = na
    if array.size(pools) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
            Pool p = array.get(pools, i)
            if (dir == 1 ? not p.buySide : p.buySide) and (p.state == PS_RAID or p.state == PS_REACT) and p.bound == 0 and not p.spent and p.raidBar > minBar and f_recent(p.raidBar, sweepLook) and f_kindPri(p.kind) >= minPri
                if na(bp) or p.raidBar > bbar
                    bp   := p
                    bbar := p.raidBar
    bp
tgtAny = tgtMode != "Best pool (v7.3)"

// convenience flags used by the score, the S/D producer and the dashboard
sslInPlay = not na(anyLoPool)
bslInPlay = not na(anyHiPool)
sslStrong = sslInPlay and anyLoStrong
bslStrong = bslInPlay and anyHiStrong

// ---- the ◆ / ◈ sweep marks v2 drew: kept, now sourced from the registry -----
eqlSwept  = confirmed and sslInPlay and anyLoBar == bar_index and anyLoKind == PK_EQ
eqhSwept  = confirmed and bslInPlay and anyHiBar == bar_index and anyHiKind == PK_EQ
sesLoRaid = confirmed and sslInPlay and anyLoBar == bar_index and anyLoKind == PK_SES
// V5.6 · the raid marks v5.5 never had: EXTERNAL liquidity (previous day /
// previous week / external swing) taken THIS bar. Registry-sourced, so a mark
// only prints where a real pool moved to PS_RAID; plus the drawn-only PWH/PWL
// sweep — PWH/PWL are ordinary PK_PW pools, so they arrive through the same
// path as everything else.
lqExtHiMark  = lqRaidChar and confirmed and bslInPlay and anyHiBar == bar_index and (anyHiKind == PK_PD or anyHiKind == PK_PW or anyHiKind == PK_EXT)
lqExtLoMark  = lqRaidChar and confirmed and sslInPlay and anyLoBar == bar_index and (anyLoKind == PK_PD or anyLoKind == PK_PW or anyLoKind == PK_EXT)
sesHiRaid = confirmed and bslInPlay and anyHiBar == bar_index and anyHiKind == PK_SES

// ════════════════════════ MODULE 8 · DEALING RANGE · PREMIUM / DISCOUNT · OTE
// External swings by default (the real impulse leg); internal is the fallback
// until an external pair exists. OTE only projects against a FRESH leg.
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
useExtRng = rangeSrc == "External" and not na(extRes) and not na(extSup)
// V7.5 · §23 · the range is the CURRENT impulse leg, not "any old high + low":
// bull = protected low → the swing high made AFTER it; bear = protected high →
// the swing low made after it. The latest-pivot pair is the fallback only while
// no protected swing exists (first leg) or the leg has not printed its extreme.
legBull   = useExtRng and extDir > 0 and not na(protLowE)  and sExt.swHiBar > sExt.protLoBar
legBear   = useExtRng and extDir < 0 and not na(protHighE) and sExt.swLoBar > sExt.protHiBar
rngHi     = legBear ? protHighE : useExtRng ? extRes : lastRes
rngLo     = legBull ? protLowE  : useExtRng ? extSup : lastSup
rngHiBar  = legBear ? sExt.protHiBar : useExtRng ? sExt.swHiBar : lastResBar
rngLoBar  = legBull ? sExt.protLoBar : useExtRng ? sExt.swLoBar : lastSupBar
rangeOK   = not na(rngHi) and not na(rngLo) and rngHi > rngLo
// v7.3 · the four na-guards that stood here were dead: a Struct writes swHi and
// swHiBar in one block, so rangeOK (which proves rngHi / rngLo) already proves
// both bar stamps, and a false rangeOK short-circuits the chain.
bullRange = rangeOK and rngHiBar > rngLoBar
bearRange = rangeOK and rngLoBar > rngHiBar
rngW    = rangeOK ? rngHi - rngLo : na
eqLvl   = rangeOK ? rngLo + rngW / 2 : na
pdPos   = rangeOK ? (close - rngLo) / rngW : na
oteBuyTop  = rangeOK ? rngHi - OTE_1 * rngW : na
oteBuyBot  = rangeOK ? rngHi - OTE_2 * rngW : na
oteSellBot = rangeOK ? rngLo + OTE_1 * rngW : na
oteSellTop = rangeOK ? rngLo + OTE_2 * rngW : na
inOTEbuy   = bullRange and close <= oteBuyTop  and close >= oteBuyBot
inOTEsell  = bearRange and close >= oteSellBot and close <= oteSellTop
// premium / discount EXTREMES — the only place a counter-trend fade belongs
inDiscExt  = rangeOK and pdPos <= PD_EXTREME
inPremExt  = rangeOK and pdPos >= 1 - PD_EXTREME
discOK     = not rangeOK or pdPos <= 0.5
premOK     = not rangeOK or pdPos >= 0.5

// ═══════ MODULE 8B · LIQUIDITY MARKING LAYER — REMOVED IN v7.10 ════════
// TradingView returned CE10117 again: 103 567 of the 100 256 cap, over by 3 311.
// This is the lineage's own standing first lever (≈ 3 960 compiled, measured at
// v6.3) and it was always the right one: the whole module is ANNOTATION. It read
// the registry and wrote labels — BSL / SSL, strength stars, EXT / INT, cluster
// text, lifecycle words — and nothing in it was consumed by a producer, a gate, a
// grade, the score, the dedupe, a state machine or the risk ladder.
//
// WHAT IS NOT LOST. The pool REGISTRY is MODULE 7 and is untouched: every pool
// still has its line, its colour by kind, its style, its width, its lifecycle
// (dashed when raided, dotted when broken), its raid grading and its descriptors.
// The target BSL / SSL labels, the  external-raid marks, the ◆ / ◈ sweep marks
// and the dashboard's liquidity rows all live elsewhere and still draw.
// Gone with it: f_lqIsExt, f_lqScore, f_lqClass, f_lqState, f_lqCluster, f_lqClear,
// f_lqSideCol, f_lsStars, f_lsMin, LQ_EXT_PAD, the LS_* strength constants, the
// lqLbs array and the Pool fields lstr / lscore / cluN / cluRep / isExt (kmask and
// legs stay — the TARGET RANKING and the registry prune read them).
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// ---- §9 · session H / L line retirement — RETIRED IN v7.5 (reclaim R5) ------
// The registry pool at the same price already stops extending and restyles
// (dashed when raided, dotted when broken), so the swept level is never shown as
// resting; the duplicate annotation on f_sesTrack's line was display-only.

// ═══════════════ MODULE 9 · SUPPLY / DEMAND + FVG (LIFECYCLE + TIERS)
// ARCH-4 / Phase 9 / Phase 10. Every POI carries a lifecycle state and a
// quality tier. TIER 3 is VISUAL INFORMATION ONLY — it can never produce a
// trade above the "Minimum POI tier" setting. A 50 % tap is SETUP
// PREPARATION, never an entry. The whole update loop is confirmed-gated
// (BUG-4) and nothing here consumes a zone — only the TRADE ENGINE can
// (BUG-5).
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
type Zone
    box    bx
    line   mid
    bool   isSupply
    float  top         = na    // V5.4-D1: geometry cache — the box's coords never
    float  bot         = na    //          change after creation, so hot loops read these
    int    state       = 0     // 0 fresh · 1 touched · 2 fifty · 3 filled · 4 dead
    int    tier        = 3
    int    bornBar     = na
    int    touchBar    = -1
    int    tap50Bar    = -1
    bool   signaled    = false
    bool   flipped     = false
    bool   isFvg       = true
    float  deepest     = na    // deepest penetration reached (fill tracking)
    bool   liqBeforeFail = false
    // ── V7.6 · F-04 · the liquidity event AT this zone, recorded IN FULL rather than
    //    as a bare flag, so a zone that later FAILS can be re-stamped with a real
    //    provenance chain instead of losing its identity for good. A BREAKER with a
    //    proven raid, displacement and structure shift behind it is STRONGER evidence
    //    than the raw gap the S/D producer trades today — and f_paRank already says so.
    bool   failBuy     = false // the recorded raid was buy-side (took highs)
    int    failId      = 0
    int    failBar     = na
    float  failExt     = na
    float  failPx      = na
    int    failRq      = 0
    int    failKind    = 0
    string failTag     = ""
    int    id          = 0     // V5: stable identity, so a POI can be deduped
    // V7.7 · F-02 · THE SETUP THAT HOLDS THIS ZONE AS ITS POI (0 = free), exactly the
    //   `Pool.bound` field and exactly the same contract. The per-side cap used to
    //   evict the OLDEST zone without asking whether a live setup was standing on it.
    //   Pine keeps the object alive through the setup's reference, but f_regrade only
    //   ever walks the `zones` array — so an evicted POI stopped decaying: its tier
    //   froze at whatever it last read, f_zoneDead (tier > minTier) could never fire,
    //   state / mitN stopped counting, and the machine went on to execute an array that
    //   had already been filled and was no longer even drawn. A bound zone is that
    //   setup's evidence; it is not the cap's to throw away for being old.
    int    bound       = 0
    int    tier0       = 3     // tier awarded at CREATION — the re-grade starts here
    // V7.6 · F-05 · the WORST tier this zone has ever held. f_regrade rebuilt its
    //   verdict from tier0 every bar, so the state / mitN penalties ratcheted (those
    //   fields are monotone) but the HTF and STRUCTURE penalties did not: a T1 demand
    //   zone downgraded when the HTF flipped bearish came straight back to T1 when the
    //   HTF flipped again — exactly the transient re-promotion f_regrade's own comment
    //   promised to prevent. The ratchet is explicit now, and only a deliberate
    //   re-classification (the IFVG promotion, a flip) may reset it.
    int    tierW       = 3
    string kindTxt     = ""
    // ── V7.0 · §19 · PROVENANCE. A zone must know which physical events made it.
    //    v6.4 graded a zone T1 from GLOBAL flags — "some raid is in play on this
    //    side" and "some MSS happened in the last ten bars" — which are exactly
    //    the unrelated-event confluences §2 forbids. These four fields record the
    //    events that were actually AT this zone, and f_gradeZone now checks them.
    int    pa          = 0     // PD-array class (PA_FVG / PA_OB / PA_BRK / …)
    int    srcPoolId   = 0     // the liquidity pool whose raid produced it
    int    srcRaidBar  = na
    int    srcMssBar   = na
    int    srcDispBar  = na
    // ── V7.5 · the REST of the chain, so the S/D producer never borrows anything
    float  srcRaidExt  = na    // the raid WICK (the S/D producer's stop candidate)
    float  srcRaidPx   = na    // the pool level (price dedupe)
    int    srcRaidRq   = 0     // raid grade A / B / C of THAT raid
    int    srcRaidKind = 0
    string srcRaidTag  = ""
    bool   srcMssCh    = false // the owned MSS was a CHoCH (else CISD)
    int    srcDispQ    = 0     // f_dispQ of the leg that made the zone
    bool   srcIdm      = false // inducement taken before the owned raid
    float  legExt      = na    // the leg's extreme, frozen at the first touch (zone OTE)
    int    mitN        = 0     // §15 · discrete mitigations: FRESH 0 → FIRST 1 → SECOND 2 → DEEP 3+
    bool   inZ         = false
    bool   ifvgPend    = false // §14 · a FAILED FVG awaiting its hold from the other side

var zones = array.new<Zone>()
var zoneSeq = array.new<int>(1, 0)

f_tierTxt(int t) => t == 1 ? "T1" : t == 2 ? "T2" : "T3"

// na-safe zone accessors, same reason as the pool ones in MODULE 7
f_zoneDead(Zone z) =>
    bool dead = false
    if not na(z)
        dead := z.signaled or z.tier > minTier
    dead

// V5.3-C1: na-safe read of a zone's LIVE (re-graded) tier. The machine
// refreshes its setup's poiTier from this every confirmed bar, so the grade,
// the hTier gate and the debug panel judge the tier the POI HAS, not the tier
// it had when it was tapped. na → 3 (worst), never a promotion.
f_zoneTier(Zone z) =>
    int zt = 3
    if not na(z)
        zt := z.tier
    zt

// V5.5-E2 · na-safe LIVE freshness of a zone. v5.4's S/D producer scored
// freshness from nbFresh / nsFresh — the NEXT buy / sell POI from MODULE 9's
// scan, which is not necessarily the zone being traded. Surviving V4-BUG-5 leak.
f_zoneFresh(Zone z) =>
    bool fr = false
    if not na(z)
        fr := z.state <= 1
    fr

f_zoneSrcPool(Zone z) =>
    int pid = 0
    if not na(z)
        pid := z.srcPoolId
    pid

// V7.7 · F-10 · na-safe read of the SETUP that is standing on this zone, same
//   contract as f_zoneSrcPool. Read by the producer priority rule in MODULE 15.
f_zoneBound(Zone z) =>
    int zb = 0
    if not na(z)
        zb := z.bound
    zb


// ═══ V7.0 · §19 · TIER GRADING IS CAUSAL NOW ═════════════════════════
// WHAT WAS WRONG. v6.4 read `bslInPlay` ("a buy-side raid exists somewhere on
// the chart") and `recentMssDn` ("a bearish shift happened within ten bars").
// Neither had to be anywhere near the zone being graded, and neither had to be
// in the right ORDER, so a supply zone printed T1 off a raid four hundred
// points away and a shift that pre-dated it. That is the exact failure §2
// names: confluence standing in for causality. T1 is the tier the whole grade
// ladder and the minTier gate are built on, so this one substitution was
// mis-pricing every downstream decision.
//
// WHAT IT DOES NOW (V7.5). The raid wick has to sit at the zone's ORIGIN side
// (inside the array, or ≤ sdRaidAtr beyond its far edge; poiBindAtr slack on the
// near edge) and still be live; the shift has to come AFTER that raid, no later
// than the displacement, and break a level that formed around the raid. The
// zone then records the WHOLE chain (f_zoneProv), so the provenance can be
// audited in the debug panel rather than asserted here.
// ═══ V7.7 · F-05 · THE RAID AT THIS ZONE, NOT THE FRESHEST RAID ON THE SIDE ═══════
//   v7.6 read the zone's liquidity from anyHi* / anyLo*, which f_findRaid fills with
//   the single MOST RECENT raid on that side. The registry routinely holds several
//   live raids per side (PS_RAID and PS_REACT both qualify), so whenever the raid that
//   physically sits at this array was not also the newest one, `liq` came back false
//   and the zone was born T3 — and since v7.6's `tierW` ratchet forbids promotion, a
//   genuinely A+ array was made permanently untradeable by nothing more than the
//   arrival order of an unrelated sweep somewhere else on the chart.
//   The test is now a search over the registry, scored the way the evidence ranks:
//   raid GRADE first, recency only as the tie-break. Same spatial rule as before
//   (inside the array, or up to sdRaidAtr beyond the ORIGIN edge, poiBindAtr on the
//   near edge) and the same v7.6 F-16 ownership rule — a raid another setup has
//   already bound, or one that has been spent, is not this zone's evidence.
f_raidAtZone(bool isSupply, float zTop, float zBot) =>
    Pool best = na
    if array.size(pools) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(pools) - 1
            Pool p = array.get(pools, i)
            if (isSupply ? p.buySide : not p.buySide) and (p.state == PS_RAID or p.state == PS_REACT) and p.bound == 0 and not p.spent and not na(p.raidExt) and f_recent(p.raidBar, sweepLook)
                bool inside = isSupply
                     ? p.raidExt <= zTop + sdRaidAtr * atr and p.raidExt >= zBot - poiBindAtr * atr
                     : p.raidExt <= zTop + poiBindAtr * atr and p.raidExt >= zBot - sdRaidAtr * atr
                if inside
                    bool better = na(best)
                    if not better
                        better := p.rq > best.rq or (p.rq == best.rq and nz(p.raidBar, -1) > nz(best.raidBar, -1))
                    if better
                        best := p
    best

f_gradeZone(bool isSupply, float zTop, float zBot) =>
    Pool  rp   = f_raidAtZone(isSupply, zTop, zBot)
    bool  liq  = not na(rp)
    // na-SAFE, the module's own rule: a UDT field is only ever touched inside an `if`
    // that has already proved the object exists. A ternary would not do — Pine
    // evaluates BOTH branches, so `liq ? rp.raidExt : na` throws the moment there is
    // no raid at this array, which is the common case.
    float rpx  = na
    int   rbar = na
    if liq
        rpx  := rp.raidExt
        rbar := rp.raidBar
    int   chB  = isSupply ? nz(iChDnBar, -1) : nz(iChUpBar, -1)
    int   ciB  = isSupply ? nz(cisdDnBar, -1) : nz(cisdUpBar, -1)
    int   mbar = chB > ciB ? chB : ciB
    // §7 · CHRONOLOGY. raid < MSS ≤ displacement (= bar_index - 1), and the level
    //   the MSS broke formed AROUND the raid, not long before it — the machine's
    //   own `rel` test, applied to the zone's chain.
    int   lvlB = mbar == chB ? (isSupply ? nz(sInt.evBarDn, -1) : nz(sInt.evBarUp, -1)) : (isSupply ? nz(bullRunBar, -1) : nz(bearRunBar, -1))
    // V7.6 · F-06 · SPATIAL, NOT ONLY CHRONOLOGICAL. v7.5 constrained WHEN the shift
    //   happened (after the raid, at or before the displacement) and how old the level
    //   it broke was — but never WHERE it happened. A CHoCH thirty ATR away still
    //   promoted this array to T1, and T1 is what minTier, f_grade's A+ rung and the
    //   whole S/D producer are built on: the last global flag deciding a tier. The
    //   level the shift broke must sit AT the zone, and must be separated from the raid
    //   wick by MSS_MIN_SEP_ATR — the machine's own stage-5 test (a shift that IS the
    //   sweep candle is not a shift), applied to the zone's chain.
    float mlvl = mbar == chB ? (isSupply ? sInt.evLvlDn : sInt.evLvlUp) : (isSupply ? bullRunOpen : bearRunOpen)
    bool  mAt  = not na(mlvl) and mlvl <= zTop + sdRaidAtr * atr and mlvl >= zBot - sdRaidAtr * atr and
         not na(rpx) and math.abs(mlvl - rpx) >= MSS_MIN_SEP_ATR * atr
    bool  mss  = liq and mAt and mbar > nz(rbar, 99999999) and mbar <= bar_index - 1 and lvlB >= nz(rbar, 0) - mssWin
    bool  htfA = isSupply ? (htfDir <= 0) : (htfDir >= 0)
    bool  strc = isSupply ? structDir <= 0 : structDir >= 0
    bool  disp = isSupply ? dispDnBar[1] : dispUpBar[1]
    int t = 3
    // V7.2 BUG-03 · the global chart-range OTE is gone from this rung. It was a
    //   dead disjunct (the OTE band is a subset of the half premOK/discOK already
    //   demands) and it imported the chart's dealing range into a zone's grade.
    if liq and disp and mss and htfA and (isSupply ? premOK : discOK)
        t := 1
    else if disp and strc
        t := 2
    // V7.7 · F-05 · the POOL itself travels with the verdict, so the provenance stamp
    //   below records the raid this grade was actually built on.
    [t, rp, mss ? mbar : -1, mss and mbar == chB]

// V7.5 · §6 · PROVENANCE IS STAMPED ONCE, HERE, AND ONLY WHEN THE RAID WAS AT THE
// ZONE. v7.4 wrote srcPoolId from the freshest raid on the side whether or not
// `liq` held, so a zone "owned" a raid four hundred points away, and the S/D
// producer then traded that ownership. Every field the producer will later read
// (stop, grade, class, MSS, displacement, inducement, leg) is copied now, so it
// can never drift as the registry moves on.
// V7.7 · F-05 · the raid arrives as the POOL OBJECT f_gradeZone actually graded on,
//   not as a side-indexed read of "the freshest raid". `liq` stays a separate argument
//   because the ORDER BLOCK re-runs the containment test on its own edges (v7.6 F-03)
//   and may legitimately refuse the same pool.
f_zoneProv(Zone z, bool isSupply, Pool rp, bool liq, int mb, bool mch, int dq) =>
    if liq and not na(rp)
        z.srcPoolId   := rp.id
        z.srcRaidBar  := rp.raidBar
        z.srcRaidExt  := rp.raidExt
        z.srcRaidPx   := rp.px
        z.srcRaidRq   := rp.rq
        z.srcRaidKind := rp.kind
        z.srcRaidTag  := rp.tag
        int idB = isSupply ? indHiBar : indLoBar
        int idI = isSupply ? indHiId  : indLoId
        z.srcIdm      := useInduce and idB >= 0 and idI != z.srcPoolId and idB < z.srcRaidBar and z.srcRaidBar - idB <= induceLook
    z.srcMssBar  := mb >= 0 ? mb : int(na)
    z.srcMssCh   := mch
    z.srcDispBar := bar_index - 1
    z.srcDispQ   := dq
    z.legExt     := isSupply ? low : high
    true

// V4-BUG-11 / V5-6 · POI QUALITY IS DYNAMIC.
// v4 graded a zone once, inside f_newZone, and that tier was then trusted
// forever — a T1 demand zone stayed T1 after the HTF flipped bearish, after
// external structure broke down and after 90 % of it had been eaten. The live
// tier is now recomputed from the CREATION tier every confirmed bar:
//   · 50 % mitigated  → never better than T2   (the discount is half gone)
//   · fully filled    → T3, visual only        (the imbalance no longer exists)
//   · HTF now opposes → one tier worse
//   · structure now opposes → one tier worse
// The tier can only ever get WORSE than the one the zone was born with, which is
// what stops a stale POI from being re-promoted by a transient alignment.
f_regrade(Zone z) =>
    int t = z.tier0
    if z.state >= 2
        t := t < 2 ? 2 : t
    if z.state >= 3
        t := 3
    // V7.5 · §15 · lifecycle: a SECOND discrete mitigation is never better than
    //   T2, a third (DEEP) is T3 — the array has been used.
    if z.mitN >= 2
        t := t < 2 ? 2 : t
    if z.mitN >= 3
        t := 3
    if not (z.isSupply ? htfDir <= 0 : htfDir >= 0)
        t := t >= 3 ? 3 : t + 1
    if not (z.isSupply ? structDir <= 0 : structDir >= 0)
        t := t >= 3 ? 3 : t + 1
    // V7.6 · F-05 · THE RATCHET, MADE STRUCTURAL. Without this the two lines above are
    //   reversible and the comment at the head of this function is false.
    if t > z.tierW
        z.tierW := t
    t := z.tierW
    if t != z.tier
        z.tier := t
        if showZones and showTier
            box.set_text(z.bx, (z.kindTxt != "" ? z.kindTxt : z.isSupply ? "SUPPLY" : "DEMAND") + " " + f_tierTxt(t))
    t

// The box IS the zone's data container, so a zone is ALWAYS created and
// `showZones` only controls whether it is visible (BUG-6 / Phase 28).
f_newZone(bool isSupply, float top, float bot, int tier, string kind, int lft, bool isFvg) =>
    c   = isSupply ? COL_BEAR : COL_BULL
    txt = showZones ? (kind != "" ? kind : (isSupply ? "SUPPLY" : "DEMAND")) + (showTier ? " " + f_tierTxt(tier) : "") : ""
    x1  = lft != 0 ? lft : bar_index - 2
    bx  = box.new(x1, top, bar_index + 1, bot,
         border_color = showZones ? color.new(c, tier == 1 ? 35 : 55) : color.new(c, 100),
         bgcolor = showZones ? color.new(c, tier == 1 ? 90 : 93) : color.new(c, 100),
         text = txt, text_color = color.new(c, showZones ? 0 : 100), text_size = size.small,
         text_halign = text.align_right,
         text_valign = isSupply ? text.align_top : text.align_bottom)
    md  = line.new(x1, (top + bot) / 2, bar_index + 1, (top + bot) / 2,
         color = color.new(c, showZones ? 72 : 100), style = line.style_dotted)
    array.set(zoneSeq, 0, array.get(zoneSeq, 0) + 1)
    Zone.new(bx = bx, mid = md, isSupply = isSupply, tier = tier, tier0 = tier, tierW = tier,
         id = array.get(zoneSeq, 0), bornBar = bar_index, isFvg = isFvg, kindTxt = kind,
         top = top, bot = bot)

// V6.5-O6 · THE FLIP, WRITTEN ONCE. The supply→demand and demand→supply
// violation blocks were mirror images: same fourteen field writes, same six
// drawing calls, differing only in the target side, the colour, the ▲/▼ arrow
// and the caption alignment. `toSup` carries all four. Identical output.
f_zoneFlip(Zone z, bool toSup, bool isBrk, bool meaningful) =>
    color fc = toSup ? COL_BEAR : COL_BULL
    z.isSupply := toSup
    z.flipped  := true
    z.state    := 0
    z.touchBar := -1
    z.tap50Bar := -1
    z.deepest  := na
    z.signaled := false
    // V7.6 · F-04 · PROVENANCE IS RE-STAMPED, NOT MERELY DESTROYED.
    //   P1-1 was right that a zone changing SIDE cannot keep the chain that built it —
    //   that raid took liquidity on the other side. But v7.5 stopped there: it zeroed
    //   srcPoolId and f_zoneProv is only ever called at CREATION, so BREAKER and IFVG —
    //   the two arrays f_paRank ranks ABOVE a raw FVG, and the ones ICT treats as
    //   primary — became permanently untradeable by the only producer that trades
    //   arrays. That is backwards: a flip whose isBrk chain holds (a liquidity event AT
    //   this zone, then displacement, then a structure shift) is BETTER evidenced than
    //   the gap it replaces. It therefore inherits that event as its own chain, on one
    //   condition — the recorded raid must be on the side the zone now serves, because
    //   a DEMAND breaker is paid for by a SELL-side raid and a SUPPLY breaker by a
    //   buy-side one. Anything short of that clears to nothing, as before.
    //   v7.5 also left srcRaidExt / Px / Rq / Kind / Tag / MssBar / DispBar / DispQ /
    //   Idm behind as stale values, unreachable only because of the srcPoolId gate.
    //   Every field of the chain is written unconditionally now.
    bool  rest = isBrk and z.liqBeforeFail and z.failId != 0 and z.failBuy == toSup
    int   fmb  = toSup ? math.max(nz(iChDnBar, -1), math.max(nz(eChDnBar, -1), nz(eBosDnBar2, -1)))
         : math.max(nz(iChUpBar, -1), math.max(nz(eChUpBar, -1), nz(eBosUpBar2, -1)))
    z.srcPoolId   := rest ? z.failId   : 0
    z.srcRaidBar  := rest ? z.failBar  : int(na)
    z.srcRaidExt  := rest ? z.failExt  : na
    z.srcRaidPx   := rest ? z.failPx   : na
    z.srcRaidRq   := rest ? z.failRq   : 0
    z.srcRaidKind := rest ? z.failKind : 0
    z.srcRaidTag  := rest ? z.failTag  : ""
    z.srcMssBar   := rest and fmb >= 0 ? fmb : int(na)
    z.srcMssCh    := rest and (toSup ? (recentIChDn or recentEChDn) : (recentIChUp or recentEChUp))
    z.srcDispBar  := rest ? bar_index : int(na)
    z.srcDispQ    := rest ? (toSup ? dqDn : dqUp) : 0
    z.srcIdm      := false
    z.liqBeforeFail := false
    z.failId   := 0
    // V7.7 · F-02 · a flip makes this a DIFFERENT array on the other side, so whoever
    //   was holding it no longer is. The holder is killed the same bar by its own POI
    //   invalidation (a flip is a close beyond the edge), so this only releases the
    //   eviction lock rather than changing any verdict.
    z.bound    := 0
    z.tier     := isBrk and not z.isFvg ? 2 : 3
    z.tier0    := z.tier
    z.tierW    := z.tier
    z.mitN     := 0
    z.inZ      := false
    z.legExt   := na
    // §13 / §14 / §15 / §16 · WHAT FAILED decides the class. V7.5: a closed-
    // through FVG is a FAILED FVG first — tier 3, untradable. It becomes a TRUE
    // IFVG (tier 2) only after price returns to it from the other side and HOLDS
    // there with a rejection (promoted in the update loop), and only when the
    // violation itself was meaningful (a displacement or a ≥ 0.5-ATR body).
    z.ifvgPend := z.isFvg and meaningful
    z.pa       := z.isFvg ? PA_MIT : isBrk ? PA_BRK : PA_MIT
    z.kindTxt  := (z.isFvg ? "FAILED FVG " : isBrk ? "BREAKER " : "MITIGATION ") + (toSup ? "▼" : "▲")
    box.set_border_color(z.bx, color.new(fc, 55))
    box.set_bgcolor(z.bx, color.new(fc, 93))
    box.set_text(z.bx, z.kindTxt + (showTier ? " " + f_tierTxt(z.tier) : ""))
    box.set_text_color(z.bx, color.new(fc, 0))
    box.set_text_valign(z.bx, toSup ? text.align_top : text.align_bottom)
    line.set_color(z.mid, color.new(fc, 72))
    true

// ---- cap zones per side: prune the OLDEST, fully (BUG-22) ------------------
// Runs FIRST, on the set the previous bar left behind. Pruning after the update
// loop could delete a box that the loop had just handed to the trade engine as
// a candidate, and the engine would then be styling a deleted drawing.
// V7.7 · F-02 · A ZONE A LIVE SETUP IS STANDING ON IS NOT ELIGIBLE. `z.bound` is
//   written by the machine's stage 3 → 4 and cleared by f_setupReset, so the cap
//   can see it one bar later — which is the only bar that matters, because the
//   eviction is what used to freeze that setup's POI tier forever. If EVERY zone on
//   a side is bound the search returns -1 and the loop breaks, so the cap can be
//   exceeded by at most the two live setups. That is the correct trade: two boxes
//   over budget, or a setup trading an array that no longer exists.
if confirmed
    for side = 0 to 1
        bool sup = side == 1
        int  cnt = 0
        if array.size(zones) > 0
            for i = 0 to array.size(zones) - 1
                Zone z = array.get(zones, i)
                if z.isSupply == sup
                    cnt += 1
        while cnt > maxZones
            int oldIx  = -1
            int oldBar = na
            for i = 0 to array.size(zones) - 1
                Zone z = array.get(zones, i)
                if z.isSupply == sup and z.bound == 0 and (oldIx == -1 or nz(z.bornBar, 0) < oldBar)
                    oldIx  := i
                    oldBar := nz(z.bornBar, 0)
            if oldIx == -1
                break
            Zone o = array.get(zones, oldIx)
            box.delete(o.bx)
            line.delete(o.mid)
            array.remove(zones, oldIx)
            cnt -= 1

// ---- POI candidates produced by the 50 % engine (NOT signals) ---------------
sdCandLong  = false
sdCandShort = false
// zone REFERENCES, not indices: the loop below and the age-prune both remove
// zones, which would re-point a stored index at a different zone
Zone  sdZoneL   = na
Zone  sdZoneS   = na
float sdTopLong = na
float sdBotLong = na
int   sdTierL   = 3
float sdTopShort = na
float sdBotShort = na
int   sdTierS    = 3
int   sdRetQL   = 1         // V7.5 · measured retest depth of the candidate zone (F-10: not a constant)
int   sdRetQS   = 1
// a raid WICK sitting AT a zone this bar (used for the breaker causal chain)
// V7.6 · F-04 · the raid AT a zone is now tracked PER SIDE. v7.5 collapsed both sides
//   into one ambiguous raidNearPx (sell-side if there was one, else buy-side) and kept
//   only a boolean, which is enough to CLASSIFY a breaker but not to re-stamp one: the
//   flip has to know whether the liquidity that paid for the failure was BSL or SSL.
raidLoNow = confirmed and sslInPlay and anyLoBar == bar_index and not na(anyLoExt)
raidHiNow = confirmed and bslInPlay and anyHiBar == bar_index and not na(anyHiExt)

if confirmed and array.size(zones) > 0
    for i = array.size(zones) - 1 to 0
        Zone z = array.get(zones, i)
        float top  = z.top          // V5.4-D1: cached geometry, not a box API read
        float bot  = z.bot
        float midP = (top + bot) / 2
        float hgt  = math.max(top - bot, syminfo.mintick)
        // a liquidity event AT this zone, recorded on the zone itself — this
        // is what makes the BREAKER classification causal (Phase 12)
        float zPad = poiBindAtr * atr
        if raidLoNow and anyLoExt <= top + zPad and anyLoExt >= bot - zPad
            z.liqBeforeFail := true
            z.failBuy  := false
            z.failId   := anyLoId
            z.failBar  := anyLoBar
            z.failExt  := anyLoExt
            z.failPx   := anyLoPx
            z.failRq   := anyLoRq
            z.failKind := anyLoKind
            z.failTag  := anyLoTag
        if raidHiNow and anyHiExt <= top + zPad and anyHiExt >= bot - zPad
            z.liqBeforeFail := true
            z.failBuy  := true
            z.failId   := anyHiId
            z.failBar  := anyHiBar
            z.failExt  := anyHiExt
            z.failPx   := anyHiPx
            z.failRq   := anyHiRq
            z.failKind := anyHiKind
            z.failTag  := anyHiTag
        bool  sup  = z.isSupply
        float fill = 0.0
        if sup
            // V7.5 · the leg extreme freezes at the first touch; discrete mitigations
            //   are counted on every re-entry from outside (§15 lifecycle).
            if z.state == 0
                z.legExt := math.min(nz(z.legExt, low), low)
            bool inS = high >= bot
            if inS and not z.inZ
                z.mitN += 1
            z.inZ := inS
            if inS
                if z.state == 0
                    z.touchBar := bar_index
                if z.state < 1
                    z.state := 1
                z.deepest := math.max(nz(z.deepest, bot), high)
            // §14 · FAILED FVG → TRUE IFVG: price came back to the inverted gap from
            //   below and HELD under it with a rejection. Tier 2 from here, not before.
            if z.ifvgPend and inS and close < bot and (zoneBearConf or strongDn)
                z.ifvgPend := false
                z.pa       := PA_IFVG
                z.tier     := 2
                z.tier0    := 2
                z.tierW    := 2     // V7.6 · F-05 · a DELIBERATE re-classification resets the ratchet
                z.kindTxt  := "IFVG ▼"
                box.set_text(z.bx, z.kindTxt + (showTier ? " T2" : ""))
            fill := (nz(z.deepest, bot) - bot) / hgt
            if fill >= 1 and z.state < 3
                z.state := 3
                if showFvgState
                    box.set_bgcolor(z.bx, color.new(COL_BEAR, 96))
            else if fill >= 0.5 and z.state < 2
                z.state := 2
            // edge-triggered 50 % tap: re-arms only after price leaves the zone
            if high < bot
                z.tap50Bar := -1
            if high >= midP and z.tap50Bar == -1
                z.tap50Bar := bar_index
        else
            if z.state == 0
                z.legExt := math.max(nz(z.legExt, high), high)
            bool inD = low <= top
            if inD and not z.inZ
                z.mitN += 1
            z.inZ := inD
            if inD
                if z.state == 0
                    z.touchBar := bar_index
                if z.state < 1
                    z.state := 1
                z.deepest := math.min(nz(z.deepest, top), low)
            if z.ifvgPend and inD and close > top and (zoneBullConf or strongUp)
                z.ifvgPend := false
                z.pa       := PA_IFVG
                z.tier     := 2
                z.tier0    := 2
                z.tierW    := 2     // V7.6 · F-05 · a DELIBERATE re-classification resets the ratchet
                z.kindTxt  := "IFVG ▲"
                box.set_text(z.bx, z.kindTxt + (showTier ? " T2" : ""))
            fill := (top - nz(z.deepest, top)) / hgt
            if fill >= 1 and z.state < 3
                z.state := 3
                if showFvgState
                    box.set_bgcolor(z.bx, color.new(COL_BULL, 96))
            else if fill >= 0.5 and z.state < 2
                z.state := 2
            if low > top
                z.tap50Bar := -1
            if low <= midP and z.tap50Bar == -1
                z.tap50Bar := bar_index
        // ═══ V7.6 · RECLAIM R1 · VIOLATION AND RE-GRADE, WRITTEN ONCE ═══════════
        //   The two side branches spelled an IDENTICAL violation block and an identical
        //   eligibility test, so f_zoneFlip (517 src) and f_regrade were each INLINED
        //   TWICE — the same duplication v7.5's own R1 removed from the zone CREATION
        //   path and left behind on the UPDATE path. Byte-identical decisions; only the
        //   copy count changed, and it is what pays for this release's new logic.
        //   Phase 12 causal chain, all recorded on THIS zone: it was created, price
        //   RETURNED into it, a liquidity event happened AT it, and the failure came
        //   with displacement AND a structure shift. Anything less is a mitigation block.
        bool viol = sup ? close > top : close < bot
        if viol
            if showFlip and not z.flipped
                bool vDsp = sup ? dispUpBar : dispDnBar
                bool vSft = sup ? (recentIChUp or recentEChUp or recentEBosUp) : (recentIChDn or recentEChDn or recentEBosDn)
                f_zoneFlip(z, not sup, z.touchBar >= 0 and z.touchBar < bar_index and z.liqBeforeFail and vDsp and vSft, vDsp or body >= BRK_BODY_ATR * atr)
            else
                // V7.7 · F-02 · TELL THE SETUP BEFORE THE OBJECT LEAVES THE ARRAY.
                //   `viol` is `close beyond the edge`, while the machine's own POI
                //   invalidation carries NEAR_LVL_ATR of slack — so a close inside that
                //   slack destroyed the zone one or more bars before the setup noticed,
                //   and (once out of `zones`) f_regrade could never tell it either.
                //   Both f_zoneDead legs are set, so the kill lands on this same bar
                //   whatever minTier is configured to.
                z.tier     := 3
                z.tierW    := 3
                z.signaled := true
                box.delete(z.bx)
                line.delete(z.mid)
                array.remove(zones, i)
            continue
        // 50 % engine → CANDIDATE only. V7.5 · §6 · ONLY a zone with a complete OWNED
        //   chain (raid → MSS → displacement → this array) may be traded by the S/D
        //   producer. Best tier wins; the retest depth is measured, never assumed.
        f_regrade(z)
